# Graftyard on Cloudflare, three-site quorum chains in one account that survive a region, a continent, and a bad deploy

## Summary

No Cloudflare storage product promises a cross-region RPO of zero (`research/cloudflare-uptime.md` finding 40), so Graftyard builds one itself. Every chain (a repository, a fork network, an owner's `Authz` and `Identity`, a token shard, an org's Actions state) is a Raft group of three SQLite-backed Durable Objects on three **sites**. A site is one Worker script whose Durable Objects, containers, and pack bucket are pinned to one region. The default install or managed cell is one Cloudflare account with three site scripts on three continents (enam, weur, apac), and members reach each other through Durable Object bindings, not public HTTPS. A push is acknowledged only after its entry is on two sites in two regions and its pack bytes are in R2 buckets on two of those sites. Leadership leaves a site by Raft leadership transfer before a deploy touches it, and when the site turns sick.

Products are Workers (a stable `graft-front`, the edge, three site scripts, and a stateless `graft-ops`), Durable Objects, R2, Containers (upstream git and the SSH front), Spectrum (native git over SSH on port 22 to the edge's `connect()` handler, off by default per install, on Pro and above; `ssh.graftyard.dev` is one since 2026-11-20), Queues, Workflows, Cron Triggers, Secrets Store, DNS, WAF, Sandbox SDK, Email, Analytics Engine, Workers Logs, and Basin for analytics only. workerd runs only the off-Cloudflare **lifeboat**, the one component outside Cloudflare in production, and evaluation installs. KV, D1, Artifacts, and K2 are not used.

A GitHub user notices nothing in normal operation, apart from push latency of about one intercontinental round trip. If a site is lost outright, a request to a group led there waits at most about 11.5 s plus its own commit [inference: promise remaining, a 1 s call deadline, and two rounds]. Campaigns are request-driven, so the bound runs from each group's first request after the loss, not from the loss: under a measured loss of one site's member class, the first push took 16.8 s and later ones 5.4 to 6.3 s, and an idle group moved only when its next request came, 247 to 284 s later [measured, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp8.md`]. A gray regional incident like the 6-hour WEUR one on 2026-10-22, with elevated errors and no clean loss, moves leaders off the sick site within about 20 s through `suspect` reports and, on busy groups, self step-down. That case stays `mitigated` until its Milestone 0 rows pass. A site deploy drains the site first, so it targets zero failed writes. An R2-wide outage still accepts pushes under 1 MiB. During a Cloudflare-wide outage, an install with a lifeboat still serves clones and reads to developers who enrolled.

A Graftyard maintainer notices that the folds and the two-release format rule of `design/SHARED.md` survive from the earlier design in `history/DESIGN.md`, whose cells do not. Its database and failover machinery is replaced by one consensus module they must own and test hard. A group has one entry in flight, and group commit packs every command of a round into it. Releases roll one site script per day through `graft-ops`, an in-account executor of signed intents. Followers on the previous release refuse entries they cannot decode, and a refusal from a follower on an older release deposes the leader that wrote it. Self-hosted means the customer runs the same artifact in its own Cloudflare account with no vendor token.

AI coding agents use this design as GHES clients, through GitHub Apps, REST, webhooks, and checks, so the base adds no GHES path for agents. A 500-agent fleet on one monorepo forced base changes. They are hot-network git cache fan-out from a per-site hot pool, lazy test merges under a budget, admission classes by origin and behavior, a smaller inline cap on hot groups that yields during R2 outages, a log byte cap per member, and hot clone-cache entries in both warm buckets. With them come agent refusals and a head-bound agent gate in `decide`, provenance and protected-path checks in `validate`, host-owned session refs, and revocation of stateless installation tokens (journeys 4 and 9). Leases on paths and refs are chain state on the repository's group, checked against the leader's entry time and extended only by a recorded stall (`ExtendLeases`), and the layer serves them with an activity feed and a task API under `/api/graftyard/v1`. The agent layer itself, its remote MCP server, controller, and cost, is in `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`.

## Usage (caller's view)

**Developer, managed.** Nothing changes from GHES targeting (`design/SHARED.md`, "Wire compatibility", reused whole).

```
gh auth login --hostname graftyard.dev
git clone https://graftyard.dev/acme/monorepo.git            # HTTPS, protocol v2, partial clone allowed
git clone git@graftyard.dev:acme/monorepo.git                 # SSH where the install turns it on: Spectrum to the edge
git push --atomic origin main release hotfix                  # one entry, two sites, then "ok"
gh pr merge 42 --squash && gh pr view 42 && gh pr list        # all three read the base chain's leader
```

**Developer, during a Cloudflare-wide outage, at a BYOC or self-hosted install that runs a lifeboat.** The lifeboat is a DNS-only hostname that the install publishes in advance. Lifeboat reads are opt-in per client. Each developer enrolls once while Cloudflare is up, and `actions/runner` and apps do not follow the lifeboat.

```
graftyard lifeboat enroll --host lifeboat.acme.example     # once: mints a lifeboat:read token that the main host refuses,
                                                           # stored in gh's hosts file and a git credential helper
git -c url."https://lifeboat.acme.example/".insteadOf="https://git.acme.example/" fetch   # read-only, typically ~30 s behind
GH_HOST=lifeboat.acme.example gh pr list                                                   # reads work, writes get 503
curl -sI https://lifeboat.acme.example/api/v3/meta | grep -i x-graftyard-lifeboat-lag     # the published lag
```

**Operator, BYOC or self-hosted (both in a Cloudflare account the customer owns).**

```
graftyard init --account $A --zone git.acme.example --customer-key customer.pub  # mints the install id; writes install.toml, creates nothing
graftyard sites add --script graft-site-a --region enam --jurisdiction us   # DO classes, container class, bucket, trust anchor; latency-checked
graftyard sites add --script graft-site-b --region weur
graftyard sites add --script graft-site-c --region apac
graftyard sites add-spare --regions eeur,wnam,oc                     # one spare per continent: no members, no warm containers
graftyard genesis --out genesis.unsigned                             # creates graft-front, the edge, graft-ops, Workflows; drain and executor keys
graftyard sign genesis.unsigned --out genesis.signed                 # offline, on the machine that holds the customer key
graftyard install --genesis genesis.signed                           # the empty Release group accepts only a genesis that matches the anchor
graftyard placement set default tri --sites A,B,C                    # refuses two sites in one region
graftyard placement set eval-org solo --site A                       # Solo: evaluation only, no region-loss guarantee, no SLO
graftyard vault init --account $V                                    # required in every mode; $A's tokens cannot reach $V
graftyard runners init --account $R                                  # hosted Actions runners in their own account
graftyard admin bootstrap                                            # one-time sign-in link, the operator's code row
graftyard release roll 2026.44                                       # signs and submits the release's next intent; rerun until done
graftyard lifeboat init --host lifeboat.acme.example                 # registers the host's own key in a customer-signed entry
# Account-separated option: each site in its own account, members over signed HTTPS through site Workers.
graftyard sites add --account $B --script graft-site-b --region weur --link https
```

**Call sites inside the code (Rust compiled to WebAssembly through workers-rs [inference]).**

```rust
// edge Worker, receive-pack: stage, validate, place, commit, report.
let pack = pack_store.place(net, body.capped(plan_cap).tee_to(site_pair)).await?;  // two buckets, If-None-Match: *
let push = Push { updates, atomicity, pack, actor, options, request, provenance, protected, changed };
let acked = match group(ChainId::Repo(repo)).commit(push).await? {       // held up to COMMIT_HOLD; edge waits 2 s
    Committed::Done(outcome) => outcome?,                                // the entry committed inside the hold
    Committed::Admitted(a) => group(ChainId::Repo(repo)).outcome(a.ticket).await?,  // AGENT_WAIT plus one round
};
report_status(&acked)   // takes &Acked, so "ok" cannot precede the quorum; the member renders it, the edge frames the bytes

// edge Worker, `gh issue list --label bug`: read on the leader, after the lease is proven.
let view = group(ChainId::Repo(repo)).fresh(arrived).await?;            // Fresh only while the leader holds its lease
view.query(IssueFilter { labels: vec!["bug".into()], sort: Sort::Updated, ..Default::default() })

// any receipt or member call that misses CALL_DEADLINE: report the leader to a follower on another site. The hedge is
// only this report, and the command is never forwarded to a second member.
suspect(&follower_on_other_site, colo, about, term).await;   // about: the member whose receipt missed

// SSH: `graft-sshd` sends the same smart-HTTP requests through `SshOutbound` and the `EDGE` binding, never the public zone.
let acked = match group(ChainId::Repo(repo)).commit(push).await? {
    Committed::Done(outcome) => outcome?,
    Committed::Admitted(a) => group(ChainId::Repo(repo)).outcome(a.ticket).await?,
};
```

## Stack

| Product | Status | Used for |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Workers | GA | `graft-front` (forwards to the edge over a `fetch()`-based service binding with a version override from the signed `EdgePointer`); the edge (HTTPS git, REST, GraphQL, web assets; calls `graft-ops` the same way with the signed `OpsPointer`); three site scripts that define each site's Durable Object and container classes; `graft-ops` (signed intents, API-calling crons, installation-token minting); the edge's `connect()` handler for SSH from Spectrum; container outbound handlers (`SshOutbound`); `graft-runners` in the runner account (hosted-runner demand, JIT configs, Sandbox start and destroy) |
| Cache API (Workers) | GA [inference] | The edge's data-center share of the read gate's `Allow` decisions and of checked chain-pack header sets, never required: a miss reads as before ("Follower read"; Shape, git caches; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`colo-gate`", built, not yet measured hosted) |
| Durable Objects (SQLite-backed) | GA | Group members and learners: Raft log, lease, folds (SQLite and FTS5), consumer cursors; git cache shards that own containers; one `SshFront` per site that owns its SSH front container; derived objects with no quorum (Shape, Identity): one `Inbox` per account, `SearchShard` FTS5 shards per residency domain, one `RateCounter` per rate-limit key; per-site derived `RunnerBroker`, `LogRelay`, and `HookSequencer` objects (Shape, Identity) |
| R2 | GA | Pack and index copies (one bucket per site), clone cache, snapshots and archives, Actions blobs, lifeboat change feed, the release bucket the vendor writes signed bundles into; the vault account's locked copy |
| Containers | GA | Upstream git in git cache shards (index-pack, upload-pack, merge-tree, repack) and the SSH front running `graft-sshd` (image `sshfront`, `basic`, one per site's `SshFront` object), both under the `durable_object` scheduling policy (beta), the only policy a new account may create; hosted Actions runners, one `RunnerBox` container per job on the same policy, started by `graft-runners` in a separate runner account with the image of its Worker version (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`r23-runnerbox`") |
| Sandbox SDK (Sandboxes) | GA | Hosted Actions runners started before `RunnerBox`, one Sandbox per job, until release M3 deletes the Sandbox classes |
| Spectrum | GA | Public port 22 for native git over SSH, off by default per install: a TCP application with a Worker origin (`traffic_type: "worker"`) whose origin is the edge's `connect()` handler, which a Pro zone accepted and served for 600 s [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp7.md`, B0-24c]; Business by the same plan table [unverified], Enterprise [inference]. One Worker app per transport per pay-as-you-go zone [doc]; the Worker origin and `connect()` are private beta per Cloudflare's blog [doc]. Created by `graftyard ssh spectrum` (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "SSH goes through a Spectrum Worker application to the SSH front (2026-11-19)") |
| DNS | GA | Authoritative DNS, including the DNS-only lifeboat hostname and the CNAME that delegates its `_acme-challenge` name to a validation zone in a separate account |
| WAF and rate limiting rules | GA | Abuse limits on clone, API, and login at the edge |
| Queues | GA | Webhook, notification, search-index, and lifeboat-feed fan-out from chain cursors |
| Workflows | GA | `ReplaceMember` and `RestorePlacement`, imports, network compaction, and the `LeaveEvaluation` group moves |
| Cron Triggers | GA | In `graft-ops`, every job that calls the Cloudflare API (bucket location, Analytics Engine reads for learners); in site scripts, pack sweeper (including orphan multipart aborts), GC, fold audit; in `graft-runners`, the 5-minute hosted-runner sweep |
| Secrets Store | beta | `graft-ops` API tokens, the SSH host keys (`SshHostKeys`, two slots), and the key that wraps the installation-token signing key, which puts it on the minting path; `SecretsWrapKey` for Actions secrets and hook secrets; `HostedRunnersAppKey` in the runner account; rotation without a new script version |
| Email Routing | GA | Reply-by-email for issues and pull requests |
| Email Sending (Email Service) | beta | Notification and password email, off every core path |
| Workers Analytics Engine | GA | Per-repository and per-group traffic counters, learner placement input, SLO data points |
| Workers Logs | GA | Request and error logs. Head sampling never drops an error, refusal, shed, 5xx, `ng`, or step-down: those lines go to Analytics Engine and Basin in full, and only ok invocation logs sample (`logs-sample`, unbuilt) |
| Basin Pipelines | GA | Stream audit entries and SLO events into Iceberg tables |
| Basin Catalog | GA | Iceberg tables for audit retention and SLO history |
| Basin SQL | GA | Operator queries over audit and SLO tables; never serves a user request |
| Access | GA | Staff and admin console sign-in only |
| Data Localization Suite | GA | Managed EU placements: Regional Services so EU traffic is decrypted in the EU |
| workerd | GA [inference; runtime GA; `localDisk` experimental; `localDocker` development only] | The lifeboat, and evaluation installs on customer hardware |

Status cells copy `research/cloudflare.md`, first table. The workerd qualifiers come from `workerd.capnp` lines 807 to 808 and 836 to 838 at commit `ec3982b2` (`history/review/cloudflare-selfhost-walk.md` F1).

## Shape

**Sites, not services, are the failure unit.** A site is one Worker script in the install's account (`graft-site-a`, `graft-site-b`, `graft-site-c`). The script defines the site's member and git cache Durable Object classes. It defines the site's container classes. The `durable_object` scheduling policy takes no `regions` constraint, so a container starts near the object that starts it, and every shard object is created with its site's hint. In Milestone 0, 90 of 90 containers started this way ran in their hinted region [measured, `tests/m0/results/container-placement.json`], but the docs say an object and its container are "not guaranteed to run in the same location", so each start is checked (Shape, "Git cache shards (derived)"). It owns one pack bucket. A site script has no public route. The default cell is A = enam, B = weur, C = apac. Residency cells add D = wnam, E = eeur, F = oc. Each install also pre-creates one spare site per continent (for example eeur, wnam, oc) with no warm containers and no members except one canary group, which replays a corpus of current formats on the spare's own site day of every release. Every script binds the spares from install (below), so a replacement needs no control-plane call and stays on the lost site's continent. In BYOC and self-hosted, `graft-front`, the edge, `graft-ops`, the zone, and Spectrum sit in the same account, and none holds chain state.

**Managed cells.** Bindings cannot cross accounts, so one edge on `graftyard.dev` cannot bind members in many cell accounts [inference from service bindings, whose target "must be on your Cloudflare account", https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/service-bindings/]. A front account holds the zone, `graft-front`, the edge, and the global `Names` and `Tokens` groups on its own three sites. The front edge resolves the owner's cell from `Names`, or from the cell bits of a `DbId` for `/repositories/{id}`, `/user/{id}`, node ids, and installation ids, and calls that cell's edge Worker over Ed25519-signed HTTPS. It reads each cell's `Release` pointers the same way. Inside a cell, members replicate over bindings. A partial proxy failure on a cell hostname cuts that cell's client traffic, and replication inside the cell continues [inference].

Each of these faults maps to one site: a region loss, or a gray regional incident once drain or step-down has moved leadership away; a bad site setting, such as a wrong jurisdiction or region constraint in one script's config; and a bad Graftyard deploy, because each site script rolls on its own day ("Install operations"). A Durable Object runs the version of the script that defines its class, and "will only be reset when it is assigned a different version" (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/versions-and-deployments/gradual-deployments/with-durable-objects/). Any new version of a site script resets its members the same way [unverified: on `graft-m2b` a site day left three busy voters on the old version until each was sent `/reset`, `tests/m3/results/m3-wp5.md`, "Where the depth comes from"; `decisions/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`, R19], including a secret, variable, binding, or container config change (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/secrets/), so every such change is a site-day operation.

Every group has one voting member per site, so each of these faults removes at most one voter of every group. That keeps a majority [inference]. A location hint is a `get()` parameter that only the first call respects (https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/reference/data-location/), so the hint table is compiled into callers (the edge, `graft-front`, and site scripts). A wrong hint is an edge failure that can misplace new members of every site, and the latency check below refuses them.

Account-scoped faults reach all three sites in the default. These are a bad account setting, a revoked or leaked account token, an operator or abuse action on the account, a Workers runtime release to the account's cohort, and the account's shared Container quota. The 2025-02-06 abuse action was aimed at one bucket and disabled all of R2 by mistake (`research/cloudflare-uptime.md` finding 7). The vault account holds a copy that none of the install's tokens can reach. The account-separated option contains the intended scope of such an action, and no split contains the mistaken one.

**Members reach each other through Durable Object bindings.** At install, each site script binds the member classes and buckets of every other active, spare, and residency site with `script_name`, which names "the Worker where the Durable Object is defined, if it is external to this Worker" (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/wrangler/configuration/). The edge Worker and the script that runs the Workflows bind all of them the same way, and the compiled hint table covers the spares, so `ReplaceMember` onto a spare creates no script version. A binding call runs inside Cloudflare's runtime and never uses the zone's public hostnames, so a proxy failure that breaks HTTP for some zones, like December 5, 2025, should not cut replication. Milestone 0 saw a binding call arrive at the object with none of the front-line proxy's headers and no `cf` object, and Durable Objects are absent from the impact lists of the November 18 and December 5, 2025 postmortems [measured, doc; the transport between colos stays undocumented] (`tests/m0/results/wp11.md`, (a)). Object routing is still shared by all three sites. The runtime bounds the nesting of calls between objects at 16 and charges each outgoing call to the object's newest incoming request, so the member-to-member call graph must not form loops: two members that keep calling each other while both are busy nest deeper with each exchange (Failure modes, chain groups, noisy-tenant). Voters do not today, because a follower calls its leader at a low rate; a learner is the case, and the leader never calls a learner that is calling it (Scale, learners). The host's inputs to a member's Raft core are `step`, `tick`, `call_failed`, `call_ended`, `confirm_begin` and `confirm_end`, `self_ack`, `catchup`, and the command and read calls. A leader counts the AppendEntries bytes alive on each call it sent (its entries' canonical encoding), from the send until its answer, an error, or `call_ended(to, round)`, which the host sends once it stops awaiting the call (answered, failed, or LATE_LIMIT past its deadline) or, for a learner, once the message leaves in that learner's own response. It sends a call only while the peer stays within `PEER_OUT_MAX` (2 MB [guess]), the learners together within `LEARNERS_OUT_MAX` (4 MB [guess]), and all peers within `OUT_MAX` (8 MB [guess]); a voter with no call alive always gets one, so the budget never stops a commit, and a call the host never reports stops counting after `OUT_KEEP` (12 s [guess]). Before, the core resent a missed call at once and on every tick while the host still awaited the first copy, and nothing bounded the sum: m3f's new leader sent each voter 10.7 to 11.2 MB in its first minute against an 8 MB log, and B0-33's new leaders held 58.3 MB of call bodies and 42.9 MB of messages for learners before their isolates reset [measured, LM-1 round 17 and m3fr3, `tests/m0/results/memory-budgets.md`].

**Account-separated sites (option).** A customer who wants account isolation puts each site script in its own account and the zone, `graft-front`, and the edge in a fourth. Members then reach each other over HTTPS through each site's Worker, with Ed25519-signed requests. That contains account faults to one voter, except in the fourth account, which holds the zone and edge and carries all client traffic. It costs replication over the public proxy. A December 2025 class failure on the ring hostnames stalls commits for its duration, and each member call adds a site Worker request and its latency: Milestone 0 measured a median of 7 ms (4 to 16 ms over 17 of 18 site pairs) at p50 and up to 51 ms at p99 over a cross-script binding, with signing under 1 ms [measured] (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp5.md`).

Location comes from documented controls plus direct measurement. DO jurisdictions pin `eu` and `us` (https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/reference/data-location/), and they stay wherever residency allows. Hints are "best effort" (same page). So when a member joins, it takes the median of 20 binding round trips to each other voter, and `Placement::new` refuses a pair under 30 ms [guess threshold: intra-region round trips are a few ms, enam to weur about 80 ms]. `Placement::new` also refuses a group whose home has no other voter within `NEAR_RTT` for the home's region, because a home leader's commit round is one round trip to its nearer follower: at 300 commands/s every B0-02 run whose near follower measured 100 ms or less passed, and only 3 of 10 over 110 ms did [measured, `tests/m0/results/carried-2.md`]; on groups placed within the bound, 15 of 16 passed, and the miss was one leader stall [measured, `tests/m0/results/carried-3.md`]. The bound is 100 ms for an enam or weur home, B0-12's median enam to weur pair, and 198 ms for an apac home, its median enam to apac pair [measured, `tests/m0/results/b0-12.json`]. Location hints place a member in a region, not a city, so a new group's leader re-places its nearer follower with new generations until one passes, up to `PLACE_TRIES`, and keeps the nearest try if none does. It does this after the create returns, while the group already serves (Invariants, "Placement is checked by latency"). A move's swap and `graftyard placement set`'s site probes check without the bound, and the next daily check catches a move that broke it. A member repeats the check at most once a day, and only within a minute of a call an edge made for a client (`CLIENT_CALL`), so a cron, fold audit, sweep, or peer call never starts one. The daily check reads against `NEAR_RECHECK`, half again over `NEAR_RTT` (150 ms, or 297 ms for an apac home), because a new generation draws from the same pair spread: on 2026-11-16, 117 daily re-placements on six installs all read 101 to 148 ms against 100, and 115 of them ended 0 to 8 minutes after a 15-minute cron run [measured, Workers Logs, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "The daily near check re-placed healthy groups"; `NEAR_RECHECK`'s value is a guess past B0-12's p99 enam to weur pair of 135 ms]. Each member keeps its own measurements (`Rtt`), outside `GroupConfig`, so a check commits no entry. R2's get-bucket API returns the bucket's `location` (https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/r2/subresources/buckets/methods/get/), so `graft-ops` refuses a misplaced bucket at install. A check that fails or errors is a no-op plus an alarm. Only a positive mismatch read twice 24 h apart starts a repair.

```rust
// ---- Sites and placement ----
pub enum Region { Enam, Wnam, Weur, Eeur, Apac, Oc }              // DO and R2 hint names that spawn locally
pub enum Jurisdiction { Eu, Us }
pub struct SiteId(u8);
/// One Worker script pinned to one region. Its DO classes, container classes, bucket, and release are its own.
/// An `Oc` site lists ["OC", "APAC"] as its container regions, and for container placement it is not a separate region.
pub struct Site { pub id: SiteId, pub script: ScriptName, pub account: AccountTag, pub region: Region,
                  pub jurisdiction: Option<Jurisdiction>, pub bucket: BucketName, pub release: Release, pub link: SiteLink,
                  pub spare: bool /* pre-created per continent: only a canary group, no warm containers; rolls on
                                     its own site day, and keeps its images until that day */ }
pub enum SiteLink {
    Binding { member_class: ClassName },                          // default: same account, DO binding with script_name
    Https { ring_host: Hostname, key: Ed25519Public },            // account-separated option and managed front-to-cell
}
pub enum MemberKind {
    Full,
    Witness,   // stores (term, index, digest), never content: used for residency
    Learner,   // stores content, never votes, serves read-index reads: hot groups only; slot k is `SiteId(192 + k)`
}
/// `do_name` is "{chain}#{generation}#{kind}{slot}": `kind` is `f`, `w`, or `l`, and `slot` the voter or learner
/// position, so members on one site never share an object. Bootstrap members take the record's generation; later ones
/// (a retry, a replacement, a move) take one above `Placement.generation`, so none wakes a removed member's object.
pub struct Member { pub site: SiteId, pub kind: MemberKind, pub slot: u8, pub generation: u32, pub do_name: Box<str> }
/// One row per other voter in the member's own SQLite, outside `GroupConfig` and the log, so a check writes no entry;
/// taken at join, then only inside a request served on an unmeasured day, so it never wakes an idle group.
pub struct Rtt { pub peer: SiteId, pub median_ms: u16 /* of 20 binding round trips */, pub at: Timestamp }
/// Built only by `Placement::new`, which reads joiners' `Rtt` rows and refuses two voters in one region or site, a
/// voter pair under MIN_PAIR_RTT, fewer than two Full voters, two voters in one account when accounts are separated,
/// and, at a create's tries or the daily check, a home with no other voter within its region's NEAR_RTT
/// (`PlacementError::NoNearFollower`, last; after PLACE_TRIES the nearest try is kept; a move's swap and `placement set` skip it:
/// `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "A group's home must have a voter within `NEAR_RTT`"). `Placement::solo` builds `Solo` and
/// needs the signed `evaluation` flag; `from_record` builds a first placement from its `PlacementRecord` alone.
pub struct Placement { voters: ArrayVec<Member, 3>, learners: ArrayVec<Member, 16>,
                       home: SiteId /* preferred leader site; transfer restores it; wins over the record's */,
                       generation: u32 /* highest member generation this group has named */, _private: () }
/// Where one owner's chains, or a system chain, live. An owner's record, the `NamesEvent::Placement` row on
/// `account_shard(owner)`, covers its `Authz`, `Identity`, `Actions`, and repository chains, and each network chain
/// whose root repository it holds. `graftyard placement set <owner>` writes it; signup copies the default
/// (`InitPlacement`). A system chain's is in the signed `Genesis.placements`, and only a customer-signed
/// `LeaveEvaluation` changes it. `sites` are logical: `from_record` swaps a site `Release` records lost for its
/// `ReplaceMember` spare. A change bumps `generation` and keeps the old record in `moving_from`. A Workflow then moves
/// each group and child group from the old sites' `MemberRegistry` rows, one voter site per change (a one-site `Joint`,
/// then `Normal`; leadership leaves a site before it is dropped; `reconfigure` refuses a disjoint `Joint`, BF-30), then
/// clears `moving_from`; `home` alone is one change and a transfer. A repository group's step that moves its leader (a
/// transfer, or a `home` the core then hands off to) first waits up to `WARM_BEFORE_TRANSFER` for that site's git cache
/// shard, then goes cold; a failover never waits. A record's `home` only seeds bootstrap (BF-11).
pub struct PlacementRecord { pub name: PlacementName, pub sites: SmallVec<[SiteId; 3]>, pub home: SiteId,
                             pub generation: u32, pub moving_from: Option<Box<PlacementRecord>>, pub at: Timestamp }
pub enum SystemChain { Release, Names, Tokens, Ids }
/// How a caller finds a group: by the `GroupConfig` cached from its last `NotLeader` or `ReplyMeta`. Without one, it
/// calls the record's bootstrap voters, in record order. If none answers with a config, it reads the chain's
/// `MemberRegistry` row on each record site, then repeats both for `moving_from`. Any member holding a config, even a
/// removed one, answers with its latest committed one. The edge finds the record by route: the owner's `Names` rows for
/// `o/r` and owner routes, `NamesEvent::RepoOwner` for `/repositories/{id}` and a network's root, and the cached
/// `Release` view for system chains. Replies carry it. An isolate runs one lookup per chain under a plain-data mark;
/// others poll the cache. A leader that answered is cached, config or not; a first miss is retried, a second walks;
/// a fetch that throws `overloaded` is waited for, doubling, within LOOKUP_FOR, and never walks (`lookup-miss-kind`).
pub struct ReplyMeta { pub term: Term, pub leader: Option<SiteId>, pub config_index: Index }
/// Bootstrap. A group's first entry is `Config(Normal(from_record(record)))` at term 1, index 1, with a zero `prev`, no
/// requests, and the record's `at`, so every voter builds and may propose the same bytes. A member with no committed
/// config serves no read, grants no vote, and stores only that entry (term 1's only one) after reading the same record
/// generation from its source (`Names` leader or genesis) and claiming its chain in the oldest record's first site's
/// `MemberRegistry`; the first generation to claim wins; others store nothing. Unclaimed, a move's two generations
/// built two groups in 20 of 20 rounds [measured, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp7.md`, BF-10]. Two edges creating a group at
/// once propose the same entry; the member called to create it campaigns for term 2 once a quorum stores it, commits
/// its `Noop`, decides the call's command, and transfers leadership to `home`. `Release`'s record is its genesis.
/// A retired member (`/purge_retire`, the test route `/retire`, joined or not) deletes its storage, keeps one `retired`
/// row, and refuses every `/join`: without it, `repo:1048577`'s and `repo:1048597`'s members were joined again by a
/// read's create on first use [measured, `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, `retire-sticks`]. A create a majority refuses
/// as retired names `RETIRED`, and the edge reads that repository 404, as a deleted one [inference, `retired-gone`].
pub fn bootstrap(chain: ChainId, record: &PlacementRecord) -> LogEntry;
pub const MIN_PAIR_RTT: Duration = Duration::from_millis(30);    // [guess]
/// A home's most RTT to its nearest voter; other regions unbounded [measured: B0-02's tail follows it, B0-12 medians]
pub const NEAR_RTT: &[(Region, u16)] = &[(Region::Enam, 100), (Region::Weur, 100), (Region::Apac, 198)]; // NEAR_RECHECK: x1.5, daily
pub const PLACE_TRIES: usize = 8;    // generations tried for the near follower; then the nearest is kept [inference]
pub enum PlacementName {
    Tri,     // A enam, B weur, C apac, all Full: survives a region, a continent, a site deploy
    Na3,     // enam, wnam, weur, all Full: lower latency for North America, does not survive an NA-wide event
    Us2w,    // enam (us), wnam (us) Full, weur Witness: content stays in the US
    Eu2w,    // weur (eu), eeur (eu) Full, enam Witness: content stays in the EU
    Apac2w,  // apac, oc Full, wnam Witness; their containers may share APAC capacity
    Solo,    // one Full member on one site. Relies on the platform's 3-of-5 follower confirmation, which stays in the
             // object's region. RPO 0 for a machine or data center, not covered for a region; RTO is Cloudflare's
             // restart, or the region's return; no availability SLO. Evaluation only.
}
pub const LEARNER_AT: u32 = 500; pub const MAX_LEARNERS: usize = 16;  // read rate (req/s) that adds learners; cap
pub struct GroupLoad { pub reads: SmallVec<[(Region, u32); 6]> }   // peak req/s per region from Analytics Engine
/// Daily `graft-ops` cron (until it exists, the leader's `/learners` route): none under LEARNER_AT, else ceil(rate /
/// 1,000) per region, busiest first, up to 16 [inference]. Learners write no `MemberRegistry` row.
pub fn place_learners(load: &GroupLoad) -> ArrayVec<Region, 16>;

// ---- Chains: the reference design's chain model, with groups in place of a table ----
pub enum ChainId { Repo(RepoId), Network(NetworkId), Authz(AccountId), Identity(AccountId),
                   Tokens(TokenShard), Names(NameShard), Actions(AccountId, u8), Ids, Release,
                   RepoMeta(RepoId) /* a repository's metadata child group; CHILD_GROUP_AT */ }
/// `cell` (8 bits) | `block` (29) | `offset` (16), so every id stays below 2^53. The cell's `Ids` group (on `Tri`)
/// leases blocks to group leaders, and a group commits `EntryBody::IdBlock` before it assigns from one, so a new leader
/// resumes at the next free id. Of each leased block Metadata assigns the lower half and Core the upper (M2-WP2)
/// [inference]. Gaps happen, reuse never. Issue numbers stay per-repository fold counters. The managed front routes
/// `/repositories/{id}`, `/user/{id}`, node ids, and installation ids to a cell by the cell bits.
pub struct DbId(i64);   pub struct Term(u64);   pub struct Index(u64);   pub struct Digest([u8; 32]);
/// Row in each member's `log` table, keyed by index. `prev` chains digests, so a gap or a changed entry is refused.
/// `requests` maps each request id to the range of its events in `body`, so a duplicate is answered from them.
pub struct LogEntry { pub term: Term, pub index: Index, pub prev: Digest, pub format: Format, pub tiers: TierSet,
                      pub at: Timestamp, pub requests: SmallVec<[(RequestId, Range<u32>); 4]>,
                      pub extend: Option<ExtendLeases>, pub body: EntryBody }
/// SHARED's `Event` gains `Names(NamesEvent)`, `Tokens(TokensEvent)`, and `Actions(ActionsEvent)` (Identity, below).
pub enum EntryBody { Events(Vec<Event>) /* <= ENTRY_SOFT 100 KB past the first command (ENTRY_SOFT_DEEP 150 KB while the queue is deep), <= ENTRY_MAX 1.5 MB with inline packs; DO rows cap at 2 MB; memory: LOG_BYTES */,
                     Config(GroupConfig), Release(ReleaseEntry), Noop /* first entry of every term */,
                     IdBlock { base: DbId, len: u32 } /* <= 2^16 */, Archived(Archived), Promoted(Promoted) /* Packs */,
                     Split(SplitEntry) /* CHILD_GROUP_AT */, Imported(ImportBatch) /* SHARED `rest:migrations` */ }
/// The recorded stall. A member marks `stalled_since` at the earliest of a follower's first `suspect` report, a
/// candidate's campaign start, the leader's first entry no quorum acknowledged within CALL_DEADLINE, or a follower's
/// store of that entry's re-send sent CALL_DEADLINE or more after its `at`, which carries it to the next leader
/// [measured, AM1 fix]. It rides the next state write [measured, AM1-WP4]; a crash first, or an old leader that never
/// reaches the winner, loses it. The next entry carries `ExtendLeases { since, by: at - since }`, and every path lease
/// (`Lease`) and Actions job lease with `expires > since` moves by `by`; idle time, a prompt append, or queueing marks
/// nothing. No `extend` is added while an unapplied entry carries one (else a 30-minute stall moved 501 leases twice
/// [measured, M0-WP12]); `since` is at least the last entry's `at`, so no lease it judged expired revives; and none
/// with `by` under EXTEND_MIN, whose entry's commit clears the marker, so an unrenewed lease lapses earlier by the sum
/// of sub-floor stalls since its renewal (20 to 78 s per 600 s TTL on B0-33 [inference]), then at its wall-clock
/// `expires` (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, `extend-floor`). The leader applies `extend` before the entry's commands
/// decide, so `decide` compares moved `expires` with the entry's `at`; reads use the leader's leased clock.
pub struct ExtendLeases { pub since: Timestamp, pub by: Duration }
pub const EXTEND_MIN: Duration = Duration::from_secs(2);        // four DRIFTs, above healthy round p99s [guess]
/// Membership is in the log. Under `Joint`, a lease and a read-index round need promises from a majority of both
/// configs. Pre-vote runs before every campaign, and votes are refused to non-members of the latest committed config.
pub enum GroupConfig { Normal(Placement), Degraded { placement: Placement, lost: SiteId },
                       Joint { old: Placement, new: Placement } }
/// One row per member, written before any reply leaves (DO output gate), so a restart never forgets a vote or a promise.
/// `promised_to` and `saved_at` are in it because the promise and `boots` rules read them (`tests/m1/results/m1-wp2.md`).
pub struct MemberState { pub term: Term, pub voted_for: Option<SiteId>, pub commit: Index, pub applied: Index,
                         pub promise_until: Timestamp,           // no vote for a rival until then
                         pub promised_to: Option<SiteId>,        // the leader `promise_until` was given to
                         pub saved_at: Timestamp,                // when this row was last written
                         pub led_term: Option<Term>,             // a restarted leader resumes this term after one round
                         pub no_lead_until: Option<ReleaseId>,   // an older follower refused our uncommitted entry; clears
                                                                 // when our release changes or `Release` records a
                                                                 // rollback of the refuser's site
                         pub cannot_follow: SmallVec<[SiteId; 2]>, // same-or-newer refusers, until their release changes
                         pub transfer: Option<TransferId>,       // the leader's transfer in progress, or the voter's last
                                                                 // accepted one
                         pub stalled_since: Option<Timestamp>,   // cleared when an entry carrying `extend` commits
                         pub boots: [Timestamp; 2],              // the last two boots that interrupted work: a boot
                                                                 // more than 5 s after `saved_at` is an
                                                                 // idle eviction (measured at about 10 s idle,
                                                                 // Milestone 0) and is not counted
                         pub stepped_down_at: Option<Timestamp> } // our last leadership ended by STEP_DOWN then
pub const LEASE: Duration = Duration::from_secs(9);              // leader serves reads alone while it holds this
pub const ELECTION_AFTER: Duration = Duration::from_secs(10);    // follower may campaign only after its promise lapses
/// Every time a member reads (`LogEntry.at`, leases) is its member clock: `max(Date.now(), newest stamp)`, stamped on
/// every `/raft` call and reply and edge call, never decreasing. A busy isolate's `Date.now()` is its last I/O poll, up
/// to 1.7 s before the event it handles [measured, `tests/am1/results/lease-clock.md`]; a sender running ahead raises
/// it by at most its own drift, inside DRIFT. Reads take a round while a stamp of the last 8 led it by DRIFT / 2.
pub const DRIFT: Duration = Duration::from_millis(500);          // subtracted from the leader's own lease end [guess]
pub const CALL_DEADLINE: Duration = Duration::from_secs(1);      // receipt and member-to-member calls, hedged; the edge
                                                                 // waits COMMIT_HOLD + CALL_DEADLINE (2 s) for a held
                                                                 // `commit`, so a `suspect` needs 2 s of silence there
pub const COMMIT_RETRIES: u8 = 2;                                // core resends of a failed commit-path call, outside the 10% budget
pub const APPEND_BYTES_MAX: usize = 100_000;                     // per AppendEntries past its first entry [measured]
pub const LATE_LIMIT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(10);        // past CALL_DEADLINE a late answer is stepped [guess]
pub const LOG_WINDOW: u64 = 1_000; pub const LOG_BYTES: usize = 8_000_000;  // `load`'s log: entries, bytes [guess]
/// A leader steps down when, over `window_s`, its commit-path error rate passes `commit_errors` or its storage confirm
/// p99 passes `confirm_p99_ms`, timed from the flush (`confirm-storage`). A new leader counts a follower's failed calls
/// only once that follower answered it in its term, because the quorum commits without a follower it has not reached,
/// such as a killed leader still resetting: without this, 7 of 60 groups changed leader again 21.8 to 25.8 s after a
/// leader kill, and with it 0 of 90 did [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp4.md`, "Leader kill"]. Misses to one follower
/// within CALL_DEADLINE of its last counted one count once: a 1.9 s stall missed 17 to 31 rounds [measured, LM-1]. The
/// commit-error rule also needs at least `commit_fail_min` failed calls to every follower in the window, as the confirm
/// rule needs `confirm_slow_min`: a far follower misses CALL_DEADLINE about once in 10 to 15 small calls with no fault,
/// and at 10 to 30 calls a window one or two misses pass 5%. AppendEntries of 500 kB or more missed it in 230 of 365
/// soak calls [measured, `tests/m1/results/m1-f43.md`], so large packs may trip it unfaulted [inference; F41]. A failed
/// call whose deadline elapsed while the leader's own storage confirm was outstanding (begun before the call and
/// unconfirmed at its deadline) counts toward neither `commit_errors` nor `commit_fail_min`, because the confirm rule
/// owns the leader's storage stalls: in two LF-12 phases one 3.5 s confirm made every failed call to the near follower
/// in the window, and the commit-error rule moved a healthy group, once onto its far member [measured,
/// `tests/m0/results/m0-wp12.md`, LF-12, ninth pass]. That the exclusion keeps such a leader is [inference]: replayed
/// offline on those records it removed 3 of 3 and 4 of 4 failed calls to the near follower, so neither would have
/// tripped, and it was inert in 26 tenth-pass phase-members, where no confirm passed CALL_DEADLINE [measured, same,
/// tenth pass]. With the count, leader kills under load gave 0 follow-on changes in 90 groups against 2 in 90 without
/// it, and leaders still left a site failing 30% of calls in 2.9 to 4.1 s and slow storage in 2.8 to 4.2 s [measured,
/// `tests/m0/results/m0-wp4.md`, "`STEP_DOWN` target on the leader's calls and the other follower's probe; F37 as
/// `min3-`"]. Failing writes were not run with the count, and a quiet group needs about 10 calls per follower a window
/// at 30% errors to collect 3 [inference]. `confirm_slow_min` has this limit: a slow leader confirming 2 writes/s left
/// in p50 10.4 s, max 21.9 s, against Milestone 0's 2.8 to 4.2 s [measured, simulator, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp3.md`,
/// "Liveness against Milestone 0"; F41]. On errors, it first asks the other follower to ping each candidate target, and
/// holds for one window if no candidate's link reads under `commit_errors`: every site is failing alike (the June 2025
/// class), and a transfer only adds an election. B0-25 ran down to 79% without this hold (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`,
/// B0-25). The confirm rule trips only on at least `confirm_slow_min` confirmations over `confirm_p99_ms` in the
/// window. A group committing about 5 entries/s holds under 100 confirmations in 10 s, so a p99 is its maximum: in
/// M0-WP9 one slow write moved the leader 6 times in 4 of 9 runs with no fault injected [measured,
/// `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, "STEP_DOWN under load"]. Durable Object storage itself stalls: a bare object writing
/// and syncing at a member's rate saw stalls over 500 ms about every 5 s, and 7 of 168 such objects hit 3 or 4 in one
/// 10 s window, so 6 leaves a margin of two [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp4.md`, "F35"]. A `STEP_DOWN` transfer
/// targets a follower outside RESET_QUARANTINE of its own `stepped_down_at`, which every AppendEntries reply carries.
/// On a confirm trip the leader's calls to the target must not fail over `commit_errors` with `commit_fail_min` failed
/// (rate alone held a stall with `to: None`, `tests/m0/results/carried-2.md`), and no probe runs. On a commit-error
/// trip the leader's calls fail over `commit_errors` to every follower by definition, so the target qualifies instead
/// on the other follower's calls to it: that follower runs a `linkErrors` probe of the target, which must read under
/// `commit_errors`, and a probe that cannot run disqualifies it. Among those it takes the one with the fewest failed
/// calls, then the most caught up, and holds if none qualifies. A target's own calls to the other follower measure that
/// follower too: after an election left a failing-write site as a follower, that reading qualified only the sick site,
/// and a group moved onto it three times; the other follower's probe of the target picked the healthy site in the same
/// case (`dr-writes2` moved to C), and B0-06 passed 12 of 12 runs, 6 of them failing-write, with leaders off slow
/// storage in 2.3 to 5.6 s, off failing calls in 2.4 to 5.2 s, and off failing writes by election in 4.9 to 13.3 s
/// [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp4.md`, "`STEP_DOWN` target on the leader's calls and the other follower's probe"].
/// A sick prober can make the leader hold, never transfer onto the sick site [measured, same]. Without the quarantine
/// two healthy sites passed a group back and forth on call errors while a third was sick. In B0-33 p4r4, a leader whose
/// calls to SIN had failed 60% of the time quarantined its only healthy peer, transferred to SIN, and SIN, overloaded
/// as leader, cost 6,188 `suspect` reports and every remaining commit [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, fourth
/// pass]. Judging commit-error targets on the leader's calls could never transfer, because the trip needs both
/// followers over `commit_errors`: p5r1 and p5r2 logged 26 holds and 0 transfers [measured, fifth pass]. The first
/// measured transfer on the other-follower probe was B0-33 p6r2: a leader whose appends to apac missed CALL_DEADLINE
/// for 30 s tripped at 0.06/0.09, the probes read 0.00, and it moved to the weur follower, which held [measured, sixth
/// pass]. With `AGENT_INLINE_ENTRY` on every group, an apac leader's agent pushes of 1 MiB are all staged, its entries
/// stay under 20 KB, and it held 600 s with commit p99 598 ms. While placement fails, Human and MergeQueue packs up to
/// 1 MiB still go inline, and the apac leader stepped down once in 9 s by the commit-error rule (6 and 3 failed calls,
/// so `commit_fail_min` was met) [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-31, eighth pass; the fourth pass, before
/// the budget applied on every group, stepped down within 10 s in both halves]. `LOOP_LAG` trips it per sync (Scale).
pub struct Health { pub window_s: u16, pub commit_errors: f32, pub commit_fail_min: u8, pub confirm_p99_ms: u32,
                    pub confirm_slow_min: u8 }
pub const STEP_DOWN: Health = Health { window_s: 10, commit_errors: 0.05, commit_fail_min: 3, confirm_p99_ms: 500,
                                       confirm_slow_min: 6 };  // [measured, B0-06; commit_fail_min M0-WP4 min3]
pub const SUSPECT: (usize, Duration) = (2, Duration::from_secs(10));  // distinct colos reporting, and the window
/// Pre-vote and transfers refuse a candidate whose two `boots`, or whose `stepped_down_at`, fall inside it. A voter that
/// has seen no leader for 2 x ELECTION_AFTER waives it. Where every site resets often, every `STEP_DOWN` holds (F42).
pub const RESET_QUARANTINE: Duration = Duration::from_secs(300);
/// Member SQLite bytes no member of a repository's groups passes, because the text the parent's metadata tier holds
/// moves to a child group first. It caps the tier at 10 GB minus LOG_MAX_BYTES, the core tier, and a margin [guess
/// margin]. Each step runs in band on the parent leader's alarm. Start. At SPLIT_AT of `ctx.storage.sql.databaseSize`
/// (https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/api/storage-api/), which leaves out free pages [doc: workerd
/// `getDatabaseSize`], the leader records `MetaSplit { child: RepoMeta(repo) }`. Seed. At its newest committed metadata
/// snapshot p, the leader records `MetaSeed { at: p, key }`, creates `RepoMeta(repo)` on the parent's placement record
/// (`repo-meta:<id>`, hosted by the repository family), and records the same `MetaSeed` there. Each child member
/// restores that snapshot in the background; nothing writes its tier after, and the child decides nothing. Commit
/// point. Once the child leader has restored it, the parent records `MetaDropped { seed: p }`, then paced `MetaSlim`
/// entries until one pass is done, each over the next SLIM_ROWS rows: a row last written at or before p loses the text
/// no `decide` reads (issue, comment, review and release bodies, a task's prompt and summary), which the child's
/// snapshot holds, and `provenance` and `path_set` rows, which no read serves, go. A row written after p keeps its
/// text. Every table and column `decide` reads stays, so every command commits on the parent with no new `deps`, no
/// write gets 503, and consumers, the feed, ids, `RestoredTo`, and `RepoReset` keep the parent's log. Installs. A metadata snapshot is due the moment the pass ends, so a later `InstallSnapshot` names the slimmed tier, not the whole one from before `MetaDropped`, and a running install keeps the snapshots it started from for its whole life: a newer source from any leader, whatever snapshots it names, extends the job to its `through` with the further archives when they continue the job's, a source from the same leader with an earlier `through` (a late delivery) changes nothing, and a batch that fails is tried again before the job is abandoned. The job starts again only when no install runs, when the newer archives leave a gap after the job's, or when another leader or term names an earlier `through`; each restart logs its reason. The pinned snapshots stay readable because a tier keeps every snapshot inside `RESTORE_WINDOW` and no archive object is deleted, so a pin needs nothing from the leader. A member whose install job is stored and has started, below the source's `through` (it is past the leader's truncation, so its own tier can serve nothing the group still needs, whether or not it applied `MetaDropped`), drops its own metadata tables before it loads and records that it did, in the job's transaction; from then until `Write::Install` its tier is unloaded: no metadata or narrow `api` read is served (a follower names the leader, the edge moves on), it does not campaign, resume, or take a transfer, and the fold audit lists it as loading, not as a mismatch. An abandoned job leaves the mark, so the member stays unloaded until the next install. Its peak is its own tier less the metadata tables plus the scratch load [measured locally, `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "An installing parent member drops its own metadata tier"]; a member that fell behind before `MetaDropped` loads the pre-slim tier, about 7.7 GB at B0-30's size [inference]. Reads. A member
/// answering a read or rendering a reply fills each slimmed field from the child's row, read once through `fresh` and
/// kept, since it never changes; a deleted row is never filled; an answer that missed a child row is rebuilt once the
/// member has read it. The parent's FTS5 rows drop slimmed text (G13). The child's log holds its bootstrap, a `Noop` a
/// term, and `MetaSeed`, so it needs no archive, and its seed snapshot outlives retention (G4, G6;
/// `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "A split moves only the text `decide` never reads").
pub const CHILD_GROUP_AT: u64 = 8 << 30; pub const SPLIT_AT: u64 = 7 << 30;  // a seed restores the tier for hours [guess]
pub const SLIM_ROWS: usize = 512;   // and at most SLIM_BYTES = 128 KiB of rows a `MetaSlim` [measured, smoke 2]
pub enum SplitEntry { MetaSplit { child: ChainId }, MetaSeed { at: Index, key: R2Key }, MetaDropped { seed: Index },
                      MetaSlim }
/// One import entry (SHARED, `rest:migrations`). `apply` applies each `event` with `created` and `author` in place of the
/// entry's `at` and actor, and `merged` records a merged pull request with no tree-diff check.
pub struct ImportBatch { pub import: Ulid, pub items: Vec<ImportItem> }
pub struct ImportItem { pub event: Event, pub legacy: i64, pub created: Timestamp, pub author: AccountId,
                        pub merged: Option<(ObjectId, Timestamp, AccountId)> }

// ---- Leadership ----
/// Raft leadership transfer. The leader stops appending and serving `fresh`, brings an undrained `to` to its last
/// index, and sends `TimeoutNow`. Before `TimeoutNow`, the leader waits up to 2 x CALL_DEADLINE for the other follower
/// to accept an AppendEntries that carries the `transfer_id`, and retries the target's catch-up and `TimeoutNow` until
/// the LEASE ends, not COMMIT_RETRIES; without the wait, a follower that had not seen the transfer refused the target's
/// vote as promised, and a group had no leader for 40 s [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp4.md`, B0-07]. While it runs,
/// every AppendEntries carries `transfer: Some((to, transfer_id))`, acks to those rounds extend no lease, and a voter
/// disregards its promise to the old leader only for a transfer vote that names the `transfer_id` of the last
/// AppendEntries it accepted [inference from Raft section 3.10]. A granted transfer vote also promises the target; else
/// a report to the old leader let it win the other follower back [measured, simulator, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp3.md`,
/// bug 1]. It lasts the campaign's 3 s, or a target that could not win held both voters leaderless (F67). A transfer is
/// abandoned after one LEASE, at once on `TransferRefused`, or, for a `/transfer` or drain's, once its target loses
/// answers while the other follower does not (F67 rule 13): it sends `transfer: None`, serves `fresh` after a round. It
/// transfers when its site is drained, STEP_DOWN trips, an older release refuses its entry, or `home` recovered (5
/// calls in a `STEP_DOWN` window, none failed, outside RESET_QUARANTINE, not tripped) and holds every committed entry,
/// retrying 60 s after a failure [guess, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp3.md`]; that hand-back waits for a quiet leader (at most 16 commands queued, the entry in flight at most ENTRY_SOFT/2) and goes anyway 10 min after it was first due [guess, `home-quiet`]. Leases read the member clock (`DRIFT`).
pub async fn transfer(g: &Leader, to: SiteId) -> Result<(), TransferError>;
pub enum TransferError { NotLeader(NotLeader), Busy(TransferId), Target(SiteId) /* self, drained, quarantined */ }
/// Edge to a follower on another site when a leader's receipt misses CALL_DEADLINE, once per chain, `about` and term
/// per isolate per SUSPECT_GAP (1 s). `about` missed or answered `NotLeader` naming none; a report counts only if this
/// follower promised and heard it this term, even once forgotten (else a transfer's old leader barred the new; F67,
/// `m1-wp3.md` bug 2), and only with its appends from it late by CALL_DEADLINE (`late-suspect`). Two colos' reports in
/// 10 s stop its promises to a renewing leader until 2 x SUSPECT's window or a higher term. For that window, not ended
/// by a higher term, its pre-vote refuses that leader and it hands off to no such member (M0-WP2: 24 s without; F67
/// rule 10), unless every report came in that leader's transfer, a bar that ends once another leader is heard (rule
/// 11). A follower whose promise lapsed campaigns on one report, or once its read-index batch is unanswered for 3 s
/// (then dropped, rule 12), naming no leader; a withheld leader's appends are no leader seen. A read-index read on such a member is shed with that leader named, and the edge reports it once, never reading on the leader (`b032-shed`: 213 to 484 plain reads a second hit a stalled leader otherwise [measured, m3fr15]).
/// A group with a request waiting and two voters up and linked completes one within 30 s on 4,000 live seeds [measured,
/// simulator, `tests/m0/results/f67.md`]: a leader whose entry, read, or transfer no quorum answered for ELECTION_AFTER
/// stops leading (F67). Only `home` gets `/campaign`; pre-vote denies a higher site id with an equal log [inference,
/// wire-fix]. A leader paces its background entries (`MetaSlim`, `Promoted`, `Archived`) by twice each one's commit, at
/// most 10 s (back to back: 110 STEP_DOWNs in 2.5 h [measured, `b030-split`, smoke 1]). A voter with such work due and
/// no leader seen for 2 x ELECTION_AFTER acts as on one `suspect` report (a group sat leaderless 39 min). Only a voter
/// that may lead and was not refused as suspected denies a rival as contending. A promise bars
/// votes for a rival of its leader, not for that leader, and an edge answered `NotLeader` naming none also reports to
/// that member, so a deposed leader with the newest log can win the next term while promises run (11 s idle without).
pub async fn suspect(follower: &Stub, from: Colo, about: SiteId, term: Term) -> Result<Verdict, SuspectError>;
pub struct Colo(u32);   // the edge's data center; `suspect` counts distinct ones
/// Only `Leader`, `Won`, and `Resumed` move an edge's receipt; `MayNotLead` is a drained or `no_lead_until` member.
pub enum Verdict { Leader, Resumed, Started, Bootstrapping, Promised, Busy, Won, Lost, MayNotLead }
pub enum SuspectError { Call(CallError) }   // the report's call failed; a member judges every report it receives
/// One per (observer site, target site), living on the observer's site. Edges and members there count calls to the
/// target site that miss CALL_DEADLINE. `graft-ops` drains a target only when both other sites' objects report its burn.
pub struct SiteHealth { pub observer: SiteId, pub target: SiteId, pub calls: u64, pub missed: u64 }

// ---- The write path ----
/// The only write path for chained state. It runs in the leader member, one entry in flight per group. A command joins
/// the next entry, up to ENTRY_SOFT (Scale), or ENTRY_SOFT_DEEP (150 KB) while more than DEEP_QUEUE (2) entries' worth of Human and MergeQueue bytes waits (`soft-grow`), frozen when the entry in flight commits, or once it has been in flight its mean round
/// less one mean dependency read (B0-29). When one mean read outlasts one mean round, the leader holds up to ceil(read / round) frozen batches (at most READ_AHEAD), freezing each and asking its reads one mean round after the one before, so each batch's reads land about when the entry before it commits and the cycle is a round plus `decide`, not a read plus `decide`: B0-31's apac leader, reading enam's identity groups, held one entry per 413 ms on a 198 ms round and a 368 ms read [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "B0-31's buckets-up commit tail"].
/// The leader reads each distinct dependency and `CredentialShard` once through `fresh`, in parallel, after the batch's commands were admitted, and decides batches in the order they were frozen, each only once the entry before it applied, with exactly the chains `deps()` names on that fold, reading any missing and dropping any it no longer names, so a read asked before an earlier entry moved a command's dependency is never used, and a revocation or suspension binds every command whose entry's read arrived after it, however long it queued. A dependency read with no answer within its chain's hedge delay sends a second request to the leader beside the first and takes the first answer (`dep-hedge`); a hedge to another member by read-index won 7 of 1,198 hedges over B0-31 m65r31 and B0-29 m3fb29, and is reversed (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`dep-hedge-other`"). A repository leader finds its owner's `Authz` group by the record it joined with, and the install's own `Authz` (which an App command's entry also reads) by the install account's record, read once from its `Placement` row on `Names` and kept beside the join; a read that fails under either record looks the chain up again, and a kept record it replaced is dropped (`install-record`): read per entry, that `Placement` read made the install `Authz` read p50 277 ms slower than the owner's, read beside it, on B0-31 m65r31 [measured, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`, "The install `Authz` read's extra hop"]. The delay is the p90 of the chain's last 64 read times in the isolate, within 100 ms and DEP_HEDGE (1 s) [guess], and DEP_HEDGE before 16, so about one read in ten is hedged whether the identity leader is near or a continent away; a fixed 150 ms would hedge about 80% of B0-31's apac reads of enam [inference, `tests/m0/results/dep-hedge-adaptive.md`] (`dep-hedge-adaptive`): F83 found single requests to an identity leader held 4.6 to 5.4 s before its handler while it served the rest at once, and one such read stalled a group for 5.2 s [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "F83"]. Each request is a leader's `fresh` read sent after the batch's commands were admitted, so the read kept is one of them and `decide`'s inputs are unchanged.
/// Holding more batches changes neither rule: still one entry in flight, `decide` still sees the fold at `applied`, the entry's earlier commands, and reads asked after its own commands were admitted, and a follower still verifies k+1 only after it applies k, since nothing reaches a follower before its entry is appended.
/// Each command's `decide` sees an `EntryView`: the fold at `applied` plus the events of the
/// entry's earlier commands, on a scratch transaction the leader rolls back once the entry is decided, so a refusal can
/// rest on an earlier command; a BF-13 simulator run had a compare-and-set refused `Stale` only by such a command, its
/// leader lost before commit, and the refusal never returned [measured, simulator, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp2.md`, BF-13].
/// The leader proves its lease, appends at last + 1, and sends to both followers at once (and to learners, which never
/// count) before its own append is durable, counting itself toward the quorum only once its storage confirms (Raft
/// dissertation 10.2.1); a follower confirms before it replies and matches entries by digest, telling a reset leader's
/// lost entry apart. Waiting for that write cost p99 645 to 791 ms (Scale). Tickets resolve on the quorum:
///   Normal, all Full:     leader + 1 follower       (2 regions, 2 site scripts)
///   Normal, 2 Full + W:   both Full members         (2 regions; the witness only votes)
///   Degraded:             surviving Full + Witness  (1 content copy; stated in "Downtime")
///   Solo:                 the member alone          (the platform's 3-of-5 followers; stated in "Downtime")
/// Every ticket in an entry, a refusal included, resolves only when that entry commits, because a refusal may rest on an
/// earlier command of the same entry. An entry whose commands were all refused is appended with no events. The leader
/// holds `commit` until the command's ticket resolves, up to COMMIT_HOLD, and answers the outcome, built by the same
/// `outcome` logic: a ticket resolves only on the commit-advance path once the quorum rule holds, on an AGENT_WAIT or
/// queue refusal, or at the end of leadership, so `Acked` is still built only inside `outcome`, where the held call
/// resolves after the quorum rule holds. A call held past COMMIT_HOLD answers `Admitted { ticket }`, and the edge calls
/// `outcome` once, which waits with a deadline of AGENT_WAIT plus one round. An edge whose `commit` timed out asks
/// `outcome` by request id, which answers from the `request` rows, waits on the ticket, or says unknown, and then
/// re-sends with the same id. On B0-33 this cut the leader from 750 to 950 to 459 to 498 req/s, `outcome` from 270 to
/// 330/s to 1 to 10/s, with up to 576 calls held at once; 0.1 to 0.2% of commands fell back in a steady run and 9% in
/// the run with a transfer [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, sixth pass]. The edge waits COMMIT_HOLD plus
/// CALL_DEADLINE for the `commit` reply. Deadlines, hedges, retries, `SiteHealth`, and `suspect` apply only to receipts
/// and member-to-member calls, never to time a command spends queued. A hedge is only a `suspect` report to a follower
/// on another site and never forwards the command, so a command is in flight at one leader at a time. A ticket from a
/// deposed term returns `NotLeader` under the ticket as issued, and the caller re-sends under its `RequestId`. `boot`
/// is drawn at random when the object starts, and no seq repeats within it (`tests/m0/results/admitted-bytes.md`). A
/// reset leader that resumes `led_term` has lost its queue, so `outcome` for a ticket of another `boot` answers
/// `NotLeader` and the caller re-sends. Milestone 0's 4-hour linearizability run failed without `boot`: the reset leader reissued `seq`
/// from 1 and answered an old ticket with a newer command's result (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`, B0-11). A new leader
/// whose `Noop` reaches no quorum in 3 s [guess] returns to follower, fails its tickets `NotLeader`, and keeps
/// `led_term` to resume later, as a resumed one whose round failed does [inference, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp2.md`].
pub async fn commit<C: Command>(g: &Leader, cmd: C) -> Result<Committed<C>, CallError>;
pub async fn outcome<C: Command>(g: &Leader, ticket: Ticket) -> Result<Acked<C::Out>, CommitError<C::Err>>;
/// Takes the command the edge would re-send under the same id, because `replay` is a method on it (`tests/m1/results/m1-wp2.md`).
pub async fn outcome_by_request<C: Command>(g: &Leader, id: RequestId, cmd: &C) -> Result<Option<Acked<C::Out>>, CommitError<C::Err>>;
/// What a held `commit` answers: the outcome when the ticket resolved within COMMIT_HOLD, else the ticket.
pub enum Committed<C: Command> { Done(Result<Acked<C::Out>, CommitError<C::Err>>), Admitted(Admitted) }
pub struct Admitted { pub ticket: Ticket }
pub const COMMIT_HOLD: Duration = Duration::from_secs(1);        // [guess]
pub struct Ticket { pub term: Term, pub boot: u64, pub seq: u64 }
/// Any reply a member gives when it cannot lead the call. `config` is its latest committed config with full member
/// names, or `None` when it has none, so callers follow moves, regenerations, and learners without a lookup.
pub struct NotLeader { pub term: Term, pub leader: Option<SiteId>, pub config: Option<(Index, GroupConfig)> }
/// Every edge-to-member and member-to-member message is a stored-format record under SHARED's two-release rule, and a
/// caller sends a new command, message kind, or field only once `FormatsEnable` enables its format, so an edge on N+1
/// talks k to leaders on N. A released wire tag never moves (SHARED). A member that cannot decode one answers
/// `Unsupported`, changing nothing. The edge renders it as a retryable 503 (`Retry-After: 5` [guess]), never a refusal.
pub struct Unsupported { pub release: ReleaseId, pub format: Format }
/// `TooLarge`: the member's own `COMMAND_MAX` check, never decided, rendered as the edge's refusal [inference].
pub enum CallError { NotLeader(NotLeader), Unsupported(Unsupported), TooLarge { len: usize, max: usize } }
/// Minted by the edge, one per client request, and reused for every re-send of that request's command.
pub struct RequestId(Ulid);
/// Per-group core-tier fold table `request(id, index, events)`, filled by `apply` from each entry's `requests` and kept
/// REQUEST_RETENTION after the entry's `at`, each event in its `Fold::row_event` form, without inline pack bytes, in
/// SQLite only, read by id (BF-20). A command whose id has a row is not decided again: `outcome` returns `replay` of
/// the stored events with the stored index. A command whose id matches one already queued or in flight joins that
/// ticket. A command `decide` refuses leaves no row, but its entry still commits (BF-13), so a refusal that must leave
/// no entry (`COMMAND_MAX`, SHARED's 10,000-ref atomic cap) is the edge's, before `commit` (M1-WP6).
pub const REQUEST_RETENTION: Duration = Duration::from_secs(48 * 3600);   // [guess]; above the 24 h orphan sweep; F15
/// The largest encoded command. It sits below the 1.5 MB entry, so one command always fits an empty entry. The edge
/// encodes each command before `commit` and refuses one over the cap: REST and GraphQL with 422 [guess], git as in
/// SHARED, "Refs and pushes". The leader checks again and answers `CallError::TooLarge`, which the edge renders the same way.
pub const COMMAND_MAX: usize = 1_200_000;                        // bytes [guess]
/// What the leader re-reads for one command once per entry: the actor's `Account` row, and the `Tokens` shard row of
/// SHARED's `Credential` (a stored token's shard, `jti_shard` of a stateless token, `fingerprint_shard` of an SSH key).
/// Web sessions, runner JWTs, and job runtime tokens name only the `Account` row (a session's key adds its issue time).
/// Commands that `graft-ops` or a consumer submits name neither. A missing, revoked, or suspended row refuses with 401.
pub struct CredentialShard { pub account: Option<AccountId>, pub key: Option<CredentialKey> }
pub enum CredentialKey { Token { shard: TokenShard, hash: [u8; 32] }, Jti { shard: TokenShard, jti: Ulid },
                         SshKey { shard: TokenShard, fingerprint: Fingerprint }, Session { issued: Timestamp } }
pub trait Command { type Out; type Err;
    fn tiers(&self) -> TierSet;
    /// Built from the command's `Principal` (`actor.credential`).
    fn credential(&self) -> CredentialShard;
    /// Read from the fold at `applied`, never from the command. For a repository command, the owner's `Authz` and the
    /// enterprise's come from the core tier's `RepoSettings { owner, enterprise }`, so a transfer changes them by entry.
    fn deps(&self, fold: &Fresh) -> SmallVec<[ChainId; 3]>;    // read once per entry through `fresh`
    /// `deps` are the folds the leader read once per entry through `fresh`, in `deps()` order.
    fn decide(&self, fold: &EntryView, deps: &[Fresh]) -> Result<(SmallVec<[Event; 4]>, Self::Out), Self::Err>;  // pure
    /// The output for a duplicate request, rebuilt from the events its first commit stored. `Push` reports each refused
    /// ref's own reason from SHARED's `RepoEvent::Refused`; reporting it `Stale` failed git's t5541 #20 (M1-WP8).
    fn replay(&self, events: &[Event]) -> Self::Out; }
/// Built only inside `outcome`, from the acks the leader counted. `report_status` and every 2xx renderer take `&Acked`,
/// which cannot cross a binding, so the member renders the reply bytes and the edge frames them (M1-WP6). A duplicate
/// answered from its `request` row gets the same logic with the current leader's latest commit proof, which covers the
/// stored index because the row exists only once its entry applied, built once the term's `Noop` committed.
pub struct Acked<T> { pub index: Index, pub out: T, quorum: QuorumProof }
/// The fold at `applied` with the earlier commands' events of the entry being decided applied on top, uncommitted.
pub struct EntryView<'a> { fold: &'a Fold, earlier: &'a [Event] }
struct QuorumProof { sites: SmallVec<[SiteId; 3]>, regions: u8 /* >= 2 unless Degraded or Solo */, leader: SiteId,
                     index: Index, entry_at: Timestamp, at: Timestamp /* commit */, acks: SmallVec<[DurableAck; 3]> }
pub struct DurableAck { pub site: SiteId, pub durable: Index, pub at: Timestamp }   // per counted site, leader's clock
/// Followers run this before they store entry k+1, using the release they run. They have applied k first, which the
/// append for k+1 makes possible because it carries commit index k. The entry must decode, chain to `prev`, and use an
/// enabled format. For `Refs` and `Merged`, the follower replays the entry's events in order on a scratch transaction
/// over its fold at k and checks each `expect` against that running state, then rolls the transaction back. Each
/// refusal carries the refuser's release. Only when the refuser runs an older release than the leader and the entry is
/// uncommitted does the leader truncate its uncommitted tail, transfer (to the refuser when it qualifies), and persist
/// `no_lead_until`. If both followers on its own release refuse one uncommitted entry, it is the leader's own defect:
/// it alarms and truncates the tail as a new `lead_seq`, so a delayed older round cannot replace what commits there
/// (F67). On any other refusal (same or newer release, or a committed entry) the leader keeps leading, adds the refuser
/// to `cannot_follow`, alarms, and `graft-ops` halts the site day. A member truncates an undecodable tail at boot.
pub fn verify(e: &LogEntry, fold: &FoldView) -> Result<(), Refusal>;

// ---- Merge queue ----
/// One pull request's place in a base branch's queue. `group_tip` is the base plus the changes of this entry and every
/// earlier one, built by `GitCacheShard::merge` with the tree-diff check. `parent_tip` is the previous entry's
/// `group_tip`, or the base tip for the first entry. A built entry has the queue ref
/// `refs/heads/gh-readonly-queue/{base}/pr-{n}-{parent_tip}` at `group_tip` [unverified which SHA GitHub names].
pub struct MergeQueueEntry { pub pr: PrNumber, pub group_tip: Oid, pub parent_tip: Oid }
/// SHARED's `RepoEvent::Queue`, core tier, on the base repository's chain. `Enqueued` pins the pull request's head.
/// `GroupBuilt` appends entries after the last built one, or replaces every built entry when its first `parent_tip` is
/// the base tip, and `refs` creates and deletes the queue refs in the same entry. `Ejected` removes one pull request and
/// deletes the queue refs of it and of every later entry, which wait to be built again. `Landed` rides in the
/// `QueueMerge` entry and deletes the landed entries' refs. The `Webhooks` consumer renders
/// `merge_group.checks_requested` from `GroupBuilt`, `merge_group.destroyed` from `Ejected` and `Landed`, and
/// `pull_request.enqueued` and `pull_request.dequeued` from `Enqueued` and `Ejected`.
pub enum QueueEvent {
    Enqueued { n: IssueNumber, base: RefName, head: Oid, actor: Actor },
    GroupBuilt { base: RefName, entries: SmallVec<[MergeQueueEntry; 8]>, refs: Vec<RefUpdate>, pack: Option<PackRef> },
    Ejected { n: IssueNumber, reason: EjectReason, refs: Vec<RefUpdate> },
    Landed { base: RefName, through: IssueNumber, refs: Vec<RefUpdate> } }
pub enum EjectReason { CheckFailed { name: Box<str> }, CheckTimeout { name: Box<str> }, HeadMoved, Conflict,
                       Protection(RuleViolation), Closed, Dequeued }
/// impl Command for the first three `QueueEvent`s, `tiers() = Core`, MergeQueue lane. The edge submits `Enqueue` and
/// `Dequeue`, `credential()` from `actor`; the `MergeQueue` consumer submits `Build` and `Eject` with `actor: None`.
/// `decide` refuses `Enqueue` unless the pull request is open, not queued, and passes `check_protection` without its
/// required checks; `Build` unless every entry's pull request is queued at its pinned head and the `parent_tip` chain
/// holds from the base tip or the last built entry; and `Eject` of a pull request not queued.
pub struct QueueOp { pub op: QueueAction, pub actor: Option<Principal> }
pub enum QueueAction { Enqueue { n: IssueNumber, head: Oid }, Dequeue { n: IssueNumber },
                       Build { base: RefName, entries: SmallVec<[MergeQueueEntry; 8]>, pack: DurablePack },
                       Eject { n: IssueNumber, reason: EjectReason } }
/// impl Command, both tiers for its `Merged`s, MergeQueue lane, no actor: one group's landing, all or nothing. `plans`
/// are built entries in queue order from the queue's head, and `entry` is the last. On a running state, `decide` checks
/// that the base tip equals the first `parent_tip`, and for each plan that `base_old` is its entry's `parent_tip`,
/// `commit` is its `group_tip`, `head` is the pinned head of an open PR, and `check_protection` passes with required
/// checks read on the queue refs: every entry's under `Grouping::AllGreen`, the last entry's under `HeadGreen`, which
/// also refuses on any entry's failed or pending one. Any failure refuses the whole command with the failing PR's
/// number. Otherwise one entry carries a `Merged` per PR, each with its own base `RefUpdate` from `parent_tip` to
/// `group_tip` and `by` set to the enqueuing user, then `Landed`. So the base moves only to a `group_tip` that descends
/// from its current value, and queue tips only fast-forward. Out: each merge commit. Err: `(IssueNumber, MergeError)`.
pub struct QueueMerge { pub plans: Vec<(IssueNumber, MergePlan)>, pub entry: MergeQueueEntry }
/// The `MergeQueue` consumer (SHARED, "Event fan-out") reads the base chain's `Queue`, `Refs`, `RequiredCheck`,
```
