/// `PrClosed`, `Merged`, and `Rule` events and, like `PrSync`, runs each batch's first attempt in the leader's isolate.
/// Per base, under `MergeQueueSettings`: it builds once `min_merge` PRs wait or `min_wait_minutes` passes (per base)
/// and fewer than `max_build` are built, asking `GitCacheShard::merge` for each next entry on the last `group_tip` and
/// submitting `Build` with the pack inline (at most 900 KB [inference]) or `Eject { Conflict }`. It ejects on a head
/// push or a close, and the first built entry not yet passing on a failed required conclusion on its queue ref or none
/// within `check_timeout_minutes`. It lands the oldest passing built entries, at most `max_merge`, by `QueueMerge`; a
/// refusal ejects the named PR. A base move not by `QueueMerge` rebuilds from the new tip; a redelivery costs a refusal.

// ---- Agent load and agent safety (journey 9; the layer is design/AGENT-LAYER.md) ----
/// Leader admission inside group commit. Lanes in every entry are Human and MergeQueue, then Agent up to AGENT_FLOOR of
/// capacity, then Bot, then Agent up to AGENT_SHARE. Capacity is measured entries/s times commands per entry by stored
/// bytes (no reply render); the Agent cap is a token bucket of AGENT_SHARE x capacity. A command is Agent when its
/// actor is an `AgentApp` (`Graftyard MCP` included), when a ref under an agent prefix or a PR with `AgentMeta` caused it
/// (PrSync, test-merge records, check runs, statuses), when an App token's push carries a trailer that classes it
/// (`TrailerKey`), when a user token sends `X-Graftyard-Agent-Session` (which grants nothing), or when its actor, user
/// tokens included, passes DEMOTE_PER_MIN write commands on this group. A trailer never classes a user-token push on its
/// own. Fair queuing by App or user runs inside each class. Overflow waits up to AGENT_WAIT. Git then gets HTTP 200 with
/// report-status `ng <ref> agent admission full, retry after <n>s` and a `remote:` line, and REST gets 429 with
/// `retry-after` and a "secondary rate limit" message. Under pressure, which is more agent bytes queued than one entry
/// holds or more agent commands queued than the agent bucket admits in 1 s [inference, the M0-WP9 prototype's rule],
/// only `PackRef::Stored` pushes queue. At AGENT_QUEUE_MAX the leader refuses agent commands at once, before staging,
/// and the edge renders it. At 150 admitted agent commands/s the 1,000-command cap is about 7 s of queue and binds long
/// before the byte cap [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-33]. Task API commands (create, cancel, and `PATCH`
/// of an `AgentTask`) do not count toward DEMOTE_PER_MIN, because a human creating tasks quickly is still a human. It
/// counts an actor's last minute of committed writes in the group's fold, which a leader change keeps (M0-WP9's leader
/// memory classed 108 of 399 writes Human after one), plus the leader's unresolved and refused ones [inference, AM1-WP1].
pub enum AdmitClass { Human, MergeQueue, Bot, Agent }
pub const AGENT_SHARE: f32 = 0.5;                                // [guess]
pub const AGENT_FLOOR: f32 = 0.2;                                // of capacity, which Bot cannot take [guess]
pub const DEMOTE_PER_MIN: u32 = 60;                              // GitHub's content limit is 80 a minute
pub const AGENT_WAIT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(30);  // AGENT_PUSH_BOUND (5 s from edge arrival; B0-32) holds an agent push: past it before its commit is sent, `ng`; sent, it carries what is left (`Admit::budget`), refused at once if its queue wait is longer and expired if still queued then, never once in an entry [measured, m3fr15: 0 of 500 within 5 s without it]
pub const AGENT_QUEUE_MAX: (u32, usize) = (1_000, 64 << 20);     // queued agent commands and bytes per group [guess]
/// A group above HOT_PUSHES pushes/s over its last 5 s of committed entries, read from the log so a new leader agrees,
/// inlines any class's push only up to INLINE_MAX_HOT while `PackStore::place` succeeds. Every group also caps each
/// entry at AGENT_INLINE_ENTRY of Agent-class inline pack bytes, and an agent pack that would pass it is staged as
/// `PackRef::Stored` (while placement fails, refused at once); Human and MergeQueue packs do not count. It is the rule
/// for a group not yet hot: without it, fresh groups and new leaders put 1.05 to 1.33 MB of agent packs in their first
/// entries, which caused B0-33's sixth-pass leader change [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, fifth to seventh
/// passes], and entries of 84 to 96 agent commands and 450 KB inline kept Human and MergeQueue p99 at 1.15 to 1.49 s
/// [measured, fourth pass]. While placement fails or a bucket's probe is red, Human and MergeQueue pushes keep
/// INLINE_MAX (LOG_MAX_BYTES archives extra), and an agent pack over INLINE_MAX_HOT, hot group or not, is refused at
/// once, never queued: from apac 1 MiB packs commit at 1.3 to 2.0 MB/s against journey 9's 4.37 MB/s, and this rule
/// refused 2,500 of 2,500 at once and 0 of 287 Human and MergeQueue pushes [measured, B0-31, third, eighth passes]. On
/// a hot group INLINE_MAX_HOT stages a 256 KiB burst before the budget: at 64 KiB merges took 129 to 227 ms, at 256 KiB
/// up to 2.3 s [measured, B0-32, eighth pass]; `cost.py` prices a Class A write a push [inference].
pub const INLINE_MAX_HOT: usize = 64 << 10;                      // [measured, B0-32]; INLINE_MAX stays 1 MiB
pub const AGENT_INLINE_ENTRY: usize = 128 << 10;                 // [guess]; and AGENT_CMDS_ENTRY = 64 agent commands per entry, every group: 191 in one entry took 2.6 s (B0-32 m3fgt1) [guess]
pub const HOT_PUSHES: f32 = 2.0;                                 // [guess]
/// Per member, beside the 30-day rule. Above it the leader archives the oldest entries to `R2Key::Archive` in two
/// buckets before followers truncate them, so RPO stays zero. Check runs and statuses are kept 90 days (settable from 1
/// to 400) as GHES 3.22 does, and `PathSet` rows are deleted when their PR closes.
pub const LOG_MAX_BYTES: u64 = 2 << 30;                          // [guess]
/// Hot networks spread over k shards per warm site (`shards_for`). A warm site's first shard reports `NetworkLoad` each
/// minute it validates (stale past `LOAD_STALE`), and the leader commits one k for both warm sites from the larger load
/// as `RepoEvent::Fanout` (32.15), with no API call or cron, so the second warm site holds k shards when the first is
/// lost; it raises its own k for the predictor (AGENT-LAYER, "Overlaps and prediction"). So a site kill kept clone time
/// to first byte p99 at 0.71 to 1.10 s and human push p99 at 0.84 to 1.50 s [measured, M0-WP9, B0-34]. A step takes
/// idle warm pool shards (`HOT_POOL`) both warm sites' ledgers granted. `fanout` is pure; the leader keeps `Fanout`.
pub struct NetworkLoad { pub cpu_p99: f32 /* most loaded of the site's k, less derived work (A31) */, pub queue_depth: u32 }
pub fn fanout(loads: [&NetworkLoad; 2], k: u8, now: Timestamp, f: &mut Fanout) -> u8;   // at most FANOUT_MAX
pub const FANOUT_MAX: u8 = 8;  // up 3 min > 0.75 (or to ceil(k x cpu / 0.75)), down 1 h < 0.4 [guess]
/// Lazy test merges, one compute per (PR, base tip), run on a head push, on Actions dispatch for a `pull_request`-family event
/// (`demand`, which `ActionsTrigger` calls), and on a single-PR read, GraphQL or REST, by a writer or CI token with no current merge,
/// at most once per PR per 30 s. List fields never compute; the shard counts each PR a list read rendered without one.
/// `MergeBudget`, per network in the memory of the shard that runs its merges (the leader site's first, at the tier's instance size; its counts are stored, so they outlive the object's reset), refills at TEST_MERGE_BUDGET of its vCPU. Its merges run one at a time, each admitted only once the one before was charged its CPU, so pushes and dispatch, run from a positive balance, pass the budget by at most one merge; GETs leave half a second's. Admitted at an estimate while earlier merges ran, LM-1 round 5 spent 0.274 vCPU of 0.25 [measured, `tests/am1/results/merge-budget-2.md`]. A merge that waits more than TURN_WAIT_MS (10 s [guess]) in that line is shed without running, as one past the budget is: the line runs merges at their wall time, so it, not the CPU, bound B0-35, whose merges waited up to 265 s with 0 shed at 0.603 vCPU of 1, and whose `mergeable` read `UNKNOWN` to 98.3% of polls [measured, LM-1 round 16d, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md` on `lm1-memory`]. When the turn frees, a waiting dispatch merge goes before waiting head pushes and GETs, at most DISPATCH_STREAK_MAX (4 [guess]) in a row while another waits, so dispatch's asks do not wait out the whole line and pushes and GETs never starve; B0-35 m54's one line held up to 371 merges [measured, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`, "B0-35 rerun on graft-m4a after site day 54"; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`trigger-stream`"].
/// Past the budget or that wait `mergeable` is null, or GraphQL `UNKNOWN`, as GHES 3.22 says. A test merge on torvalds/linux costs 5 to 50 ms at p50 and at most 338 ms at p99
/// [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-34], so journey 9's head-push merges need 0.21 vCPU of a `standard-4`'s 1.
pub struct TestMerge { pub n: IssueNumber, pub base: Oid, pub head: Oid, pub result: MergeabilityResult } // `TestMerged`; never over the head's merge at the base tip
pub const TEST_MERGE_BUDGET: f32 = 0.25;
/// An App is `ActorKind::Agent` when it is one of Graftyard's GraftyardRun Apps (`graftyard-agent`, `graftyard-workspace`),
/// or when an admin flagged it as an `AgentApp` and set `confine`. A flagged App without `confine` is `Bot`, though its
/// commands take the Agent admission class and its PRs carry `AgentMeta`. `Graftyard MCP` and user tokens are never
/// `ActorKind::Agent`, so neither these refusals nor `AgentsExclusive` bind them. For an `Agent`, `decide` refuses an
/// APPROVE review, `Merge`, ready-for-review, ruleset and bypass edits or use, repository dispatch, workflow dispatch and
/// re-run, label writes outside the repository's allowlist, branch rename, release and tag creation, webhook and deploy-key
/// edits, commit statuses whose `context` and check runs whose `name` start with `graftyard/`, a ref update outside its
/// task's prefix (a third-party App's `confine` prefix), a push that would need a ruleset bypass even when the App is a
/// listed bypass actor, a Graftyard-run push with no live `AgentTask`, and a push with any `protected` hit. Workflow re-run
/// includes `rerequestCheckSuite`. When a push breaks more than one rule, the refusal names the first in this order:
/// host-owned ref, stale or missing object, tag creation, live task, prefix, ruleset bypass, protected hit, lease. Refusal
/// texts name the allowed prefix or the protected path. Actions runs for its pushes follow the repository's agent policy
/// and use fork-PR semantics (read-only token, no secrets) even after approval, until `AgentGate` holds for that exact
/// head. A push whose token carries a `task` claim lists the base repository's chain in `Push::deps()`, read `fresh`, so in
/// fork mode the fork group's `decide` checks the task's liveness, the base's `AgentPolicy`, and the base's leases there.
/// `protected` and `changed` then take their merge base against the base's default-branch tip.
pub enum ActorKind { User, Bot, Agent(AgentActor) }
/// `task` is the installation token's `task` claim. `requester` is the task's `requester`, never a value the controller
/// supplies. A third-party App's comes from the `External` task its session names.
pub struct AgentActor { pub app: AppId, pub task: Option<SessionId>, pub requester: Option<UserId> }
/// The task API's row (`/api/graftyard/v1/repos/{owner}/{repo}/tasks`). `app` is the controller's App for a
/// `GraftyardRun` task and the creating App for an `External` one, and `creator` is whoever sent `POST`. The host derives
/// `prefix` from `session` (`refs/heads/graftyard/<session>/`) and never reads it from the body. `snapshot` is the create
/// index. A token request's additive `graftyard_task` field must name a live `GraftyardRun` task. The requesting App must
/// be the task's App or the install's `graftyard-workspace`, and every repository the token covers must be the task's
/// repository or its workspace fork, so a token request with `graftyard_task` must name `repositories` [measured,
/// `tests/m0/results/m0-wp13.md`, L0-14]. The minted token carries the task as its `task` claim. A title or body edit by a non-writer
/// after the task's latest `Assigned` entry sets `waiting_for_user` and a host pause. While paused the task counts as
/// not live for pushes and minting, and its App may set only `waiting_for_user` or a final state. A writer's
/// reassignment to the controller's bot clears the pause, sets `queued`, and moves `snapshot` to that entry's index
/// [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp13.md`, L0-09].
/// An `External` task reports an `AgentApp`'s own work, names the session its App's leases and PRs use, carries the
/// `requester` that App names, and gets no prefix and no `task` claim, so it links pushes only by trailer and sends its
/// session on lease calls. Completed, Failed, TimedOut, Cancelled are final. Only `POST .../cancel` sets Cancelled, and
/// a task reads as Cancelled, the check that releases its leases, once the owner `Authz` read finds no row for its App
/// reaching the repository with `not_before` at or before the task's creation, the token rule. Only the host sets
/// TimedOut: a task with no update for TASK_IDLE reads timed out. In both cases the next command records the state,
/// answering 409. Its App sets the other states with `PATCH`. Cancelling or finishing a task releases its leases in the
/// same command. `state` is GitHub's agent task states. Core: `session`, `kind`, `state`, `prefix`, `requester`, `app`,
/// `installation`, and the create and last-update times. Metadata: the rest. `issue` and `pr` are in its repository.
pub struct AgentTask { pub session: SessionId, pub kind: TaskKind, pub state: TaskApiState, pub prefix: Option<RefPattern>,
                       pub requester: Option<UserId>, pub app: AppId, pub installation: InstallationId, pub creator: Actor,
                       pub issue: Option<IssueNumber>, pub snapshot: Index, pub prompt: Option<Box<str>> /* <= 64 KiB */,
                       pub pr: Option<IssueNumber>, pub summary: Box<str> }
pub enum TaskKind { GraftyardRun, External }
pub enum TaskApiState { Queued, InProgress, Completed, Failed, Idle, WaitingForUser, TimedOut, Cancelled }
/// A task's id. For a `GraftyardRun` task the host mints a lowercase hyphenated UUID at create, and `prefix` is
/// `refs/heads/graftyard/<uuid>/`, matched as the `RefPattern` `refs/heads/graftyard/<uuid>/*/**`, because `**` also
/// matches zero segments, so a ref needs at least one segment after the id. For an `External` task the creating App
/// names it: 1 to 64 bytes of `[a-z0-9._-]`, starting with a letter or digit, with no `..` and no `.lock` suffix,
/// unique among that App's tasks on the repository. Its string form in URLs, JSON, and `X-Graftyard-Agent-Session` is
/// the UUID, or `<app_id>.<name>`. An App may send its own bare `name`, which the host scopes to the calling App.
pub enum SessionId { Run(Uuid), External { app: AppId, name: Box<str> } }
/// `POST .../tasks` for a `GraftyardRun` task returns 422 while the org's `OrgPolicy.agents_enabled` is off, its default.
/// It needs a human user credential with `contents: write`, never an installation token, an `AgentApp`'s user token, or
/// a demoted or agent-session actor. A demoted actor is one past DEMOTE_PER_MIN at the create's own entry, and task
/// commands are not counted. The one exception is `graftyard-agent`, the controller's App, from an issue whose latest
/// `Assigned` entry names its bot, with that entry's actor as `requester`. Every other App creates `External` tasks
/// only. Creating or cancelling a `GraftyardRun` task sends the controller a first-party-only delivery on the event
/// pipeline to its webhook hostname, which no other App can subscribe to, so GHES clients never see it. It is the
/// `graftyard_task` event, signed with the `graftyard-agent` App's webhook secret (`design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "API
/// reference"). Other Apps' subscriptions to `graftyard_task` are ignored [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp13.md`, L0-14].
pub const TASKS_OPEN_PER_REQUESTER: u16 = 100;                  // open tasks per requester, or per App without one [guess]
pub const TASKS_OPEN_PER_REPO: u16 = 2_000;                     // [guess]
pub const TASK_IDLE: Duration = Duration::from_secs(86_400);    // [guess]
/// `Merge` of a PR with `AgentMeta` is refused unless `head` is the current head and `satisfied_by` is a writer who is
/// not the agent App, not the requester, and did not push to the PR's head ref while the PR was open. A PR whose
/// `AgentMeta.requester` is empty, as when a confined App opens it without `X-Graftyard-Agent-Session`, needs one more
/// such approval than `extra_human_approvals` asks, because the requester cannot be excluded. With `requester` empty,
/// every writer's approval qualifies, the requester's included, which is why one more is needed. Each qualifying
/// APPROVE writes a row; `Merge` counts distinct `satisfied_by` on the current head and needs
/// `1 + extra_human_approvals`, plus one when `requester` is empty [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp13.md`, L0-15]. A ref update of a PR's base
/// whose new tip contains the PR's head is a `Merge` of that PR for these rules, from any surface; `validate` reports
/// which open `AgentMeta` heads each new tip contains [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp13.md`, L0-03, L0-15]. Only an APPROVE review
/// sent with a credential that is neither an `AgentApp`'s nor MCP-issued writes it, and an `AgentApp`'s credential
/// includes its user-to-server tokens [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp13.md`, L0-14]. `token/scoped` for `Graftyard MCP` takes the additive
/// `graftyard_grant` and `graftyard_write` fields and stores the grant id and its `WriteScope` on the token's `Tokens`
/// row, and the host refuses approve and merge to a token whose scope lacks them. The commit status renders from the
/// row for display.
pub struct AgentGate { pub pr: PrNumber, pub head: Oid, pub satisfied_by: UserId }
/// Plus the install's harness template paths and the paths a repository's agent policy adds.
pub const PROTECTED_PATHS: &[&str] = &[".github/workflows/**", ".github/actions/**", ".gitmodules", "CODEOWNERS",
    ".github/CODEOWNERS", "docs/CODEOWNERS", "**/AGENTS.md", "**/CLAUDE.md", "**/GEMINI.md", ".github/agents/**",
    ".github/copilot-instructions.md", ".github/instructions/**", ".claude/**", ".cursor/**", ".cursorrules",
    ".codex/**", ".mcp.json", ".vscode/**"];
/// From `validate`, for each ref update by an `Agent` or under an agent prefix, the paths that the new tip's tree
/// changes against its merge base with the default branch or against the old tip (the union of both diffs), where they
/// match the protected list. This catches an existing outsider commit pushed under an agent ref.
pub struct ProtectedHits { pub refname: RefName, pub paths: SmallVec<[Box<str>; 4]> }
/// From the same tree diff, every changed path, or past PATHS_MAX their directories with `truncated` set. `Push`
/// carries `changed` beside `protected` and `provenance`, `decide` will match it against live Exclusive leases
/// (AM1-WP4), and `PathSet.paths` is copied from it. REST ref writes (`POST` and `PATCH /git/refs`, `POST /merges`) by
/// an `Agent` or under an agent prefix run `validate` on an empty staged pack, so they get all three outputs.
pub struct ChangedPaths { pub refname: RefName, pub paths: Vec<Box<str>> /* <= PATHS_MAX */, pub truncated: bool }
pub const PATHS_MAX: usize = 1_000;
/// From `validate`, the new commits' trailers and any `refs/notes/ai` update in the same push. A parse failure leaves
/// it empty and never fails the push. Trailers come from the last paragraph of each new commit's message, as `git
/// interpret-trailers --parse` reads them, with at most 32 per commit and 1,024 bytes per value, control and bidi
/// characters stripped, past which the rest is dropped and the push proceeds [guess, the M0-WP10 prototype's caps]. A
/// commit that fails `index-pack --strict` never reaches the parser. `AiNote` is recorded for each blob of the
/// `refs/notes/ai` tree diff whose fan-out path names a commit object id, and other entries are ignored. An `Agent`
/// update of `refs/notes/ai` is refused as outside its prefix, because one shared ref under a fleet's pushes would
/// conflict on every write; an agent commit's provenance is its `Session` link and trailers. `decide` records `Session`
/// only when the pusher is an App's installation token and the commit names, by `AgentMeta` or by a `Graftyard-Session`
/// value of exactly `https://<host>/<base owner>/<base repo>/_graftyard/sessions/<id>`, an `AgentTask` of that App,
/// live or final. Every other trailer stays `Claimed`, shown unlinked after https scheme and host checks [measured,
/// `tests/m0/results/m0-wp10.md`, B0-37]. `refname` is the ref the push moved. An agent commit with no `Session` link
/// still maps to its PR through that ref after the branch is deleted: in M0-WP16, 6,166 of 10,489 commits resolved only
/// this way [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp16.md`, L0-10].
pub struct Provenance { pub commit: Oid, pub refname: RefName, pub sources: SmallVec<[Source; 2]> }
pub enum Source { Claimed { key: TrailerKey, value: Box<str> }, AiNote { note: Oid }, Session(SessionId) }
/// Only GraftyardSession, ClaudeSession, AmpThreadId, and AssistedBy class a push. CoAuthoredBy is recorded, never classes.
pub enum TrailerKey { GraftyardSession, ClaudeSession, AmpThreadId, CoAuthoredBy, AssistedBy }
/// Host-owned. `decide` refuses every update under `refs/graftyard/` except a create or fast-forward of its own task's
/// session ref by `graftyard-workspace` with that task's `task` claim while the task is live, and deletion runs only
/// through a retention command. Receive-pack never advertises the ref, so a later update must name the current tip,
/// which `graftyard-workspace` reads by v2 `ls-refs` with a `ref-prefix`, raw or by `git fetch` with a refspec (git
/// 2.56's `ls-remote <pattern>` sends none [measured, am1-rest]); plain `git push` only creates it [measured, M0-WP10].
/// Session logs are always `PackRef::Stored`. Never in v0 or v1 advertisement, and listed by v2 `ls-refs` only when a
/// `ref-prefix` begins with `refs/graftyard/sessions/` and the ref with that `ref-prefix`; no other `refs/graftyard/`
/// ref is advertised or listed. A v2 `fetch` of a session log's object id is served to any reader, as for any object;
/// listing is the only gate [inference, as GitHub serves objects]. The vault, lifeboat, and `RestoredTo` carry it.
pub const SESSION_REFS: &str = "refs/graftyard/sessions/";
/// A claim by one holder on paths or refs of one repository: core-tier rows on its group, written by `LeaseAcquire`,
/// `LeaseRenew`, and `LeaseRelease`. Live while an entry's `at` is before `expires`, checked on read and in `decide`, so
/// expiry needs no command and compaction drops expired rows. `at` is the leader's clock and never decreases, so after a
/// leader change a lease can look live up to DRIFT longer, or lapse up to DRIFT sooner, which is advisory slack. A stall
/// extends leases only through the base's `LogEntry.extend` (`ExtendLeases`, Chains), never by idle time. A lease whose
/// holder fails the owner `Authz` read that `decide` already does counts as released. Uninstalling or suspending an App
/// fails that read for its leases and its tasks alike, so they read as released and cancelled with no sweep.
pub struct Lease { pub id: LeaseId /* ULID */, pub holder: LeaseHolder, pub scope: LeaseScope, pub mode: LeaseMode,
                   pub intent: Box<str> /* <= 280 bytes, untrusted, shown through the trust view */,
                   pub granted: Timestamp, pub expires: Timestamp }
/// `actor` keys SHARED's `Actor`: a user (a site admin as its user) or an App installation (a job's token as the
/// Actions App's). No other actor holds one. `app` is a user-to-server token's App. `session` is the task the holder
/// works for. An App token names one on acquire: its `task` claim, or a third-party App's own `External` task.
pub enum ActorId { User(UserId), App { app: AppId, installation: InstallationId } }
pub struct LeaseHolder { pub actor: ActorId, pub app: Option<AppId>, pub session: Option<SessionId> }
/// Gitignore-style: `*` matches within one path segment and `**` across segments. Unlike gitignore, every glob is
/// anchored at the root unless it starts with `**/`, and there is no negation. Two globs overlap when some path matches
/// both, found by walking both segment lists. A `RefPattern` uses the same syntax over full ref names.
pub struct PathGlob(Box<str>);   pub struct RefPattern(Box<str>);
pub struct LeaseScope { pub paths: SmallVec<[PathGlob; 8]> /* <= 32 globs */, pub refs: SmallVec<[RefPattern; 2]> }
/// An acquire conflicts only Exclusive against another holder's overlapping Exclusive. Shared leases never block; they
/// return as overlaps. An Exclusive lease needs a live `AgentTask` named by the holder's `session`, at most one per
/// task, and a literal first segment in every path glob, so `**`, `*`, and `*/...` fail. Past EXCLUSIVE_MAX after
/// `granted` it reads as Shared until it expires. Plan-block leases are Shared, held by an editor with push access,
/// count toward LEASES_PER_HOLDER and PLAN_LEASES_PER_REPO, and end once their draft is readied, closed, or merged.
pub enum LeaseMode { Shared, Exclusive }
pub const LEASE_TTL: Duration = Duration::from_secs(600);        // default; a holder may ask for 60 s to 3,600 s
pub const EXCLUSIVE_MAX: Duration = Duration::from_secs(4 * 3_600);
pub const LEASES_PER_HOLDER: u16 = 8;                           // live leases per holder per repository
pub const LEASES_PER_APP: u16 = 1_000;                          // live leases per App per repository [guess]
pub const PLAN_LEASES_PER_REPO: u16 = 500;                      // live plan-block leases per repository [guess]
pub const LEASES_PER_REPO: u16 = 4_000;                         // live leases per repository
/// `AgentPolicy.leases`. Advisory never refuses a push. AgentsExclusive refuses an `ActorKind::Agent` push whose `changed`
/// paths overlap another holder's live Exclusive lease, and names the lease and its holder. Another holder's lease is one
/// whose `session` differs from the pusher's task, or, for a pusher with no task, whose actor differs, so a vendor App
/// with only external tasks is never bound by its own leases. With `truncated` set, a changed directory overlaps any
/// glob under it. Exclusive globs are indexed by literal prefix, so the check fits in group commit. Humans, bots, `Graftyard MCP`, user tokens, and the merge queue are never refused by a lease.
pub enum LeaseEnforce { Advisory, AgentsExclusive }
// Agent-layer entries: variants added to SHARED's event enums, each with its `tier_of`. Core rows are what pushes,
// minting, and lease checks read (a merge also reads its pull row and writes `Merged`, both Metadata). `LeaseAcquire`
// sets `granted` to the entry's `at`, and `LeaseRenew` changes only `expires`.
pub enum AgentRepoEvent {   // flattened into `RepoEvent`
    Assigned { n: IssueNumber, assignees: SmallVec<[AccountId; 2]>, actor: Actor },          // Metadata
    IssueEdited { n: IssueNumber, actor: Actor, title: bool, body: bool },                  // Metadata
    TaskCreated(AgentTask),                                       // Core and Metadata, as PrOpened
    TaskUpdated { session: SessionId, state: Option<TaskApiState>, pr: Option<PrNumber>, summary: Option<Box<str>>,
                  actor: Actor },                                 // Core always; Metadata too for pr and summary
    AgentGate(AgentGate),                                         // Core, in the APPROVE review's entry
    AgentPolicy(AgentPolicy),                                     // Core
    PathSet(PathSet),                                             // Metadata, in the push's entry
    InsightVerdict(InsightVerdict),                               // Core; its check runs and labels are Metadata
    LeaseAcquire { lease: Lease, plan: Option<IssueNumber> },     // Core; `plan`: the plan block's draft
    LeaseRenew { id: LeaseId, expires: Timestamp },               // Core
    LeaseRelease { ids: SmallVec<[LeaseId; 4]>, by: Actor },      // Core
}
// `ExtendLeases` rides on the base's `LogEntry.extend`, not as an event, and moves Core lease rows. A `Refs` entry
// whose push carries `provenance` is Core and Metadata. `PrOpened` with `AgentMeta` keeps `AgentMeta.app` in the core
// `pull_head` row for `Merge`. `AuthzEvent` gains `AgentApp { app, row: Option<AgentApp> }` (`None` unflags), only on
// `Authz(Genesis.account)`. Every command whose actor is an App lists that chain in `deps()`, so `decide` reads the
// rows `fresh`, and admission uses the leader's cached copy. `AuthzEvent::OrgAgents` sets `agents_enabled: bool`
// (default false) and `agents_workspace: WorkspaceMode` (default `Fork`, into `<org>-agents`) apart from `Policy`; AM2
// adds `code_mode`. `IdentityEvent` gains `McpGrant { grant: McpGrant, revoked: bool }`. Both chains are core only.
pub enum WorkspaceMode { Fork, InRepo }

// ---- The read path ----
/// A fold with every entry acknowledged before `arrived`, built only by a leader whose term's `Noop` is applied: from
/// its fold while `now < lease_end - DRIFT`, else after a read-index round to the voters (renewing the lease), shared by
/// every read that arrived first; the next waits for the isolate's own clock, then an answer or READ_ROUND_GAP (20 ms
/// [guess]), empty to a voter with calls out under READ_RESEND (500 ms [guess]). Read-index answers wait for `Noop`.
/// `Fresh` reads touch SQLite after the lease check, isolate state only under a key read there (F65's dump): a replaced
/// instance cannot answer. Followers batch read-indexes per 10 ms, learners per 50 ms; a learner copies only its newest at HEDGE (500 ms
/// [guess]), and a later batch's answer grants the earlier ones (`read-newest`).
pub async fn fresh(&self, arrived: Instant) -> Result<Fresh<'_>, NotLeader>;
/// Stale mode starts only by a customer-signed `StaleMode` flag, committed in the `Release` group or, when it cannot
/// commit, verified uncommitted (Release group, `STALE_MAX`), never automatically. It serves reads and fetches at
/// `applied` for the flag's limit (default 4 h); writes return 503. Stale reads break read-your-writes across clients.
pub fn stale(&self) -> Result<Stale<'_>, StaleError>;
pub enum ReadView<'a> { Fresh(Fresh<'a>), Stale(Stale<'a>) } // held: at release, ≥ floor (`held-grant` [measured])

// ---- Release group (one per install, cell, and front account; placed on `Tri`) ----
/// Every entry is signed over `Bound` plus its body, so it verifies once, on one install, at one position. `prev` is
/// the digest of the last committed signed `ReleaseEntry`, and `Noop` and `Config` do not count. A pre-signed undo
/// binds `parent` instead of `prev`. The group accepts an undo only while its parent is the current intent, and only
/// when its bytes equal an entry of the parent's `undo` list, or of the `Resume` that replaced that list. Undo
/// signatures are checked against the `Keys` that were current at the parent intent's index. `decide`'s clock is its
/// entry's time. Entries after genesis are format 0x30.2. `FormatsEnable`'s release-signing half signs only the format. The group and `graft-ops` refuse any other
/// signature by a key absent from the latest committed `Keys` (genesis carries the first), a `Keys` whose `generation`
/// is not the latest plus one, and an intent past `not_after`. Signers. `Keys` takes the current customer key, and also
/// the new one when it changes. Intents take the vendor or customer intent key, except `ReplaceMember`, which only the
/// executor key signs, and `LeaveEvaluation`, which only the customer key signs. `Halt`, `Resume`, `Abort`, `SiteLost`,
/// `StaleMode`, `LifeboatAdd`, `LifeboatRemove`, `RepoRestore`, and `RepoReset` take the customer key, so the vendor
/// can sign no restore. `FormatsEnable` takes the release-signing key, which every mode registers, plus the customer
/// key. `SiteMode` takes an intent key or `graft-ops`'s drain key, which signs nothing else. The executor key, also
/// held by `graft-ops`, signs only `ReleaseUploaded`, `IntentStep`, `IntentDone`, `SiteRelease`, and `ReplaceMember`.
/// The agents executor key signs only a `ReleaseUploaded` that names `AgentsFront` and `Controller` alone, and the
/// `IntentStep` and `IntentDone` of a `ControllerRoll` or of a `Rotate` of an agents-account secret
/// (`design/AGENT-LAYER.md`). `LifeboatDecoder` is accepted only under the key its `LifeboatAdd` registered.
/// Self-hosted sets `vendor: None`. `decide` also refuses, and pages on: a `SiteDay`, a pointer, a `Rollback`, or an
/// `Abort` whose release's `ReleaseUploaded.reads` lacks an enabled format (SHARED rule 4 and the registry's
/// `rollbackBarrier`); an intent or undo naming a release with no `ReleaseUploaded`; a new intent while another is
/// current, except `ReplaceMember`, which halts the current one; a drain-key `SiteMode { Drain }` while another site is
/// drained; and an `EdgeRoll` that would drop a version that an undo of the current intent still names.
pub struct Bound { pub install: InstallId /* from genesis */, pub group: ChainId, pub prev: Option<Digest>,
                   pub parent: Option<IntentId> }
pub enum ReleaseEntry { Genesis(Genesis), Keys(Keys), Intent(SignedIntent), SiteMode(SiteMode),
                        ReleaseUploaded(ReleaseUploaded), IntentStep { intent: IntentId, step: u8 },
                        IntentDone { intent: IntentId }, SiteRelease { site: SiteId, release: ReleaseId },
                        Rollback { target: DayTarget, release: ReleaseId },           // undo only
                        EdgePointer { pin: ReleaseId }, OpsPointer { pin: ReleaseId },   // undo only
                        McpPointer { pin: ReleaseId }, ControllerPointer { pin: ReleaseId },   // undo only
                        SiteLost { site: SiteId }, StaleMode { scope: Scope, until: Timestamp },
                        LifeboatAdd { host: Hostname, key: Ed25519Public }, LifeboatRemove { host: Hostname },
                        LifeboatDecoder { host: Hostname, release: ReleaseId }, FormatsEnable { format: Format },
                        RepoRestore { repos: Vec<RepoId>, refs: Vec<RefName>, at: Timestamp, reason: Box<str> },
                        RepoReset { repo: RepoId, at: Timestamp, reason: Box<str> },
                        Halt { intent: IntentId },
                        Resume { intent: IntentId, undo: SmallVec<[(ReleaseEntry, Signature); 8]> },
                        Abort { intent: IntentId, target: ReleaseId } }
/// `graft-ops` turns a committed `RepoRestore` or `RepoReset` into a `RestoredTo` or `ResetTo` command on each named
/// repository, with `ChainId::Release` among its `deps`. Its `decide` checks that the `Release` entry names the
/// repository, and its request id is that entry's index, so a retry changes nothing. `to` is the last index with
/// `at <= t`. In managed, the cell's customer key is the operator's, so a restore is a support action.
/// `Halt` stops the current intent at its next step and keeps it current, so its undos stay valid. `Resume` restarts it
/// at its next step. A `Resume` of a vendor-signed intent after a `Keys { vendor: None }` must carry a non-empty `undo`
/// re-signed by the customer key, which replaces the parent's list. `Abort` ends the intent's forward steps and refuses
/// its undos. `graft-ops` then moves every site the intent touched, each on its own site day, and every script it
/// pinned or rolled, to `target`, recording `IntentStep`s under the aborted intent until `IntentDone`.
pub enum IntentState { Running { step: u8 }, Halted { step: u8 }, Undoing, Aborting { target: ReleaseId } }
/// The first entry an empty `Release` group accepts, and only when `install` and the customer signature match the trust
/// anchor: the `GRAFTYARD_INSTALL` and `GRAFTYARD_CUSTOMER_KEY` variables that Wrangler set on every script it created.
/// `graftyard genesis` builds it unsigned after the sites exist, and the customer signs it offline. `account` is the
/// install's own account, and genesis creates its `Authz(account)` chain for install-scope rows. A restore genesis names
/// its source and cutoff ("Install operations", Whole-install restore).
pub struct Genesis { pub install: InstallId, pub account: AccountId, pub keys: Keys, pub sites: SmallVec<[Site; 9]>,
                     pub placements: SmallVec<[(SystemChain, PlacementRecord); 4]> /* Release, Names, Tokens, Ids;
                                                                                     changed only by LeaveEvaluation */,
                     pub evaluation: bool /* the signed `evaluation` flag; only LeaveEvaluation clears it */,
                     pub restore: Option<RestoreFrom> }
pub struct RestoreFrom { pub source: RestoreSource, pub cutoff: Timestamp }
pub enum RestoreSource { Vault { account: AccountTag }, Lifeboat { host: Hostname } }
pub struct InstallId(Ulid);                                       // minted by `graftyard init`
pub struct ReleaseId([u8; 32]);                                   // SHA-256 of `release.json` (SHARED, registry)
pub struct VersionId(Uuid);                                       // the Workers version id an upload returns
/// One stored record format, as `LogEntry.format` names it: a record family and its version.
pub struct Format { pub family: u16, pub version: u16 }
pub struct FormatSet(SmallVec<[Format; 32]>);                     // sorted, no duplicates
pub struct ImageRef { pub name: Box<str>, pub digest: Digest }    // digest-pinned, in the account's registry
/// The chains a `StaleMode` flag covers.
pub enum Scope { Install, Owner(AccountId), Repo(RepoId) }
/// Committed by `graft-ops` under the executor key after it verified the release signature over `record` and uploaded
/// every script as a version without deploying it. The group checks the same signature under `Keys.release`.
/// Human-signed intents and undos name a `ReleaseId`, and `graft-ops` and `apply` resolve it to `VersionId`s from this
/// entry. `reads` comes from the bundle's formats file, whose SHA-256 the signed record lists. The agents account's
/// `graft-ops` commits its own `ReleaseUploaded` for `AgentsFront` and `Controller`.
pub struct ReleaseUploaded { pub release: ReleaseId, pub record: Bytes, pub record_sig: Signature,
                             pub versions: SmallVec<[(Script, VersionId); 16]>, pub images: SmallVec<[ImageRef; 4]>,
                             pub reads: FormatSet }
pub enum Script { Site(SiteId), Workflows, Front, Edge, Ops, Mcp, AgentsFront, Controller }
/// The whole key set; a `KeyId` is the 32-byte Ed25519 public key. `ring` holds the account-separated sites' keys.
/// `graftyard genesis` creates `drain` and `executor` in `graft-ops`'s Secrets Store, `graftyard agents init` creates
/// `agents_executor` in the agents account's, and a customer-signed `Keys` then adds it; every key rotates through `Keys`.
pub struct Keys { pub generation: u64, pub customer: KeyId, pub vendor: Option<KeyId>, pub release: KeyId,
                  pub drain: KeyId, pub executor: KeyId, pub agents_executor: Option<KeyId>,
                  pub ring: SmallVec<[(SiteId, Ed25519Public); 6]> }
/// A leader on a drained site checks its site's mode on every request and from a Durable Object alarm every 2 s [guess]
/// while its group served a request or committed an entry in the last 60 s [guess], and hands off within one lease. A
/// group idle longer is not woken, so B0-15's idle groups bill nothing; its leader hands off on its next request. With
/// request-only checks Milestone 0 measured groups moving at p50 14.6 to 20.8 s and up to 68 s after a drain, and with
/// the alarm at p50 5.4 to 6.3 s [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp4.md`, B0-07]. Members on a drained site keep voting
/// and storing. They campaign only when pre-vote finds no undrained voter that can lead. A drain-key `Drain` is ended
/// only by a drain-key `Normal`, which `graft-ops` appends at its site day's undrain step, or, for an automatic drain,
/// when both other sites' `SiteHealth` show the site under UNDRAIN_BURN for UNDRAIN_AFTER ("Install operations", Site
/// day). An intent-key `Drain` is ended only by an intent-key `Normal`.
pub struct SiteMode { pub site: SiteId, pub mode: Mode }
pub enum Mode { Drain, Normal }
pub struct IntentId(Ulid);
/// Executed by `graft-ops` only for a release with a `ReleaseUploaded`, and only before `not_after`. Each kind runs the
/// steps that ROLL_STEPS gives it ("Install operations", Site day). `graft-ops` commits `IntentStep` after each step
/// passes its gate, so a restarted `graft-ops` reruns only the first unrecorded step, and every step is idempotent. It
/// commits `SiteRelease` when a site serves one release at 100%, and `IntentDone` at the end. A release adds one
/// `SiteDay` per spare and residency site, gated by a canary group on that site that replays a corpus of current
/// formats. `ReplaceMember` and `RestorePlacement` run as Workflows ("Install operations", Site loss).
pub enum Intent { SiteDay { target: DayTarget, release: ReleaseId }, ContainerDay { site: SiteId, release: ReleaseId },
                  EdgeRoll { release: ReleaseId }, FrontRoll { release: ReleaseId }, OpsRoll { release: ReleaseId },
                  McpRoll { release: ReleaseId }, ControllerRoll { release: ReleaseId },
                  ReplaceMember { lost: SiteId, spare: SiteId /* on lost's continent */ },
                  RestorePlacement { site: SiteId }, Rotate { secret: SecretName, slot: u8 /* 0 or 1 */ },
                  LeaveEvaluation { sites: SmallVec<[Site; 6]>, placements: SmallVec<[(SystemChain, PlacementRecord); 4]>,
                                    default: PlacementRecord /* the `Tri` records */ } }
/// `Solo` to `Tri` on Cloudflare. Only the customer key signs it, and `decide` refuses it unless the flag is set. First
/// `graftyard sites add` creates the new sites on the current release, and a site day for the existing site and an
/// `EdgeRoll` bind them. `graft-ops`'s first step runs the latency check to each new site. The commit adds `sites` to
/// the `Release` view, swaps each system chain's record (old one in `moving_from`), and clears `evaluation`, so
/// `Placement::solo` refuses from then. `graft-ops` moves, one `IntentStep` each, a site per one-site `Joint`
/// [inference, BF-30]: `Release`, then `Ids`, `Names`, and `Tokens`. It then writes `default` as the install's default
/// record and over each owner record named `Solo`, one owner at a time [guess], which starts that owner's move Workflow
/// ("Sites and placement"). It commits `IntentDone` when every owner's `moving_from` is clear. The Workflows script
/// defines no member class, so its day skips drain and undrain.
pub enum DayTarget { Site(SiteId), Workflows, Containers(SiteId) /* undo of a `ContainerDay`: shards return to `git_prev` */ }
/// Each rotatable secret has two Secrets Store slots, both bound at install, so `Rotate` switches slots with no new
/// script version ("Install operations", Rotation runbook). The agents account's `graft-ops` holds `AgentsAppKey`,
/// `WorkspaceAppKey`, and its own `DeployToken`, and `graft-runners` holds `HostedRunnersAppKey`. Only `graft-ops`
/// reads `SecretsWrapKey`: it opens a job's secrets at `acquirejob` and unwraps hook secrets for `HookSequencer`.
/// `TunnelToken` is retired with Tunnel (2026-11-19) and kept only for its wire tag; nothing binds or rotates it.
pub enum SecretName { DeployToken, ReadToken, TunnelToken, MintWrapKey, SshHostKeys, McpAppKey, McpClientSecret,
                      InsightsAppKey, AgentsAppKey, WorkspaceAppKey, SecretsWrapKey, HostedRunnersAppKey }
/// `undo` is pre-signed with the intent: a `Rollback` per day target, and a pointer pin per pinned script it rolls.
/// Each undo signature is the intent's key over the undo bound to `parent: Some(id)`. `graft-ops` or an edge submits
/// one on a gate breach, unsigned by a human. A committed undo ends forward steps.
/// `graft-ops` runs a `Rollback` as drain, N at 100%, then undrain, deploys N at 100% for a pinned script when the API
/// answers, and commits `IntentDone`. A `Containers` rollback needs no API call; shards restart onto `git_prev`.
pub struct SignedIntent { pub id: IntentId, pub issued_at: Timestamp, pub not_after: Timestamp, pub intent: Intent,
                          pub undo: SmallVec<[(ReleaseEntry, Signature); 8]> }
/// Each pin as the fold holds it. `None` follows the script's deployment split, so a call carries no version
/// override. `Some(v)` pins every pinned call to `v`, which is the swap-back. Only a pre-signed undo sets `Some`, and
/// `apply` resolves its `ReleaseId` to the script's `VersionId` from that release's `ReleaseUploaded`. The next
/// committed intent that rolls the same script sets `None`. No forward pointer move exists, so no key signs one.
pub struct Pointers { pub edge: Option<VersionId>, pub ops: Option<VersionId>, pub mcp: Option<VersionId>,
                      pub controller: Option<VersionId> }
/// Each `graft-front`, edge, and site isolate, members included, caches the whole `Release` view (`Keys`, `Pointers`, site modes, `IntentState`, `SiteRelease`s, enabled formats, and flags) for POINTER_TTL. The view is a replica: the isolate replays the group's committed entries, read by position from any member's `/view`, through the same `check` and `apply` a follower runs, against its own trust anchor, and never moves to a lower index. A refresh reads members, the last-named leader first, until one that leads answers, because a follower applies a commit only when a later round carries it, and an idle group sends none: an undo read from followers reached isolates 59 s late [measured, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp2.md`, BF-43 run 1]. A busy isolate refreshes beside its requests once the view is POINTER_TTL - 1 s old, so a pin reaches every request within POINTER_TTL of its commit [measured, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp2.md`, BF-43]. A request waits, at most 1 s, only on a cold or idle isolate whose last refresh did not fail. A failed refresh keeps the last value and is retried after 1 s rather than a full POINTER_TTL [inference], and no request waits once one has failed or while one has hung for 4 s [measured, same, BF-8]. An isolate that starts while no member answers had no view and served the deployment split: in BF-8 run 2, 123 of 126 `graft-front` isolates started during a 10-minute blackhole served unpinned, while all 18 started before held the pin [measured, same]. So each member also writes the committed entries it applies, as the group signed them and with their times and positions, to a copy outside the group: the `Release` chain's own `MemberRegistry` object on the member's site (`release_copy`), which only grows. A refresh that requests wait for, on a cold isolate or one idle past POINTER_TTL, also reads every site's copy, for at most COPY_MS, and takes a copy only if every entry it lacks passes `check` against the isolate's own anchor, all or none, so an unsigned or forged copy is ignored. A copy never replaces a longer view, so an older copy cannot roll an isolate back, and the group's view replaces the copy once a member answers. The waiting requests go out once the group answers, or once every copy has answered, so they carry the longest copy: a follower of an idle group applies a commit only with the next round, so its site's copy can be one entry short, and in BF-8 run 3, with requests released on the first copy to answer and idle isolates reading no copy, 159 requests on isolates started during the blackhole and 4,059 on six idle isolates went out without a pin the leader's site held [measured, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp2.md`]. A cold isolate waits at most COPY_MS, an idle one at most 1 s, then serves what is held, unpinned if nothing came. In BF-8 run 4's 601 s blackhole, all 277 isolates took a site's copy in at most 139 ms and pinned 200,416 of 200,416 requests, 166,774 of them on the 240 isolates started during it [measured, same]. Every pinned call is a `fetch()`-based service binding, never RPC, because "Version overrides only apply
/// to `fetch()`-based service binding calls" and "A version override will only be applied if the specified version is
/// in the current deployment" (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/versions-and-deployments/version-overrides/).
/// The edge returns the version it ran from its version metadata binding (`x-graftyard-edge-version`, only on a request that carries `x-graftyard-front`, and `graft-front` takes it off the client's response), and `graft-front` logs an alarm and counts it on every pinned request answered by another version or none. The managed front reads each cell's view over signed HTTPS. An edge isolate takes the view's genesis from its deploy-time `GRAFTYARD_GENESIS_SIGNED` first, accepted against its anchor, until a wrong placement or a missing or refused variable sends it to the group ("Install operations", Genesis, step 6).
pub const POINTER_TTL: Duration = Duration::from_secs(5);         // compiled [guess]
/// How long a cold isolate's first requests wait for the sites' copies when no member answers: above an idle cross-region Durable Object round trip, 111 to 135 ms at p50 with p99 within 10 ms [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`, "Transport"], and above the copy reads of BF-8 run 4, 81 ms at p50 and 139 ms at most over 277 isolates [measured, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp2.md`]; half the 1 s a cold request already waited for the group [inference].
pub const COPY_MS: Duration = Duration::from_millis(500);      // compiled [inference]
/// Stale reads matter when groups cannot commit, and the `Release` group may be one of them, so members and isolates also accept a `StaleMode` that has not committed. The edge verifies it against its cached `Keys` (the customer key alone, this install, no parent, `until` ahead), and when `commit` has not answered in 10 s [guess] it writes the flag to every reachable `Release` member's `pending` row, outside the log, each member verifying it against its own applied `Keys`, and answers 202 with the members that kept it. Isolates read `pending` with the view and verify it again against their cached `Keys`. An uncommitted flag is honored only while `until` is in the future and at most STALE_MAX after its earliest arrival at any member. A covered member serves every read from `stale()`, on any member and with no lease or read-index round, its answer marked `"stale": {"until"}`, and answers every write `{"error": {"stale": {"until"}}}`, which the edge returns as 503 [measured, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp2.md`, BF-48].
pub const STALE_MAX: Duration = Duration::from_secs(24 * 3_600);  // compiled [guess]
/// `POST /api/graftyard/v1/release/entries` takes one signed entry, encoded as stored (`Bound`, body, signatures), with
/// no token: the signatures are the credential. The edge checks them against its cached `Keys` before `commit`, under a
/// WAF rate rule: decoding, the bound install and group, and each signature against a signer its view holds, refreshed once more before an unknown signer is refused; `decide` runs every other rule. 201 returns `{"index": n, "digest": "<hex>"}` after `Acked`. 409 returns `{"message": "stale prev",
/// "prev": "<hex>", "index": n}` when `prev` is not the current digest, a second genesis included, with `prev` the held
/// genesis's digest, so a retried install after a lost 201 sees its own, and the signer re-signs. 422 returns
/// `{"message": "...", "refusal": "<variant>"}` when `decide` refuses. Signed entries land hours apart, so offline
/// signers rarely meet a 409 [inference]. Reads are `GET` on the same path (`design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "API reference").
/// One per (site, shard) on each site script, 64 shards by chain hash [guess]. A voter writes its row before its first
/// entry and on each config change. A placement try that does not go in is put back to the kept member, by a put that
/// replaces only a row naming a try. `ReplaceMember { lost }` and the owner-placement move read the surviving or old
/// sites' registries for rows whose `sites` include the site; all but `Solo` have a voter off a lost site. The edge
/// reads a `do_name` here when the names it computes miss (Shape, "Sites and placement").
pub struct MemberRegistry { pub site: SiteId, pub shard: u8 }
pub struct RegistryRow { pub chain: ChainId, pub do_name: Box<str>, pub generation: u32,
                         pub sites: SmallVec<[SiteId; 3]> }

// ---- Packs ----
pub struct PackId(Ulid);                                          // fresh for every placement
pub enum PackRef { Inline(Bytes) /* <= 1 MiB, or INLINE_MAX_HOT on a hot group; lives in the entry */,
                   Stored { id: PackId, checksum: [u8; 32], bytes: u64, sites: SiteSet } }
/// Built only by `PackStore::place`: the bytes are in the buckets of two sites in two regions, each put with
/// If-None-Match: *, and a container's `index-pack --strict` passed on the same bytes. `Solo` places one bucket. Each
/// object's R2 custom metadata names its chain and `RequestId`. `decide` refuses a stored pack whose `PackId` time is
/// older than PACK_COMMIT_WINDOW, so a pack that has not committed by then never will.
pub struct DurablePack { pack: PackRef, _sealed: () }
pub const PACK_COMMIT_WINDOW: Duration = Duration::from_secs(3600);   // [guess]
/// Archives and snapshots are keyed by range and content digest, and each is put in two buckets. The leader commits
/// `Archived` after both puts, and followers truncate only through a committed range whose key they can HEAD in two
/// buckets, so a deposed leader's put never stands in for a range nobody holds. A member that HEADs a committed key in
/// fewer than two buckets and holds the range's entries re-puts the same bytes create-only, checks the digest, and
/// HEADs again, and an alarm fires while any committed key has one copy; otherwise one lost copy stops truncation for
/// good [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp8.md`, negative test]. A snapshot's `from` is 0. An `Archive` is `gyfarch\0`
/// v1, then each stored row in the raft codec behind a 4-byte length (`crates/fold`). A `Blob` is placed like a stored
/// pack: create-only puts in two sites' buckets in two regions before its entry commits (`DurableBlob`, SHARED "Issues
/// and pull requests"). An `ArchiveCache` object is derived, lives only in the warm site's bucket beside `CloneCache`
/// entries, is named by no entry, and is rebuilt by `git archive` on a miss.
pub enum R2Key { Pack { network: NetworkId, id: PackId }, Idx { network: NetworkId, id: PackId },
                 InlineIdx { chain: ChainId, index: Index } /* `.idx` of an inline pack on a hot network */,
                 Snapshot { chain: ChainId, tier: Tier, from: Index, to: Index, digest: Digest },
                 Archive { chain: ChainId, from: Index, to: Index, digest: Digest },
                 CloneCache { key: CloneKey, replica: u8 }, Export { minute: u64, seq: u32 }, ExportState,
                 Compose { network: NetworkId, file: String } /* derived: a composed-miss chain's `head`, base, or part */,
                 Blob { repo: RepoId, oid: Digest } /* SHA-256 of the bytes: LFS objects and release assets */,
                 ArchiveCache { network: NetworkId, commit: Oid, prefix: Digest, format: ArchiveFormat } /* derived */,
                 Committed { of: Box<R2Key> } /* marker for a committed `Pack` or `Blob`: (chain, index, checksum, bytes) */,
                 Upload { network: NetworkId, id: PackId } /* journal of an in-flight multipart upload's id */ }
/// The marker's checksum and size, also in its metadata, let the filler verify a copy without the chain: it streams the copy bucket to bucket, and the binding
/// checks both, since 16 copies read whole killed every cron run [measured, `tests/am1/results/r22-sweep-fill.md`]. The binding cannot list open multipart uploads, so the edge journals each under `Upload` first (`tests/m1/results/m1-wp4.md`).
/// `from_at` and `to_at` let `RepoRestore` find the last index with `at <= t` inside an archived range from the fold
/// alone (M0-WP8 found the day-0 index truncated on every member by day 30).
pub struct Archived { pub key: R2Key, pub from: Index, pub to: Index, pub from_at: Timestamp, pub to_at: Timestamp,
                      pub digest: Digest }
/// After `Acked`, the leader puts one create-only `Committed` marker per stored pack and `Blob` its entry names, in
/// the same two buckets. `Promoted` re-puts the markers of its range first, so markers are complete below each chain's
/// last promotion. The orphan sweeper and the third-bucket filler list markers by network or repository, never chains.
/// The sweeper deletes a pack or blob older than 24 h only when it has no marker and the chain named in its custom
/// metadata has no `request` row for its `RequestId` naming its `PackId` (a retry's row names the retry's pack, not the
/// first placement's orphan). When one exists, it writes the missing marker. An object older than REQUEST_RETENTION
/// with neither is kept and alarmed [measured, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp4.md`, BF-19, `request` table stubbed]. A chain
/// whose group is gone (every member reached answers not joined) gets no marker or fill: a purge that missed one bucket
/// had its packs copied back into the others [measured, `retire-sticks`]. Each site sweeps the networks it homes, and keeps each network's home (`RepoOwner`, then `Placement`, on `Names`) in its cursor object for HOME_FOR_MS (24 h [guess]), reading at most HOMES_PER_RUN (64 [guess]) new or day-old homes a run: read every 15-minute run, the two `Names` reads per network woke idle `Names` members, which wrote about 2.6 k rows a run, four runs an hour, on an idle `graft-m2b` [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, "Idle Durable Object rows" and `sweep-quiet`]. A run asks a chain whose `request` lookup failed nothing more that run, so its other packs are unavailable (kept and alarmed) and a group no member answers costs the lookups in flight when the first fails, one LOOKUP_FOR of wall time, not one per pack, and it starts no network and asks no lookup once SWEEP_WALL (8 minutes [guess]) of the Cron Trigger's 15 [doc, `research/cloudflare.md`] have passed, then stores its cursor, leaving the network in flight and the fold audit their time: a run cancelled at 15 minutes stores nothing, so every later run died at the same network, which on `graft-m2b` site a was retired test group `repo:131322`, whose packs cost LOOKUP_FOR each, 16 at a time [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, "Site a's sweep wall"].
pub fn sweep(network: NetworkId) -> Result<SweepReport, SweepError>;
/// Before archiving a range, the home shard packs the objects its packs brought (no `--fix-thin` bases), named by tree
/// entry, into one self-contained `DurablePack` [measured, `lm1-memory` 23b]; the leader commits `Promoted`, then
/// `Archived`. A drain run starts past LOG_MAX_BYTES less 1/16 and archives to the cap less 1/8, so a minute's sum
/// wait at 2x B0-30's pushes stays under the cap [inference, `b030-split.md`, "Third drain fix"]. It promotes ahead
/// first past LOG_MAX_BYTES / 32 above the last `Promoted` [guess] until a run passes its trigger, warm (no stopped
/// shard starts, `lm1-memory` 28); a promotion takes 8 ranges, an `Archived` 8 objects [inference]. Streamed unlocked
/// in parts to buckets and a validate that catches up first; PROMOTE_MAX batches, an entry over it alone [measured,
/// `lm1-memory` 20]. `Compacted.consumed` names promoted packs; restore checks inline ones by digest chain. A failed
/// promotion's range retries after 1 minute, doubling to 8 [guess], warm (no stopped shard starts) unless 6 h [guess]
/// passed since a try that could start one; the member keeps that state across isolates. Retried at every alarm, one
/// that could never pass started three containers a try [measured, `retire-sticks`].
pub struct Promoted { pub from: Index, pub to: Index, pub pack: PackRef /* always Stored */ }
/// A shard rehydrates a network from every stored pack with a marker, minus those a `Compacted` consumed, plus the packs
/// each chain names after its last `Promoted`, a page at a time with the next read meanwhile (two pages held), saving
/// its place per page with its disk id and loaded promotions, so a cut-off run resumes (B0-30, 9 to 11). `--fix-thin`,
/// promotions first, a midx per 50 ingests [measured, `hot-shards-2`]; inline packs one `index-pack` per 4 MB run
/// [measured, `rehydrate-batch`]. A promoted or rolled pack has its `.idx` beside it (`R2Key::Idx`, create-only,
/// before its entry), loaded in place of `index-pack` once it checks against the trailer and kept (`.keep`). Pushed
/// packs in a range a committed `Promoted` or `Compacted` and its `.idx` both cover are not loaded;
/// then every ref's history and tip tree must be on disk, checked before the repack [measured, `lm1-memory` 21], else
/// the skipped packs load and it checks again; a disk failing both never serves. Stored packs and `.idx` stream four at
/// once [`fetchmem`], in log order. Thin: G5. Repack rolls unkept packs at nice 19 on 2 threads, a midx over all; kept
/// and named `--keep-pack` (its hint walk ignores a `.keep`): 104 CPU-s not 588, 1.7 GB not 2.2 [measured, `lm1-memory` 25, 27].
pub async fn rehydrate(shard: &GitCacheShard, network: NetworkId) -> Result<IngestCursor, GitError>;
/// One follower writes each tier's snapshot: the Full follower at position `to / SNAPSHOT_EVERY` modulo the number of
/// Full followers, in config order, and for `Solo` the member itself. It snapshots once SNAPSHOT_EVERY entries or
/// SNAPSHOT_BYTES of log have passed since the tier's last snapshot, and at least once on each day the group commits.
/// It snapshots at one index, `to`, without pausing `apply`. Every fold row carries the index that last wrote it. While
/// a snapshot runs, `apply` copies a row's prior value into `snap_undo` the first time it changes or deletes a row last
/// written at or below `to`. The writer reads rows in primary-key order, in alarm-driven batches that each fit one
/// request's limits, emits the `snap_undo` copy where one exists, and skips rows written after `to`. It uploads the
/// frames as multipart parts to its own site's bucket and one other and sends the key to the leader. The leader HEADs
/// both and commits `Archived` with `R2Key::Snapshot { from: 0, to }`, and the writer then drops `snap_undo`.
pub enum SnapshotFrame { Rows { table: Box<str>, rows: Vec<Row> }, End(SnapshotTrailer) }
pub struct SnapshotTrailer { pub chain: ChainId, pub tier: Tier, pub to: Index, pub term: Term,
                             pub entry: Digest /* entry `to`'s, so a restore refuses a wrong `prev` on `to + 1` */,
                             pub fold_digest: Digest, pub release: ReleaseId, pub format: Format, pub rows: u64 }
pub const SNAPSHOT_EVERY: u64 = 100_000;                         // entries [guess]
pub const SNAPSHOT_BYTES: u64 = 256 << 20;                       // [guess]
/// Leader to a member whose next entry it has archived, in an AppendEntries round: each tier's newest committed snapshot,
/// the archives from there (or from REQUEST_RETENTION before `through`, so `request` rows are rebuilt) to its first log
/// entry, and `through`'s term, digest, config, and id block. The member applies them, acks nothing until it has applied
/// `through`, and follows; its metadata tier loads lazily. `ReplaceMember` members and new learners join the same way.
pub struct InstallSnapshot { pub term: Term, pub core: R2Key, pub metadata: Option<R2Key>, pub archives: SmallVec<[R2Key; 8]>,
                             pub through: (Index, Term, Digest), pub config: (Index, GroupConfig), pub id_block: Option<IdBlock> }
/// GC roots are each member chain's fold refs plus its `ref_log` fold table, which keeps each ref value until
/// RESTORE_WINDOW after the entry that replaced it, and records per ref the newest index it has pruned, so `RestoredTo`
/// refuses rather than read a pruned value as an absent ref [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp8.md`, `wp8-win-r1`]. Cruft expires 14 days after an object leaves that set, and `RestoredTo` reads `ref_log`. Each tier
/// keeps at least one snapshot at or below every index inside the window. The vault deletes a pack no sooner than
/// RESTORE_WINDOW after the install's GC deletes it.
pub const RESTORE_WINDOW: Duration = Duration::from_secs(35 * 86_400);
/// Same response bytes for the same key: protocol v2 fetch with no haves is deterministic for a fixed ref state. A composed miss's entry is a manifest line naming the chain's R2 packs and counts, then its tail; the edge hashes the trailer (`edge-compose`, built, not deployed).
pub struct CloneKey { pub network: NetworkId, pub wants: Digest, pub depth: Option<u32>, pub filter: Option<FilterSpec>,
                      pub caps: CapSet, pub tags: Option<Digest> /* refs/tags/ digest, with include-tag (M1-WP6) */ }

// ---- Git cache shards (derived) ----
/// One container-backed Durable Object per (site, shard), under the `durable_object` scheduling policy, because a new
/// account cannot create an application under `default` [measured, B0-16]. Networks map to shards by rendezvous hashing
/// of (network, shard) within a site. Each shard keeps an LRU of bare repositories with reftable on its ephemeral disk,
/// rebuilt by `rehydrate` (Packs), and holds nothing canonical. Each network is warm on its group's home site and the
/// next of its group's sites by rendezvous, never a site outside the group (`chain-record`), on one shard per warm site, or on k shards when its git CPU passes one. The first of the k
/// is its rendezvous shard among `count`, and the others come from the site's hot pool by rendezvous, so `count` never
/// changes and no other network moves. A push validates on the shard of the k that rendezvous on its pack's trailer
/// picks (else the first), on its disk as it stands (journey 1); a human push's buffered pack that one site's shard has not answered in `VALIDATE_HEDGE` (1 s [inference, from measured validate times]) also goes to every site left at once, the first answer decides, and the last site keeps its whole wait (`validate-hedge`). Shards stream bodies on unread; the URL names the
/// SHA-256 of the body's head and buffered pack, and a body that does not match is a 503, never the pack's 422 (G35).
/// They validate concurrently, 16 at most; an agent's waits 2 s, then `agent admission full` (Scale). Inline `.idx` go to `R2Key::InlineIdx` (thin packs; `rehydrate`, G5). Upload-pack and predictions spread over all k; merges run on the leader's shard (BM-2).
/// `AdmitClass` rides into each shard's work queue, so Human and MergeQueue validates and merges run first, and agent
/// test merges, predictions, and clone misses are shed first. A hot network's clone-cache entries are written to both
/// warm sites' buckets. A depth-1 miss with one want, no haves or filter, SHA-1, and `ofs-delta` is composed: a base of
/// the tip's trees and blobs, a part per tip move of its objects earlier packs lack, and a pack of the want's own new
/// objects, under one pack header and trailer. No object is in two packs and no delta leaves its pack, so `index-pack`
/// takes it. At nice 19 the home's first shard builds the base at warm at HEAD (the default branch, `refs/heads/main` with no `Settings` row, never the first ref), a part per move after its catch-up, and a fold
/// every 20, and writes the warm buckets' `compose/<net>/base-<tip>`, `part-<from>-<to>`, and `head`, which others
/// follow. A want at a ref's tip or behind HEAD composes from the shard's chain, lagging or not, plus its diff from the
/// chain tip, with no lock or catch-up first. A step whose chain tree is past 20,000 changed entries from the new tip builds a base, not a part. A want the disk lacks yet (a just-moved tip), or past 20,000 entries from the chain, starts the step and is composed again after the running catch-up, then after one that starts later (`compose_then_catch_up`); `include-tag` on an annotated tag, no chain, or a want still refused goes to stock. All of one upload-pack's waits draw on one deadline, the caller's `wait`, past which the object answers 503 `warming` and the edge asks the next site. A forwarded miss waits at most 2 s [guess] for its chain step and the last site all its deadline, but only on a site whose bucket holds the chain (a warm site); any other site waits for none and, last, asks its container at once (`chain-site`). Every shard on a chain site installs the chain after its own catch-ups, not only when a miss asks, so the chain site that takes the misses when the home shard goes already holds one (`chain-install`). A container runs at most `STOCK_MAX` stock fetches (2 [guess]: linux's took 2.6 GB each) and answers 429 at once past it; the object queues the rest in arrival order within that deadline, since git retries neither 429 nor 503 by default [doc, git-config, `http.maxRetries`] and sets no read timeout unless `http.lowSpeedTime` is. Each write of an answer gets 30 s [guess] and a stock answer holds the network's lock at most 10 s [guess], then streams on unlocked, so a caller that stops or reads slowly holds nothing (B0-34 mt3: two stalled stock reads held it 15 minutes; mt5: whole clones held it to 119 s). Under `lock-split` the additive writers (ingest, adopt, `/refs`, the midx) exclude one another but not validates or readers, which take the lock shared; a validate reads its refs from one snapshot, adopt claims its aside by rename, and only evict, a fresh init and a `/refs` file/directory rename take it alone (`TestValidateSerialEquivalence`). Direct pushes take no merge, so parts follow catch-ups;
/// `refs/pull/N/merge` stays as PrSync builds it. The shard writes each stock miss's whole answer to the clone cache, read or not, from its spool, which crosses the container twice (`put_spooled`). Under `edge-compose` (built, not deployed), a composed miss leaves no copy in a container: the edge streams the chain's base and parts from R2 (free egress), with the shard's tail (`compose=tail`, the want's own objects and the object count) last, writing the pack header and hashing the trailer itself, and the shard fills the entry as a manifest. The edge streams a hit from R2. Before the first byte it checks each chain pack with a 12-byte ranged read, four at a time, and holds no body; checks that do not end within `CHAIN_CHECK_WITHIN` (3 s [guess]) answer 503 with `Retry-After` before any git byte. It then opens one pack's body at a time, on the etag its check read, after the one before it is sent, since one invocation holding eight R2 bodies gets no ninth `get` [measured]; a pack changed since its check, or a read stalled past `CHAIN_STALL`, ends the answer short (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, `chain-hold`). The checks run in both warm buckets, home first, and the answer streams from the first that holds every pack, since a fold's step that stops between buckets leaves the other warm bucket's shards naming a chain the home bucket deleted (`chain-warm`). A hit's manifest is read and its chain checked while the read gate reads, and nothing is sent until the gate allows; only the manifest's line enters the isolate, and the tail streams from the entry's body or the shard's answer, since read whole an 8 MiB tail held 16.8 MB [measured, edgebed] (`hit-gate`). A checked header is not immutable: chain files are named by commits (`base-<tip>`, `part-<from>-<to>`), not by their bytes, and a fold deletes the old base's files, so a header kept by its key alone would stream an old manifest's deleted pack and cut the answer after bytes left, where today's check declines it to the shard. Under `colo-gate` the edge reads the bucket's `head` beside the manifest and keeps that head's checked headers (key, count, etag, length) in the isolate and the Cache API under the bucket and the head object's R2 `version` (unique to each upload [doc]; an etag can repeat when the same bytes are put again), which every put of `head` changes, and only a set whose every check passed; the builder puts every file a head names before that head and deletes an old base's files only after the head that replaces it, so every file of the head an edge read was there when it read it; a pack the head names comes from the set, any other is checked as before, and each body still streams on its etag, so a fold after the head read cuts the answer as it does today. That is one `head` get and one match where 13 packs' checks took p50 374 / p99 1,399 ms [measured, `tests/am1/results/b034-colo.md`]; a set is rebuilt once per data center per new head. Under `head-etags` (built, not yet measured hosted) the builder writes beside the chain in each bucket's `head` each pack's etag, size, and header count as that bucket held it after the puts (one 12-byte read per new pack; a pack this step did not put keeps the old head's record), under `HEAD_MAX` (2 KB) or not at all, and `ready_chain` compares and installs the chain alone, so the records never make a step; an edge whose set lacks a pack the head names takes its header from the head's record at the manifest's count and checks none, and still streams on that etag, so a pack replaced after the head cuts the answer as before, since each new version rebuilt its set from 11 to 20 checks at `chain_open` p99 up to 1,209 ms on B0-34 mt15 [measured, `tests/am1/results/b034-tail.md`]. On torvalds/linux a composed miss's pack began at 0.02 s, stock's at 5 s
/// after 30 CPU-s [measured, `tests/m0/results/composed-miss.md`]. A shard object starts its container with
/// `ctx.container.start()`, naming its image, the tier's instance size, and `enableInternet: false`, so the container
/// reaches R2 and members only through its object. It starts one only while its index is under `count + HOT_POOL`. With
/// no `max_instances` in the policy, this check over a fixed set of shard names is each site's start cap. At start the
/// object reads `CLOUDFLARE_REGION` in the container and replaces one outside its site's regions, at most three times,
/// then alarms. A warm shard holds a 6 h inactivity timeout, the API's maximum, renewed hourly by its alarm until 24 h
/// after its last request, and set by any object instance that finds it running [measured, `hot-shards`]. Each
/// site-script version's image map holds its release's git image as `git` and the prior one's as `git_prev`. A new
/// version restarts no running container [measured, `container-survival.json`], so a shard changes image only when its
/// site's `ContainerDay` step covers it, and returns to `git_prev` on that day's committed `Rollback { target:
/// Containers(site) }`. Per site, `count + HOT_POOL` lets any two sites carry every network warm. A shard's maintenance (the geometric repack, its commit-graph write, a roll-up) yields to moved load: a client push or fetch reaching a shard on a site other than the record's home as the edge's first try (`wait` = `FIRST_TRY_WAIT_S`, 15 s; the edge tries the home first unless the home failed it within `COLD_SHARD_MS`, and a hedge's try waits 60) holds its container `YIELD_HOLD` (60 s [guess]), in which it starts none and stops a running one by SIGTERM to its process group, freeing the repack slot and the midx lock; client CPU p99 above `FANOUT_UP` also starts none. git writes temporary files and renames, so any point is safe [measured: 100 of 100 random stops of a 12 s repack and commit-graph left the repository whole, `tests/maintyield/stop.sh`]. A stopped run starts over; a held roll-up answers 503 and moves on. Held `MAINT_DEFER_MAX` (10 min [guess]), the next runs unstopped.
pub struct GitCacheShard { pub site: SiteId, pub shard: u16 }
pub fn shards_for(network: NetworkId, site: SiteId, count: u16, pool: &[u16]) -> ArrayVec<GitCacheShard, 8>;
pub const SHARDS: [u16; 3] = [2, 5, 25];   // at 50, 300, 2,000 developers: `cost.py` from a [guess] working set
/// Per site, the expected hot networks [guess 1] times FANOUT_MAX - 1, counted in the site's start cap, so it uses the
/// tier's instance size: `standard-2` at 50 developers, `standard-3` at 300, and `standard-4` at 2,000. A `Fanout`
/// step up takes 1 plus the fewer pool shards both warm sites' `PoolLedger`s (first pool shard's SQLite) granted, each
/// by `pool_grants`, holding each owner to its share and keeping held shards, so another owner never takes them; a
/// ledger silent 3 s [guess] keeps k. `shards_for` serves each network its own leased slots, never shared (by
/// rendezvous all shared the same k - 1). Unrenewed past LOAD_STALE, a lease lapses. No API call, no `decide` read.
/// With no plan field, an owner's share is HOT_POOL - 1 [guess, B0-34's monorepo share]. Above `FANOUT_UP` a leader
/// reserves a slot past its k - 1 (`pool_reserve`), pool-warms each held slot (`Caller::PoolWarm` starts a stopped
/// shard) and steps only to warm slots (`warm_step`); cold, a step waited 290 s [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`,
/// B0-34, `b0-34-r8`]. A slot is warm once it holds a cursor for its disk and no rehydrate runs, whatever its catch-up takes (`catchup_answer`): its pool warm starts a catch-up and waits for none [measured: as "caught up within 2 s", 34 of 59 warms of a rehydrated slot answered cold, B0-34 m41, `tests/am1/results/b034-trace.md`]. A cold slot holds the high line. A ledger beat tears down given-up slots. `cost.py` prices it.
pub const HOT_POOL: u16 = 7;
/// Per (shard, network), in the shard's SQLite, derived: the index through which it applied each member chain's `Refs`,
/// `Merged`, and `Promoted` entries and `Compacted` ones, saved per log page at the page's last index,
/// never the range's end (B0-30 blocker 12). A shard behind reads pages, two at most in memory, and `ranges`, from
/// its site's member first, before it serves (`catchup-local`); a pack missing a base drops the cursor. Another disk id, or a cursor below an
/// `Archived` truncation, means `rehydrate` (BF-18). Catch-ups run in the object instance, which reads its member by
/// the committed config, with no lookup mark: a page from its committed log only when it starts at the index asked for, and `ranges` by read-index, which a follower answers at or above the commit index the leader confirmed; no member here, not joined, behind, a miss or any other answer goes to the leader by the lookup rule. Through the lookup, a catch-up of one entry with no packs waited 19 s behind 13 other requests' walks of its chain in the shard isolate and set B0-35 m59's dispatch tail [measured, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`, "Deeper analysis of m59"; that the wait was the mark's is an inference].
