A catch-up runs in pieces of at most `PIECE` (32 stored or inline packs, or 8 MiB, whichever binds first, and always at least one whole entry [inference: about 1.5 s at B0-32 m3fst1's 45 to 54 ms and 0.25 MB a pack, `tests/fanout/raw/shardev-m3fst1-b0-32.jsonl.xz`]): each piece saves the cursor, wakes every caller waiting on the shard, and the next starts at once toward the head the run's first piece read, so a run still ends at that head. A held validate is asked again on the disk as it stands after each piece, and is held again while its base is still missing; a merge that missed a commit merges again after each piece. Only a run that started after the caller arrived still settles it, and every failure after that run is the pack's or the merge's. Run whole, one catch-up of a burst's staged packs (294 packs, 74.5 MB, 15.8 s) held 53 first-shard validates to the run's end and a merge 14,421 ms, and answered another `warming` at 15 s [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "B0-32 status after m3fst1"] (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`catchup-pieces`"). A caller waits its `wait`, then `warming`; a rehydrating shard says so to a caller with another next. A read that names only commits (a test merge's base and head, a tree or blob at a commit) answers from the disk with no catch-up once the object holds a cursor for its running container, and waits as above only when an object it needs is missing: a commit's answer never changes, so a catch-up cannot change it (`disk-first-reads`, the rule `disk-validate` set for pushes). Pushes, fetches and the
```rust
/// archive's (stateless) promotion try own site, then other Full sites; no shard checked is 503 [measured, `hot-shards`]. `head`: the `settings` row's `default_branch`, which HEAD names, as the edge's ls-refs does, set when it changes (G26).
pub struct IngestCursor { pub network: NetworkId, pub chains: SmallVec<[(ChainId, Index); 4]>, pub head: Option<RefName> }
impl GitCacheShard {
    /// The new commits for `Provenance` are those reachable from the new tips and not from the updated refs' old tips or
    /// the default branch's tip; connectivity still uses the full ref set (`--not --all`, 3 s at 5,000 refs, m0-wp10; a
    /// commit-graph each delta-searching repack keeps it flat, `hot-shards-4`) [measured]. Only `rehydrate` reads all refs.
    pub async fn validate(&self, staged: StagedPack, refs: &CoreRefs)
        -> Result<(PackIndex, Provenance, SmallVec<[ProtectedHits; 1]>), PackError>;
    pub async fn upload_pack(&self, req: FetchRequest, refs: &CoreRefs) -> Result<PackStream, GitError>;
    pub async fn merge(&self, req: MergeRequest) -> Result<MergePlan, MergeError>;   // leader site's shard; tree-diff check
}

// ---- SSH front ----
/// Off by default per install. A Spectrum TCP app on port 22 with a Worker origin (`traffic_type: "worker"`), the edge,
/// whose `connect()` tries each site's `SshFront` object (`ssh#<site>`) until one answers with its SSH banner, before any
/// client byte is forwarded. The object starts its `sshfront` container (`basic`, `durable_object` policy, no internet),
/// intercepts `keys.graft` and `git.graft`, and pipes the socket to port 22 (B0-24c, B0-24d). `graft-sshd` (Go, no
/// OpenSSH, git, or cloudflared) checks a key by `GET /_graftyard/ssh/key?fp=<hex>` and runs upload-pack (v2 only) and
/// receive-pack as the edge's smart-HTTP requests with `x-graftyard-ssh-key`, honoured only on the `EDGE` binding whose
/// `props` are `{"ssh": true}`. A push is `Credential::Ssh` in format 32.26, keyed `CredentialKey::SshKey` (journey 4).
pub const REPO_FORMAT_SSH: Format = Format { family: 0x20, version: 26 };
/// `SshOutbound`, a `WorkerEntrypoint` of the site script: `keys.graft` answers the host keys from Secrets Store, and
/// `git.graft` forwards to the edge over the `EDGE` binding.
pub async fn ssh_outbound(req: Request, env: &Env, ctx: &OutboundCtx) -> Result<Response, SshError>;
/// One Ed25519, one ECDSA P-256, and one RSA 3072 key per slot, as github.com serves, private keys only in Secrets Store
/// (`SecretName::SshHostKeys`: `SSH_HOST_KEY_A` and `SSH_HOST_KEY_B`, written by `deploy/ssh-front/`). `graft-sshd`
/// fetches both slots from `keys.graft` at start, serves the active slot's keys in key exchange, announces both slots
/// with `hostkeys-00@openssh.com` and answers `hostkeys-prove-00@openssh.com`, so `UpdateHostKeys` clients learn the new
/// set, and every replica serves the same fingerprints. `/api/v3/meta` keeps GHES 3.22's four properties
/// (`research/wire-census.md` finding 34); the edge serves both slots at `/api/graftyard/v1/meta/ssh` from a variable
/// the deploy writes.
pub struct SshHostKeys { pub ed25519: Ed25519Public, pub ecdsa: EcdsaP256Public, pub rsa: RsaPublic, pub slot: u8 }

// ---- Identity ----
/// 1,024 shards per home family (na, eu, apac). A new token's secret is redrawn until its hash lands in the owner's
/// family, about 3 draws on average, so its group's leader sits near the owner.
pub struct TokenShard(u16);   pub fn token_shard(secret: &[u8; 30]) -> TokenShard;
pub fn fingerprint_shard(key: &Fingerprint) -> TokenShard;   // the uniqueness point for SSH fingerprints
/// `DELETE /installation/token` commits the stateless token's `jti` and `exp` to the shard of a hash of the `jti`.
/// Write-class requests check that shard on its leader, reads honor it within READ_CACHE, and the row expires at `exp`.
pub fn jti_shard(jti: &Jti) -> TokenShard;
/// Token hashes, SSH keys, `jti` rows, OAuth and sign-in codes, lifeboat token hashes, and installation-token verifying
/// keys (on `TokenShard(0)`). `Names`, `Tokens`, and `Actions` chains have only a core tier. In `Stored`, `app` is the
/// App behind a `ghu_` or `ghr_`, and `client` the OAuth client behind a `gho_`, `ghu_`, or `ghr_`. `resource_owner`,
/// `repos`, and `permissions` belong to a `github_pat_`, and `permissions` also caps a `ghu_`. `paired` is the other
/// token's hash on a `ghu_` and its `ghr_`: the `ghr_` secret is redrawn until its hash lands on the `ghu_`'s shard, so
/// both rows commit in one entry and revoking either revokes both. `grant` and `write` are set only on a `ghu_` from
/// `token/scoped` called by the install's `Graftyard MCP` App with the additive `graftyard_grant` and `graftyard_write`
/// fields (`design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "API reference"). The host refuses approve and merge to a row whose `write` is not
/// `WriteApproveMerge`. `Code` rows are keyed by the hash of a device code, user code, authorization code, or
/// `graftyard admin bootstrap` sign-in link, on the `token_shard` of that hash. A device code is redrawn until its hash
/// lands on its user code's shard, so both rows commit in one entry. A row lapses at `expires` with no command, like a
/// lease: 900 s for a device code, 10 minutes for an authorization code [unverified for GHES 3.22], 15 minutes for a
/// sign-in link [guess]. Approving a user code commits `CodeApproved`. An exchange commits `CodeUsed` first, then the
/// new token's `Stored`, so a code never yields two tokens; a failed second commit sends the client back to the start.
/// The leader keeps a device code's polling interval in memory, so a leader change can skip one `slow_down`.
/// `Installation` tokens are never stored. A `Stored` with fields is encoded `StoredWith` (tag 11) to keep M1's bytes.
pub enum TokensEvent { Stored { hash: [u8; 32], kind: TokenKind, account: AccountId, scopes: ScopeSet,
                                expires: Option<Timestamp>, sso_orgs: SmallVec<[OrgId; 2]>, app: Option<AppId>,
                                client: Option<ClientId>, permissions: Option<PermissionSet>,
                                resource_owner: Option<AccountId>, repos: Option<RepoScope>, paired: Option<[u8; 32]>,
                                grant: Option<GrantId>, write: Option<WriteScope> },
                       Revoked { hash: [u8; 32] }, SshKeyRemoved { fingerprint: Fingerprint },
                       SshKey { fingerprint: Fingerprint, account: AccountId, repo: Option<RepoId> /* deploy key */ },
                       JtiRevoked { jti: Jti, exp: Timestamp }, LifeboatRead { hash: [u8; 32], account: AccountId },
                       VerifyingKey { kid: Kid, key: Ed25519Public, retired: Option<Timestamp> },
                       Code { hash: [u8; 32], kind: CodeKind, client: Option<ClientId>, scopes: ScopeSet,
                              account: Option<AccountId>, expires: Timestamp },
                       CodeApproved { hash: [u8; 32], account: AccountId }, CodeUsed { hash: [u8; 32] } }
pub enum CodeKind { Device { user_code: [u8; 32], interval: u8 }, User { device_code: [u8; 32] },
                    Authorization { redirect: Url, pkce: Option<Digest> }, SignInLink }
/// 1,024 shards per install or front account, on `Tri`. `account_shard` holds the account's `Account` row.
pub struct NameShard(u16);   pub fn name_shard(key: &NameKey) -> NameShard;
pub fn account_shard(id: AccountId) -> NameShard;
pub enum NameKey { Login(LoginKey), Email(Digest), Shortcode(Shortcode), App(AppId), AppSlug(Box<str>),
                   Client(ClientId), Installation(InstallationId), Repo(RepoId) }
/// Names hold only routes to an owner. The App's or OAuth app's record (keys, permissions, events, webhook, callbacks,
/// and secret hashes) is SHARED's `AppRecord` or `OAuthAppRecord` on the owner's `Authz` chain. Registering an App
/// claims `AppSlug` first, the commit point, then writes `AppRecord`, `AppRegistered`, and `ClientRegistered`, and
/// `Audit` finishes a registration nobody finished (an OAuth app's commit point is its `Client` route). The edge
/// renders an account by id from `AccountLogin`, at most 24 shards a request [guess]. `Placement`, `RepoOwner`: Shape.
pub enum NamesEvent { LoginClaimed { key: LoginKey, account: AccountId, kind: AccountKind },
                      Account { id: AccountId, suspended: bool, sessions_not_before: Timestamp },
                      FormerName { key: LoginKey, account: AccountId }, Retired { owner: LoginKey, repo: RepoNameKey },
                      Shortcode { code: Shortcode, enterprise: AccountId },
                      EmailClaimed { email: Digest, account: Option<AccountId> }, AppRegistered { app: AppId, owner: AccountId },
                      AppSlug { slug: Box<str>, app: Option<AppId>, owner: AccountId },
                      ClientRegistered { client: ClientId, owner: AccountId, app: Option<AppId> },
                      InstallationRegistered { id: InstallationId, app: AppId, owner: AccountId },
                      Placement { owner: AccountId, record: PlacementRecord } /* on account_shard(owner) */,
                      RepoOwner { repo: RepoId, owner: AccountId } /* on name_shard(NameKey::Repo(repo)) */,
                      AccountLogin { account: AccountId, login: Login } /* on account_shard(account), tag 12 */ }
/// SHARED's run, job, runner, and session events on an owner's `Actions` shards (0x15.1). `Job` applies `transition` at the
/// job's version; leases extend only by `ExtendLeases`. The leader's DO alarm commits `LeaseExpired` or `QueueExpired` at
/// the earliest deadline. `RunOwner` alone writes `RunStatus`, and `OwnerCursor` after its `RunChecks` (32.21; 32.24 adds each job's `WorkflowJob` steps, SHARED's Workflow run).
pub enum ActionsEvent { RunCreated { run: WorkflowRun, jobs: Vec<JobSpec> }, RunStatus { run: RunId, status: RunStatus },
    Job { job: JobId, version: u64, event: JobEvent }, Runner { id: RunnerId, record: Option<RunnerRecord> },
    Session { runner: RunnerId, session: Option<RunnerSessionId> }, OwnerCursor { through: Index } }
/// `hash(run.repo) % 16`: `/actions/runs` is one query. `Repo`-scope runners register there, `Owner`-scope on shard 0.
pub fn actions_shard(run: &WorkflowRun) -> u8;
/// Site-script classes, derived only, on their shard's leader site per cached `ReplyMeta`: the registration shard for a
/// broker, the job's shard at `acquirejob` for a relay, which `FeedStreamUrl` names. A leader move starts a fresh object.
/// Past BROKER_HELD_MAX a broker answers 503 with `Retry-After: 15`, and the runner polls again after 15 s, up to 4
/// tries, then after 0 to 15 s, session intact [measured, BF-54]. A relay hibernates its sockets and keeps 1,000 lines [guess]. A broker has at most ASKS_MAX asks of its shard's leader in flight, and past them a poll waits its turn: a hint wakes every held poll, and on BF-54 a broker of 2,000 polls ended `exceededMemory` 21 times in 25 minutes, dropping them all [measured, F76; that its asks in flight held the memory is an inference].
pub struct RunnerBroker { pub owner: AccountId, pub scope: RunnerScope }   pub struct LogRelay { pub job: JobId }
pub const BROKER_HELD_MAX: u32 = 2_000;   pub const ASKS_MAX: u32 = 64;   // held long polls, and asks in flight, per broker object [guess]
/// Site-script class, one per hook owner on its `home` site: SHARED's delivery store (`HookDelivery` rows) and sequencer, with each App installed on the owner as hook `-app` (SHARED, "Event fan-out", App deliveries).
/// Delivery Workers: keyless `hooklane:<wire>:<hook>` objects, each POSTing a queue's signed run in order [measured, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp1-hosted.md`]; one unreported for 30 s is re-sent under its GUID.
pub struct HookSequencer { pub owner: AccountId }
/// `graft-runners`, a Worker in the runner account, is SHARED's hosted-runner service. It holds `HostedRunnersAppKey`,
/// reaches the install only through the front, sweeps on a Cron Trigger, and runs each runner in one `RunnerBox`
/// container named by the runner id, `standard-3` or, for a 4-core label, `standard-4`, chosen at each start. A pass only registers the runner and arms its box: the box stores the JIT config and its own alarm starts the container at most once with `ctx.container`, on the `runner` image of the Worker version that runs it and with the runner as its main process, gives up before HOSTED_IDLE, and drops the config, so no Worker invocation waits on placement [doc; F80] and a 10% version runs its own image (R23). The box's application has no instance cap, so three rules bound a cold burst: `decide`'s per-owner cap refuses a registration (409) and the pass moves on; within a pass this share of account vCPU, counted from every registered runner, skips the start and alarms; and the account's vCPU limit refuses placement, which the box retries every 10 s until it gives up, leaving the registration to go stale. Overlapping passes each count only what they read, so the cap bounds them per owner and the account limit across owners [measured in memory, `workers/runners`].
pub const RUNNER_VCPU_STOP_PCT: u8 = 70;   // [guess]
/// SHARED's `IdentityEvent` gains `Record(UserRecord)` on the actor's group. These gate nothing. The `Counts` consumer
/// reads every `Record` and writes the derived watcher index, in the Bot class and at most one entry a second, to each
/// repository's metadata tier: `watcher(account, starred_at, watching, ignored, src)`, `thread_sub(thread, account,
/// subscribed, src)`, and the counts: each row the account's whole state at `src`, the `Identity` index read, never a
/// delta or an older `src` [measured, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp2.md`]; then `Counts` moves the account's `CountsCursor`.
/// `/stargazers`, `/subscribers`, and `Notify` (on a follower, with authors, assignees, commenters, mentions) read it.
pub enum UserRecord { Star { repo: RepoId, on: bool }, Watch { repo: RepoId, subscribed: bool, ignored: bool },
                      Follow { user: AccountId, on: bool }, Subscription { thread: ThreadId, subscribed: bool },
                      Thread { thread: ThreadId, read: bool, done: bool } }
/// Derived, one object per account on its home site. The `Notify` consumer fills it from chain cursors. Notifications,
/// feeds, `/issues`, and `/user/repos` read it, merged with `Identity` thread state, and lag commits by seconds (SHARED,
/// "Known deviations from GitHub"). On each day it changes, it writes a snapshot with a `sources` table of (chain, last
/// index applied) to its site's bucket and one other site's. Rebuild loads the newest snapshot, takes read and done from
/// the account's `Thread` records, and replays from followers and archives each chain in `sources` from its index, and
/// each repository in the account's `Watch` and `Subscription` records from INBOX_WINDOW before the snapshot. An event
/// from any other chain in the snapshot's last day is missing until that chain's next event.
pub struct Inbox { pub account: AccountId, pub site: SiteId /* the home site, or the next record site after a loss */ }
pub const INBOX_WINDOW: Duration = Duration::from_secs(2 * 86_400);   // [guess]
/// Cross-repository search (SHARED, `rest:search`): FTS5 tables in one object per (residency domain, shard), defined
/// by every site script and placed on the domain's first Full site with the domain's DO jurisdiction (FTS5 works in DO
/// SQLite, `eu` too [measured, `tests/m4/c15`]). `Search` writes each document to its repository hash's shard, 16 per
/// domain [guess]. A lost shard is rebuilt under the next `generation` on the domain's next site from the metadata tiers.
pub struct SearchShard { pub domain: Domain, pub shard: u8, pub generation: u32 }
pub enum Domain { Default, Us, Eu, Apac }      // the owner's residency, from its `PlacementRecord` name
/// The counters behind `x-ratelimit-*`, where GHES rate limits are on (SHARED, "Public service concerns"). One object
/// per `RateKey`, defined by `graft-site-a` created with no hint, so it is near its first caller. An isolate awaits it
/// on a key's first request, then adds its count every RATE_FLUSH requests or 1 s; replies return the window total.
pub struct RateCounter { pub key: RateKey }
pub enum RateKey { User(AccountId), Installation(InstallationId), JobToken(RepoId), Token([u8; 32]), Ip(IpPrefix) }
pub const RATE_FLUSH: u32 = 20;                 // requests per isolate and key between flushes [guess]
/// Per-isolate cache. A read-class request may use an entry up to 10 s old, keyed by a stored token's hash or an
/// installation token's `jti`, never a session: uncached, `ghs_` reads cost 260 leader `/read`s a second at 500 agents
/// [measured, fanout], which keying by `jti` removes [inference] (`04487ca`; SHARED, `authorize_read`); API reads keep
/// names and `Authz` here too ("Follower read"). The leader reads each distinct credential shard (the token or `jti`
/// row, the actor's `Account` row) once per entry, so a revocation or suspension binds every command decided after it,
/// and a write whose `decide` checks it may take its token row here (`write-identity`). A new PAT, OAuth token, or SSH
/// key commits first on its `Tokens` shard. The edge then appends the `Identity` side before it answers, and `Audit`
/// appends it only if nobody did within a minute. Installation tokens are signed in `graft-ops` with a key wrapped by a
/// Secrets Store key. The edge mints over a `fetch()` binding to `graft-ops`, versioned by `OpsPointer`, and each
/// `graft-ops` isolate caches the unwrapped key. The main edge refuses a developer's `lifeboat:read` token.
pub const SIGNING_KEY_CACHE: Duration = Duration::from_secs(3600);   // at most, per `graft-ops` isolate
pub const READ_CACHE: Duration = Duration::from_secs(10);
pub const IDENTITY_BUDGET_PER_ISOLATE: u32 = 50;   // group reads per second; compiled, never config

// ---- Lifeboat feed ----
/// A round on site A is due 10 s after the last claim (cron, then a delayed message on A's queue; hosted delivery adds
/// about 5 s, so ~15 s apart [measured, m3fr37]). It takes leaders' commit reports to A's `export` registry object
/// (higher wins), raised by probing a non-leader's commit: `Release` and `Ids` each round, one `MemberRegistry` shard a
/// round, so an unled chain is carried within a sweep [guess: 32 min]. At most 200 moved chains [guess] form one
/// `R2Key::Export` object, put create-only in site A's export bucket (7-day lifecycle [guess], by Terraform) with
/// entries after each cursor (8 MiB [guess]) from `/log_rows` on a non-leader. `FeedState` follows under `ExportState`.
pub struct ChangeBatch { pub minute: u64, pub seq: u32, pub chains: Vec<(ChainId, Index)> }
pub struct ExportObject { pub batch: ChangeBatch, pub entries: Vec<(ChainId, Index /* first */, Vec<Bytes>)> }
pub struct ExportCursor { pub chain: ChainId, pub index: Index }   // FeedState { last: Option<(minute, seq)>, cursors }
/// Export endpoints. The edge serves them under an `ExportClaims` token, or for a lifeboat's tail pulls a request
/// signed by the host's `LifeboatAdd` key. Entries and snapshots come from a follower or learner, or from archive
/// objects for archived indexes, never from the leader. Bodies are `application/octet-stream` frames, each a 4-byte
/// length, the stored bytes, and their SHA-256. Pages follow GitHub's `Link` header with `rel="next"`. A bad cursor is
/// 422, a range past retention 410, and a chain outside the token's scope 404, as a chain with no group is. - `GET
/// export/batches?after=<minute>.<seq>`: up to 100 `ExportObject`s or 8 MiB, `Install` tokens only. - `GET
/// export/chains/{chain}/entries?from=<index>&limit=<n>`: stored `LogEntry` bytes, at most 1,000 or 8 MiB a page
/// [guess]. - `GET export/chains/{chain}/snapshots?at=<index>`: the newest snapshot at or below `at`, as a member
/// installs it. - `GET export/packs/{pack_id}?network=<id>`: a pack a `Committed` marker names, its checksum in
/// `X-Graftyard-Checksum`. Paths are under `/api/graftyard/v1/`. `{chain}` is `repo:<id>`,
/// `network:<id>`, `authz:<id>`, `identity:<id>`, `tokens:<shard>`, `names:<shard>`, `actions:<id>.<shard>`,
/// `repo-meta:<id>`, `ids`, or `release`. An export token is these claims as JSON, base64url-encoded, a `.`, and the
/// customer key's signature, sent as a bearer token. The edge verifies it against its cached `Keys` and refuses any
/// chain outside `scope`; `Install` covers every `ChainId`, `Release` included. It is revoked like an installation
/// token, by `JtiRevoked` on `jti_shard(jti)` through `DELETE /api/graftyard/v1/export/token`, and export reads honor
/// that row within READ_CACHE. `graftyard vault init` mints the install-scoped one; a tenant's org token, the cell's.
pub struct ExportClaims { pub install: InstallId, pub scope: Scope, pub holder: Box<str> /* "vault", or the org */,
                          pub jti: Ulid, pub iat: Timestamp, pub exp: Timestamp /* <= 400 days [guess] */ }
/// A `lifeboat:read` token is an `OpaqueToken` of kind `Pat` (`ghp_`), so gh and git accept it. Its only claim is the
/// account in its `TokensEvent::LifeboatRead` row, and it has no expiry. The main edge refuses it, because no `Stored`
/// row holds its hash. `POST /api/graftyard/v1/lifeboat/tokens` with `{"host": "<lifeboat host>"}`, authenticated by
/// the developer's own token, commits the row, revokes that developer's previous one for the host, and returns
/// `{"token": "..."}` once. `graftyard lifeboat enroll` calls it.
/// Served in `/api/v3/meta` and the `X-Graftyard-Lifeboat-Lag` header. The host reports `decoder` to the `Release`
/// group by signing `LifeboatDecoder` with its key and sending it to `POST /api/graftyard/v1/release/entries`, when
/// its decoder changes and at least daily, and that daily entry is what the 7-day silence alarm watches.
pub struct LifeboatStatus { pub decoder: ReleaseId, pub cursor_age: Duration }
```

**Reused with the additions below.** The shared specification, `design/SHARED.md` ("SHARED" in the Shape comments), holds each of these, and the Shape blocks above define each addition.

- `RepoEvent`, `AuthzEvent`, `IdentityEvent`, `NetworkEvent`, `RefUpdate`, `Push`, and `Atomicity` ("Chain events and commands": "Event types" and "Refs and pushes"), with the stored formats of their payloads in "Issues and pull requests" and "Identity and tokens". `Event` gains `Names`, `Tokens`, and `Actions`, `IdentityEvent` gains `Record(UserRecord)`, the agent block adds its variants, and `Push` gains `provenance`, `protected`, and `changed`.
- The issue and pull request model, merge semantics, required checks as chain entries, check runs and statuses as metadata-tier entries, `TestMerged`, and the merge queue ("Issues and pull requests"), with `QueueEvent`, `QueueOp`, `QueueMerge`, and the `parent_tip` rule above added.
- The core and metadata fold tiers ("Folds"). They now live in each member's SQLite instead of node files, and `tier_of` gives the base's new chains the core tier only.
- Token formats, stateless installation tokens, first-party client ids, `AppRecord`, `OAuthAppRecord`, and invitations ("Identity and tokens"), with `Tokens` and `Names` entries, code rows, and the `Account` row read on every write.
- The Actions state machine ("Actions runs and jobs"), with job leases extended only by `ExtendLeases`.
- The two-release format rule ("Record formats and the two-release rule"), with the `Release` group's rollback barrier.
- `RestoredTo`, `ResetTo`, tombstones, and retention ("Repository repair, tombstones, and retention"), with `ref_log` GC roots and customer-signed `RepoRestore` and `RepoReset`.
- Event consumers ("Event fan-out"), with `Consumer` gaining `MergeQueue`, `Counts` (star and watcher counts), and the agent layer's `Insights`.

**Not needed here.** `Mirror` chains and cross-cell heartbeats are gone. Every group in an install or cell is reachable from that install's or cell's edge, so authorization reads the owner's `Authz` group directly, and a huge org's `Authz` group adds learners instead of mirrors. Managed logins are claimed on the front account's `Names` groups, placed on `Tri`, so a continent loss never stalls a claim or loses a name.

**Invariants:**

- **Acknowledgment point.** A 2xx follows a quorum, and `Acked` exists only after one. At that moment the entry is in the SQLite of two members on two site scripts in two regions (one member for `Solo`). Each member's copy was confirmed by its own storage before it counted toward the quorum: a follower's before its reply left, and the leader's before the leader counts itself, though its AppendEntries may leave first (Shape, the write path). A Durable Object write is confirmed "once at least three of these followers respond" across five data centers (`research/cloudflare-uptime.md` finding 32), and the output gate holds replies until writes are durable (https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/api/sqlite-storage-api/). Pack bytes are in two buckets in two regions, each persisted before its 200 (https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/reference/durability/). A leader keeps resolved tickets for a late `outcome` at most `DONE_KEEP` (4,096), `DONE_BYTES` (8 MB [guess]) of the bytes their commands carry that no event stores (an issue command's reply render, which its outcome keeps), and `OUTCOME_KEEP` (15 s [guess]). `outcome` for a ticket pruned from there answers `NotLeader` naming the same leader, and the edge's `outcome_by_request` then answers the committed command from its `request` row through `outcome`'s `replay`, so `Acked` is still built only inside `outcome`; by count alone 4,096 issue outcomes held about 60 MB of a leader's heap [measured, native allocator, `tests/m0/results/memory-budgets.md`]. The leader's host keeps no second copy of an open ticket's command: only what its marker puts read (a stored pack ref, a confirmed blob), and for a refusal the core's own command, which the `NeedIdBlock` lease loop re-sends. B0-33's leaders held 60 to 80 KB a ticket, and its deposed leaders kept 581 to 1,172 tickets open for minutes, answered under the new term [measured, LM-1 round 19, `tests/m0/results/admitted-bytes.md`].
- **One value per index.** This is Raft's log matching. A member stores at most one entry per (term, index), and a leader exists only after a majority of voters voted for it in that term. A minority side can neither commit nor, after its lease, serve a `Fresh` read [inference from the protocol]. Learners never vote, so adding 16 of them changes no majority. A restarted member that resumes `led_term` is the term's one leader, and a replaced instance cannot append, because requests that touch storage on it "will be stopped immediately" (https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/concepts/durable-object-lifecycle/).
- **One entry in flight per group.** `decide` runs on the fold plus the earlier commands of the same entry, and a follower verifies k+1 only after applying k, by replaying k+1's events in order on a scratch transaction.
- **An event exists if and only if its change committed.** Consumers read the log through cursors, never a separate outbox.
- **One writer per fold** (`apply` on each member), **one owner per fact.**
- **Merge-queue tips only fast-forward.** Each queue entry's `parent_tip` equals the previous entry's `group_tip`, checked in `decide`, so a group merge cannot drop an earlier group's changes (`research/uptime.md` finding 49).
- **Placement is checked by latency.** At join, and at most once a day after a client call reached it, each member measures round trips to each other voter and keeps them in `Rtt`, outside `GroupConfig`. `graft-ops` checks bucket `location`. A member confirmed misplaced twice 24 h apart is replaced with a new generation. The near bound is a goal, not a gate: a new group leads, then its leader re-places the home's nearer follower until the home has a voter within `NEAR_RTT`, up to `PLACE_TRIES` generations. If none is within it, the leader keeps the try with the lowest near round trip, or the member it started with when no try was nearer, stores that round trip in its `Rtt` rows, and counts the group in `placedOverNear`; the create succeeds. On `graft-m2b` every try of a create measured 108 to 114 ms against enam's 100 ms, and the create failed before this rule [measured, `tests/m0/results/carried-4.md`]. The leader runs these tries after it answers the create's `/place`, so the create returns once a member leads. Each try's join measures 20 round trips to each peer, about 7 to 12 s, so a home over the bound on every try held each create for 60 to 95 s, a fresh repository's create of its `Names` shard group and its own group up to 141 s, and a read of the repository in that time found no `RepoOwner` row and answered 503 "0 of 3 joined" [measured, `tests/am1/results/fresh-repo.md`]. A read that creates a repository's group on first use while its create has not committed `RepoOwner` answers 404, as GitHub answers a repository no one can see yet: each bootstrap member reads the row through `Names` and refuses with `noRepoOwner`, and only a majority of those refusals makes the 404 (`graftyard_lookup::NOT_CREATED`). A member that failed to read `Names`, did not answer, or refused for any other reason keeps it a 503, so an outage never reads as "not found". The group serves while the leader re-places a follower, as it does through the daily check, and two placements of one group never run at once. The daily check on the home's leader applies `NEAR_RECHECK`, half again over that bound, and a home over it on two checks a day apart re-places its nearer follower by the same path, aiming for `NEAR_RTT` and keeping a new member only if it is nearer.

**Dominant access patterns:**

- *Fetch or clone.* `graft-front`, edge, token cache (a fetch is read-class, "Follower read"), then the repository group's core tier (one DO binding call; refs from SQLite). A v2 `ls-refs` is answered there with no shard (`graftyard_git::ls_refs`), by read-index on a learner or follower and never the leader ("Follower read"; `b034-reads`, F91): one whole page per `ref-prefix` read and HEAD as `default_branch`, all from one fold, which GHES's clone checks out [doc, https://docs.github.com/en/enterprise-server@3.22/pull-requests/reference/branches]; `peel` over a non-branch, a partial page, a failed read, or an unborn HEAD goes to the shard, and the bytes match github.com's recorded answers [inference: no GHES 3.22 oracle yet]. On a clone-cache hit, R2 serves the bytes through the edge with free egress (https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/pricing/). On a miss, the network's `GitCacheShard` on its home site runs `upload-pack`, or composes it, and the shard writes the whole answer to R2 even if the client stops reading.
- *Push.* Edge, then the token read on its leader and the owner `Authz` read by read-index ("Follower read"). The pack is teed to two buckets and into the shard for `index-pack --strict`, then `commit`.
- *Issue or pull request list.* `fresh` metadata tier, then one indexed SQLite query on the leader.
- *Follower read.* The edge sends a repository's API tier reads (whole-tier rows), the read gate's access, agent, and `acquirejob`'s actions rows, and a v2 `ls-refs`'s HEAD and refs pages to the group's learners first, then its followers, at most three members, one request per read, with learners ordered by measured answer time: each edge isolate keeps a moving average of each learner's answer time as it saw it and of its deviation (a shed or unanswered read counts as `FOLLOWER_CALL`), scores a learner by its average plus `ROUTE_DEV` (4 [guess]) deviations, and sends a read at random to one of the learners scored within `ROUTE_MARGIN` of the best, padded to `ROUTE_SPREAD` (3 [guess]) with the next best whose score is under `ROUTE_LATE` (1 s, a member's `FOLLOWER_WAIT`). So no learner takes more than about a third of an isolate's reads while others answer in time, and one that mostly answers late is asked only after the eligible ones. A learner this isolate has not timed, or not timed for `ROUTE_STALE` (10 s [guess]), is eligible and does not set the best, so one that recovers is timed again. Ordered by the average alone, with one read in 20 at random, one learner took 50.2% of a gate's reads, shed 54 and answered 720 late, and the far learners answered at `FOLLOWER_WAIT` 425 to 1,567 times each; replayed over gates 13 to 15's traces, the score and spread cut the busiest learner's share from 36% to 24% and late answers from 2.6% to 1.7% of reads on that gate, and the projected API p99 from 1.44 to 1.25 s, from 1.50 to 1.29 s on gate 13, and from 0.76 to 0.76 s on gate 14; the replay holds each learner's answer times as traced, so it cannot show herding feeding on itself, and it ran about 0.6 s under gate 15's measured 2,019 ms [measured, gate 15; the replay is an inference, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-3.md`, "The herding guard"]. Every learner still asks the leader for its read index, so the choice changes latency, not what a read sees. Picked at random among all learners, a far learner (apac from an enam leader) answered about a quarter of reads at p99 2.2 to 3.0 s while the near ones answered at p99 0.8 to 1.2 s, which set the API p99 [measured, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-3.md`, "Gate 11"], and an owner's `Authz` fold (permissions, the read gate, App and install rows) and its repository-name rows (`o/r` to an id) to the `Authz` group's the same way: at 500 agents those reads were about 123/s on the `Authz` leader, and the repository leader's own `Authz` dep read queued behind them at p99 17.7 s, which with one entry in flight set its commit p99 [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, section 12]. If none answers a value and any sheds it (`overloaded`, `busy`, a 5xx, or no answer within 2 s), the edge never sends it to the leader, which took both followers' overflow and stepped down on every 500-agent row while it did [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, section 10]: a read-class request answers 503 with `Retry-After`, and a write (a push, a REST call other than `GET`, a GraphQL mutation) asks the same members again within `SHED_RETRY_FOR` (2 s [inference]: at 4 s each write that met a shed made about five member reads before its 503, against about 1.2 for a served one, and that feedback held B0-33 m56's learners at 520 to 604 reads a second for 166 offered requests; a model fitted to m56's traces puts the storm's threshold between 3 and 4 s, `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`shed-retry-2s`"), since an `Authz` leader stalled 7.4 s left both followers with no read index and failed a Human push 503 for a read of a few hundred bytes [measured, `tests/am1/results/lease-clock.md`], waiting 250 ms doubled per shed up to 2 s, with up to half of each wait taken off at random and never under the `retryAfterMs` the members named, and past it answers 503 with `Retry-After: 1`: asked again every 250 ms for all of `LOOKUP_FOR` (20 s), the writes held during B0-33 m51's leader change raised its learners' reads from 15 to 54 a second each to 136 to 514, and 4,200 writes answered 503 after 19 to 24 s [measured, `tests/am1/results/health-gate-lease.md`]; only a read every member asked declines as not its own (it names the leader, or is not joined) goes to the leader, and never a whole-tier dump or an `ls-refs`; a member that forgot its leader (F67 rule 12) sheds, not declines (`b032-shed`). A whole-tier dump (`{"rows": true}`) or an `ls-refs` never runs on the acting leader, whether its learners and followers shed it or declined it, unless the group has no other member to serve it (`Solo`): a leader answers such a read-index read `notLeader`, naming itself, so an edge isolate whose cached leader is stale moves to its next member and learns the leader, and a dump every member declined while one names a leader is shed (503 with `Retry-After`, or a write's shed retry), and an `ls-refs` goes to the shard; when members answered `notLeader` but none named a leader, a probe read (never the dump) goes the leader's way first, whose lookup reports the leaderless group and starts its election, as F67 requires of a group read only by dumps (`tier-off-elect`); only one that no member is joined for creates the group and asks again (`graftyard_lookup::leader_never_serves`). On B0-33 m53 the acting leader served 5,460 whole-tier dumps in two bursts right after leader changes, 1,625 by read-index from passes that took it for a follower and 3,835 as leader reads after every member asked declined; its first stall held 1,052 reads in 5.2 s with no write stepped, and leader-seconds with 200 or more dumps in the 4 s before stalled 6 of 20 times (30%) against 12 of 218 (6%) with fewer [measured, `tests/am1/results/b033-m53.md`, "The leader's tail"; `tests/fanout/tieroff.py`]. The cost is a 503 where the leader used to answer, for a dump no learner or follower could take; that is the flip (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`tier-off-leader`"). A follower or learner asks the leader for its commit index, which the leader confirms by lease or round as for its own `fresh` (a round while its member clock is suspect, Shape, `DRIFT`), and answers once it has applied that index, so the answer holds every entry acknowledged before the read arrived. A follower whose metadata tier is loading names the leader. One that has not applied the index within `FOLLOWER_WAIT` (1 s [guess]), because it is behind or installing a snapshot, answers `overloaded`. Neither answers from an older fold, and neither is reported with `suspect`. `READS_MAX`, `ANSWER_BYTES_MAX`, and the dump cache hold on followers and learners too, and `HEAVY_READS` on followers, and a learner behind or installing answers `overloaded` as a follower does. A learner is not held to `HEAVY_READS`: every read at one state of its tiers answers the same serialized dump, built once, it never votes, so its reads delay no AppendEntries reply or confirm, and at `HEAVY_READS` 8 learners shed 74% of journey 9's API reads at 9 to 20% CPU and 71 MiB at most [measured, `tests/am1/results/learner-dumps.md`]. It rebuilds a stale dump at most once per `LEARNER_DUMP_EVERY_MS` (500 ms [guess]), and a read whose rebuild is not due waits for it within `FOLLOWER_WAIT` and is granted again, not shed. A cached dump answers any read whose floor it is at or above, for the narrow answers' reason below: every applied entry is committed and the fold is a function of the committed log, so a dump built at index i holds every entry acknowledged before a read whose floor is at most i arrived. Required to stand at the tiers' current state, a dump built for the first of several waiters was stale for the rest once an entry applied while they waited, and each found the next rebuild past its deadline and shed: on B0-33 m61, at about twice m60's entry rate, the learners' `rebuild` sheds rose from 2,412 to 17,264 and their `READS_MAX` sheds from 4,126 to 10,873 on the same reads, and "every member asked shed" made 94% of the row's 503s [measured; the waiters' second shed is read from the code, `tests/am1/results/b033-m61-why.md`] (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`dump-floor`"). A member whose metadata tier is loading, or a metadata child, never takes another member's dump: its answer holds other rows. Only a whole-tier dump is spaced this way: a merge's rows (`{"rows": {"merge": id}}` or `{"mergeN": n}`) build no dump and are answered at once, and spaced as one they waited out a rebuild they never made, so a learner held a REST merge's tier read up to `LEARNER_DUMP_EVERY_MS` and a follower shed it to the next member; at 500 agents that read was the largest stage of a REST merge, 1.0 to 1.7 s at p90 and up to 2.8 s [measured, the stage; the cause is read from the code, `tests/am1/results/merge-p99.md`]. An `ls-refs` is one read that answers HEAD and every page from one fold at one applied index, at or above the read's floor, so a push acknowledged before it shows and HEAD and its branch's line never come from two states; a mismatch between them goes to the shard. It is never sent on to the leader: a read its learners and followers shed or decline goes to the shard, as a failed read did (an edge isolate that has not yet learned the leader may ask it among the voters, and the leader then answers `notLeader`, as for a whole-tier dump), because 36 refs pages a second on run 6's leader took its round p99 from 272 to 2,353 ms and commit p99 to 7,720 ms, and with them off the leader served none, at round p99 325 ms and commit p99 817 ms [measured, `tests/am1/results/b029-m2b.md`, runs 6 and 7] (F91). A command's `fresh` deps, receive-pack's advertisement and other paged refs reads, `ranges`, `/entries`, and alarm reads stay on the leader, except a git cache shard's own catch-up, which reads `ranges` by read-index and `/entries` from its committed log on its own site's member first (`IngestCursor`, `catchup-local`), whose one thread spent up to 942 ms a second on reads before it stepped down on B0-29 [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`]. Above `LEARNER_AT`, learners take these reads first (Scale, learners). A REST `GET` or GraphQL query over a repository is a narrow read, not a whole-tier one: the edge sends the route (`{"rows": {"api": spec}}`, `graftyard_api::narrow`), the member runs the route's renderer over its own fold and records every row it reads, and answers those rows, the read gate's access rows, and its selections. A selection is a list's filter, sort, and page, a connection's `first`/`after` page and `totalCount`, or a lookup by a non-key column, which the member makes over the whole table without sending it; the edge renders the same route over the rows with the member's selections, so the reply is the bytes a whole-tier read renders and holds only the rows it names. A selection reads only what it orders and counts by: a GraphQL `issues` or `pullRequests` connection with no filter but its states, when the states are ones `issue_order`'s open flag tells apart (OPEN, CLOSED and MERGED together, or all), counts and pages from `issue_order`'s keys and reads only the page's items; a REST `issues` list with no `labels` sorts by `created`, `updated`, or `comments` over the `issue` rows alone, which hold all three, with no `pull` or `pull_head` row; a filter by labels, assignee, author, head, base, or one of CLOSED and MERGED still reads every item of the kind and state. Loading every item before paging, a 30-item page of a 500-PR repository took 1,556 to 1,655 storage reads, and builds carried 77% of the learners' heavy CPU on B0-29 at 0.043 ms a read; paged from the keys it takes 56 to 154 [measured, `crates/api/tests/narrow.rs`, `a_page_of_a_wide_repository_reads_its_page_not_every_item`; `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-3.md`, "Instrument run (b)"]. The accounts a narrow answer checks its logins against (every account the whole tiers name, `hosted::accounts`) come from a derived index each member keeps per tier in memory (`hosted::NamedIndex`): built from the tier's rows the first time a narrow read needs it after the member starts, installs a snapshot, or finds the tier at an index the index did not follow, then moved by `apply` with each row it writes, so a narrow read at a later state reads no row for it. It is never stored, so it is not in `fold_digest` or a snapshot, needs no format or refold, and a member restored from any snapshot builds the same one from the same rows (SHARED, "Folds", derived indexes). Rebuilt by a scan at every state change, as before, it took about 1,500 of a build's 2,605 storage reads at 500 PRs [measured, same]. The routes it serves are `GET /repos/{o}/{r}`, `issues` (filters, sort, and pages), `pulls/{n}`, an issue's comments, a pull request's reviews and review comments, a commit's check runs and statuses, `check-runs/{id}`, `commits/{ref}`, releases by tag and id, `tarball` and `zipball`, and every GraphQL query but the two below. A member keeps each query's newest narrow answer with the applied index it was built at, and serves it to any read whose floor it is at or above; the floor is the commit index the leader confirmed for that read, the leader's read index on a follower or learner and its applied index on the leader (`raft::ReadGrant::floor`). Every applied entry is committed, so an answer at or above the floor holds every entry acknowledged before the read arrived, and none is older than the confirmed index. One query's answer is built at most once per `NARROW_EVERY_MS` (100 ms [guess]; a build of journey 9's lists over 500 PRs took 2 to 4 ms natively [measured, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-3.md`]), and a read whose floor the newest answer does not cover waits for the next build within `FOLLOWER_WAIT` and is then answered at the member's state at that moment (`raft::Member::fresh_after`, refused after a restart), with no second read-index round: before, each wait redid the read-index and its own 50 ms batch floor and round trip, and 8 learners counted 448 to 4,427 waits and 428 to 3,461 late answers each in 300 s at 200 reads/s [measured, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-2.md`]. A follower's read-index batches are numbered from its boot, so an answer its leader sent an earlier boot of the same member grants no read of a later one: numbered from 1 every boot, such an answer granted a restarted follower's read below a write acknowledged before it, which the simulator's live sweep found once reads were answered later [measured, `crates/raft/tests/leadership.rs`, `a_read_index_answer_from_a_previous_boot_grants_nothing`, which fails on `main` 20147db]. Whole-tier reads remain for a GraphQL query that selects by login (`filterBy` and search qualifiers such as `author:`, which read the owner's names), every GraphQL mutation and every REST write whose plan reads the fold the command sees (an issue's labels, a review comment, a release), any repository route `narrow::rest_serves` does not name, and an edge whose member answers a selection it did not make, which an older member does: the edge then reads the whole tiers. A command whose edge reads no row but the read gate's and the one task it names reads only those (`{"rows": {"access": true, "command": {..}}}`, `hosted::command_rows`): a lease acquire, renew, or release, a task create, `PATCH`, or cancel, a REST check-run, commit status, or label create, and a `graftyard_task` token claim. The member answers the access rows, the named task's `agent_task` row and its `lease_by_session` rows (which `lease::marks` reads), and no metadata row, with `"command": true` beside them; an edge whose member answers without it, which an older member does, reads the whole tiers. Every other command, whose reply may name accounts (a task's requester and a user creator, a status's creator), also gets, as a narrow read does, which of them the whole tiers name and their lowest name shards, so the edge reads the logins a whole-tier read read and the leader renders the same bytes; only a member whose metadata tier is loaded answers those, as a whole-tier read needed (a follower or learner still loading declines it, and a loading leader answers no metadata, which the edge answers 503 as it did a whole-tier read), while a lease command and a token claim, which read the core tier alone as before, ask for none and are served as the metadata tier loads. The answer is a few rows, read from the fold for each read and not cached. On B0-33 m62 these commands' whole-tier reads were 9,604 of the 15,040 tier reads every member shed (task 5,755, renew 2,881, lease 968), for 22,347 of 34,564 side requests, and each streamed a packed dump of 2.4 to 2.6 MB of which the edge kept the core tier or less [measured, `tests/am1/results/b033-m62.md`] (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`command-rows`"). An issue command's first send carries its render without the owner's `Authz` fold, about 10 KB of each command that `ENTRY_MAX` and the request body counted [measured, LM-1 round 15b]. When a command's render has no `Authz`, its `decide` puts the owner's `Authz` dependency of its entry in its output as one copy shared by every command decided against an equal fold, so the resolved tickets `DONE_KEEP` keeps hold one fold, not one each; the member fills `HostedDir.authz` from it and renders from `&Acked` as before, and its answer names where the `Authz` came from (`"authz": "dep"` or `"render"`). Every later send of the same command, and its `outcome_by_request`, carry the whole render, so a duplicate answered from its `request` row, which has no dependency, renders from the edge's fold as before; an answer that names neither (an older member) is asked again by `outcome_by_request` with the whole render, and the edge frames that reply (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`leader-render`"). The 1 MB whole-tier answer cost the edge 68 to 77 ms of CPU and about 2 s of wall per API read at journey 9, which set its API p99 at 5.0 s with member reads at 1.2 to 1.3 s [measured, `tests/am1/results/learner-dumps.md`]; narrow reads cut that CPU to 11 to 13 ms at p50 and served 99.8% of 500 agents' API reads, at p99 4.4 s, which the token, name, and `Authz` reads before the tier read now set [measured, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads.md`; the cause is an inference]. So a REST request, a `GET` or a write such as `PUT pulls/{n}/merge`, reads its token and `o/r` at once, as GraphQL did (a write resolved after its token: 135 to 705 ms of each REST merge at 500 agents fell outside every timed stage [measured], which that serial resolve fits [inference], `tests/am1/results/merge-p99.md`); a merge's own rows (`{"rows": {"merge": id}}`, `{"mergeN": n}`) go to the repository's leader, whose lease answers them in one call, since its commit goes there next and 10 merges a minute add nothing to its load, while through learners and followers they waited a read-index round and reached p99 1.0 to 1.4 s at 500 agents [measured, `tests/am1/results/merge-p99.md`]; the same REST merge reads the owner's `Authz` (its name lookup and its read ahead) on the `Authz` group's leader too, which read through learners and followers at p50 451 to 489 ms against 30 to 47 ms for the rows on the repository's leader, and set the merge's longest serial stage after the fixes above [measured, `tests/am1/results/merge-p99.md`], while 10 merges a minute add two leader reads each to an `Authz` leader that the 123/s of API reads had overloaded [inference]; and a REST merge whose isolate holds the owner's login, `Placement`, and `Authz` from earlier reads (at most `READ_CACHE` old) reads its rows and the owner's `Authz` fresh for that repository while its own fresh `resolve` runs, and keeps them only if the resolve names the same repository and placement record, else reads again, so a moved or renamed repository costs one more read and never serves another's rows; a `ghs_` token reads its keys and its `jti` row at once; the `o/r` resolves in the owner's `Authz` fold that the read gate needs anyway, so its tier read and gate start after three reads, not four; those three reads are a chain, since the login names the account whose `Placement` row is read and the record addresses the `Authz` group, so when the isolate holds an earlier login and `Placement` answer, at any age, `resolve` starts all three at once on those answers and keeps a later read only if the reads before it answered the same account and record, else reads it again: every answer it keeps is one its own chain would have read, a wrong guess costs one or two reads, and a write still reads fresh (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`git-read-class`"); and the token row, keys, `jti` row, login, `Placement`, `Authz`, and account logins come from the isolate's cache (Shape, Identity) when it holds one at most `READ_CACHE` old from the start of the read that filled it. Only a REST `GET`, a GraphQL query, or a git fetch (upload-pack's advertisement and its POSTs, a v2 `ls-refs` among them) reads the cache; a write whose command's `decide` checks the caller's credential shard and answers its refusal with the front's 401 (receive-pack's POST, a GraphQL mutation other than `mergePullRequest` and `createRepository`, and the REST, task, lease, and policy writes `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`write-identity`", lists) takes its token row and a `ghs_` `jti` row from it, else from its batch, since the leader's read binds; every other request reads fresh and refills it; a fetch whose read gate a cached `Authz` closes reads the fold again before it answers, as an API read does, and a gate decision made on a cached fold is kept from that fold's read, not from the decision, so a revocation binds git reads within one `READ_CACHE`, not two. A read-class request's gate also shares `Allow` decisions with the other isolates of its data center through the Workers Cache API (`colo-gate`, built, not yet measured hosted). The key is a URL on a fixed origin that no request reaches, never the request's host, since through a service binding the request URL carries the caller's hostname and a key built on it did not match [measured, `tests/am1/results/b034-colo.md`]; its path is a SHA-256 of the credential (the stored token's hash, a `ghs_` `jti`, or none; never a session, never the raw token), the repository id, and the placement record. The value is the decision and the time of the oldest fold read it was made on (`reopened`'s stamp), and it is served only while that time is under `READ_CACHE` less `COLO_SKEW` (1 s [guess]: the matching isolate's clock is another machine's), whatever the entry's `max-age`. Only `Allow` is put; a miss, a refusal, an error, or a match that has not answered within `COLO_MATCH_WITHIN` (50 ms [guess]) reads fresh as before, and a decision copied into the isolate keeps its original time, so a revocation binds within one `READ_CACHE`, not two. A request served from an entry at least `COLO_AHEAD` (5 s [guess]) old starts one fresh gate read after its answer (`waitUntil`), at most one per key in flight per isolate, whose `Allow` replaces the entry with its own stamp and whose refusal is not put, so the entry lapses; a test install's `x-graft-colo: off` with the bed token keeps one request on the isolate caches alone under their own keys, so one row measures both arms: replayed over mt12 to mt14's hits, sharing alone left 28 to 35 hits a row waiting on members and mt14's p99 at 1,451 ms, and re-reading ahead left 4 to 8 for 52 member reads a row [inference, `tests/am1/results/b034-colo.md`]. No revocation relies on a purge: entries never leave their data center and `delete` purges only there [doc]. In one colo a match took p50 6 / p99 12 ms and a put p50 7 / p99 17 ms, and an entry one isolate put matched from 9 others, against `gate_rows`' p50 of 634 ms on B0-34 mt14 [measured, same]. Load34 sends one PAT and its probes stick to about 8 isolates, so B0-34's 28 to 36% of hits whose gate reads members is this harness's share: clients spread over more isolates read members more often and gain more from the shared entry [inference]. Under `beside` (built, not yet measured hosted), a fetch on an isolate that holds no login and `Placement` answer for the owner matches a data-center link entry, keyed by a SHA-256 of the login and the name in their compared forms, naming the repository, owner, and placement record a fresh `resolve` last answered: `resolve` takes it as its guess, so its three reads start at once, and unless this isolate's or the data center's gate entry would answer, the gate's access rows start on it beside the front; those rows are kept only when the resolve names the same repository, owner, and record (the rule `chained` follows), the decision is still made at the gate, on the owner's `Authz` as before, and stamped no later than the rows' read, a refusal on them is read again fresh, and a guessed group with no leader is never created, so a guess addresses reads and never answers one, and a moved or renamed repository costs one read again; mt15's first second paid 905 to 1,216 ms of front, three reads in turn, before a gate of up to 1,031 ms [measured, `tests/am1/results/b034-tail.md`]. Fetches set the cache's reach: one clone is three requests, each of which read the token, the login, `Placement`, and the `Authz` name fresh while git requests were not read-class, and B0-34 mt6's 26,395 answered monorepo upload-pack POSTs took p05 792 ms on the edge, 718 to 898 ms by phase, at 11 to 13 ms of CPU [measured, `tests/fanout/raw/edgecpu-mt6.json.xz`; that those reads set the floor is an inference]. Fresh reads of one key in one isolate are batched: a write-class read with none of its key in flight starts one, and one that arrives while a read is in flight waits for it to end and then shares the next read, which the first waiter starts. So a write never takes an answer read before it arrived, it waits at most for the read in flight and one more read (each counted up to `BATCH_STALE`, 2 s [guess]), and an error fails every call that shared the read, the same way. Waiters poll plain data on their own timer, as `lookup-poll` does, so no request holds another's future. On B0-32 m3fb32 every burst push read the token, the install's `Authz`, and the owner's login, `Placement`, and `Authz` fresh, and those stages took p50 1,563 ms against 775 ms on m3fr17 while the members answered the same reads in single-digit ms [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "Rows m3fb32"]; that the time is the isolate's own work per read is an inference (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`edge-read-batch`"). It keeps only answers that let a request through: an unknown token, login, or name, a revoked `jti`, a key that fails to verify, and a gate that an `Authz` from the cache would close are all read fresh, so a new token, repository, key, or grant serves at once and only a revocation, a removed grant, a rename's old name, or a moved placement may serve up to 10 s, as SHARED's "Token revocation" deviation already allows for tokens (`workers/edge/src/rcache.rs`). The access rows already ride the narrow answer, so the repository costs one member round trip. Every API request carries `server-timing` stages (`auth`, `resolve`, `tier`, `authz`, `logins`, `render`, `total`) on test installs, and one in 100 logs them on every install. Journey 9's API reads target p99 at most 2 s, gated on B0-29's row: GitHub's API gives up on a request at 10 s and a GraphQL query answers 502 or 504 past it (`research/wire-census.md`, item 22), an agent polls its PR every 5 s (journey 9's test-merge row), and B0-18's learner read p99 of 1 s is the member's share, which leaves 1 s for the edge's identity, name, and render [inference; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`narrow-reads-2`"]. With these reads cached and run at once, 500 agents' API reads served 99.9% at p50 655 ms and p99 3.15 s, against 99.8% at 1,515 ms and 4,865 ms before; the edge's identity, name, `Authz`, and login stages are 0 ms at p50, and the tier read alone sets the p99, 2.5 s on the edge for learner answers whose member side was 1.2 to 1.3 s on learner-dumps, so the target is not met and the learner read is the next limit [measured, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-2.md`]. With the floor rule and no second read-index, the tier read's p99 at the edge fell from 2,879 to 1,669 to 1,964 ms on two clean 500-agent runs; a learner's read is now its read-index round trip (p99 350 to 720 ms, longer from weur and apac) plus, on far learners, the wait to apply the leader's index (p99 up to 1.7 s on apac), and its build wait is at most one `NARROW_EVERY_MS`. A leader that answers a learner's read-index batch at index i, and has not sent it the entries through i, first sends it AppendEntries, each within `APPEND_BYTES_MAX` and none while its link is lossy: after its calls in flight, up to `PIPELINE` calls in all, or from the first entry the learner has not acknowledged when a refusal or failure emptied that pipeline. With one call per read an apac learner got one to two entries per round trip and stayed about 12 entries behind for a whole run (gate10) [measured, same]. The learner gets both in the same response, entries first, and applies them through the normal append path, and an answer with no entries ahead of it is still valid. A learner's calls and their hedged copies end in any order on a far link, and one refused out of order empties the pipeline; with nothing sent then until `CALL_DEADLINE`'s resend, one apac learner's wait for its entries reached p99 4.8 to 7.8 s on two runs, and re-sending from the acknowledged entry on every read instead took the leader to 122 MiB and its group through two leader changes [measured, same; that the re-sends caused the change is an inference]. Split per read at 500 agents, a far learner (weur, apac) had its answer at p99 476 to 639 ms, then waited p50 574 to 668 ms and p99 1.9 to 4.5 s for the entries through its read index, which in 1,236 to 1,309 of about 1,500 batches arrived only in a later response to its own call, and applied them at p99 461 to 709 ms; the near learners held those entries already in 1,087 to 1,568 of 1,739 to 1,894 batches, and their wait was their own apply, at p99 0.6 to 1.3 s, with storage confirms of p99 1.0 to 1.4 s [measured, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-3.md`, "Where a far learner's wait goes"; that the confirms set it is an inference]. Those runs served 99.97% at p99 2,112 ms and 99.9% at p99 3,279 ms; the second's p99 is cold edge isolates whose token, name, `Placement`, `Authz`, and `Release` genesis reads took 1.2 to 3.8 s before the tier read began, so the 2 s target is not yet met on every run [measured, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-3.md`]. A follower or learner keeps up to `READ_PIPELINE` read-index batches in flight, still at most one sent per 50 ms, and an answer grants its own batch's reads and those of every batch sent before it, never a later one's, in whatever order answers arrive: the earlier batches' reads arrived before the answered batch left, so before the leader confirmed its index, and a write acknowledged before any of them is at or below it. Only the newest batch in flight is resent at `CALL_DEADLINE`, and a learner copies a read-index call at `HEDGE` only while its batch is the newest it sent, since a later batch's answer serves the older ones too. Each batch waiting on its own answer, a loaded leader's learners copied or resent every slow batch: on B0-35 m61 these duplicates were 9,535 of f0's 46,250 learner read-index calls, 30 to 40% of them a minute once the admission queue built, and replayed with the traced call times the rule sends 36,467 (21% fewer) with batch waits p99 1.78 s against 2.26 s; learner read-index calls carried about half of f0's CPU, at 2.9 ms each [measured, the calls and CPU; the replay is an inference, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`, "f0's `/raft` CPU on m61, by call kind"] (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`read-newest`"). The pipeline itself came from gate 12: with one in flight, a near learner's read waited out the batch ahead of it and then its own round trip, a grant wait of p99 0.9 to 1.6 s against round trips of p99 0.6 to 0.9 s, while its own apply was p99 0 to 6 ms, because 40 to 44% of reads had their index applied when the answer came [measured, gate 12; the replay that puts the near learners' wait at p99 0.63 to 0.77 s with 8 in flight is an inference, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-3.md`, "Gate 12 on fdf363f3"].
- *Webhook.* Leader alarm, cursor, Queue message, consumer Worker, POST. `PrSync` differs: leader alarm, cursor, the handler run at once in a new invocation of the leader's site script through its own service binding, commit, and its Queue message carries only a re-send [measured, `tests/m2/results/m2-wp5.md`, "2026-11-05"]. Run in the leader's own isolate, the handler's calls were charged to the member's newest request, one hop deeper for each test merge whose shard read back from the member, and its eighth `/merge` failed "Subrequest depth limit exceeded" [measured, `tests/am1/results/merge-budget-3.md`]. `Notify`, `Search`, and `Counts` run in band on their chain's leader alarm, from the committed cursor, with no Queue hop (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "M4-WP2's consumers run in band on the leader alarm"). A `Notify` or `Search` round reads each thread's readers (author, assignees, commenters, thread subscriptions) and each document once for its whole batch, from the leader's tier as it stands, which is what each entry of the batch already read. Read again for every comment entry, a busy issue cost each comment two rows per earlier comment in `Notify` and one in `Search`. On B0-31, about 210 comments a minute on one issue took the leader from 207k to 2.09M rows read a minute over a 600 s row, at r = 0.981 against its CPU, while its followers stayed under 100k [measured, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`, "Why the leader's CPU per request grows across a B0-31 row"] (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`round-memo`").

## Commit and read paths

Every request enters at the Cloudflare data center nearest the client and runs `graft-front`, which forwards it to the edge through a `fetch()`-based binding. The deployment's percentage split picks the edge version, unless an undo has pinned one in the `Release` group's `Pointers`, which `graft-front` caches. The edge mints one `RequestId` per client request. It calls a group member through the binding for that member's site class, by name, with that site's hint and jurisdiction, and finds the members from its cached `GroupConfig` or the chain's `PlacementRecord` (Shape, "Sites and placement"). Members call each other the same way. Every receipt and member-to-member call has a 1 s deadline. A member-to-member call that misses it is reported failed at the deadline, so `Health` and the core's resends see the miss then, and the host still awaits its answer up to LATE_LIMIT (10 s) [guess] more and steps it if it comes: a host that dropped late answers left a leader resending a 1.08 MB catch-up for 25 minutes and held one round 25 s, and with the fix 250 of 303 misses in a 53-minute soak were answered late, the slowest in 8.8 s [measured, `tests/m1/results/m1-f43.md`]. The host reports each call's end (`call_ended`), so a call resent while its first copy is still awaited counts against the leader's byte budget (Shape, "Members reach each other"). The host sends each member-to-member message once and never retries it; the resends are the Raft core's, outside the 10% retry budget: a commit-path call that fails is resent at once at most `COMMIT_RETRIES` (2) times in a row and then on `tick` after each `CALL_DEADLINE`, votes are resent on `tick`, a follower that holds every entry but whose acknowledged commit is below the leader's gets one empty round once the last round to it is CALL_DEADLINE old, so a group that commits and goes quiet still tells its followers within about one CALL_DEADLINE, and an idle leader then sends nothing (a follower learns a commit only from an append; on graft-m4a's 65567 both followers sat one commit short of a deleted repository's tombstone until a `/campaign`, `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`r29-commit-heartbeat`") [measured], and a campaign, or a new leader whose term's `Noop` reaches no quorum, gives up after CALL_DEADLINE x (1 + COMMIT_RETRIES), 3 s (`tests/m1/results/m1-wp2.md`, "On Durable Objects", Transport; `tests/m1/results/m1-wp3.md`, bug 4). An edge's re-send of a command under its request id is its own. Its only hedge is a `suspect` report to a follower on another site, and a command is never forwarded to a second member. A receipt that misses `COMMIT_RECEIPT` (2 s) from a member that answered this edge isolate a 200 on the group's commit path within the last `COMMIT_RECEIPT`, in the highest term the isolate has seen from the group, is a busy member, not a gone one: the edge keeps that send open up to `RECEIPT_OPEN` (8 s [guess]), reports nothing, sends nothing again, and every `COMMIT_RECEIPT` asks the member `outcome_by_request` with the request id and the command without its pack, since a push's replay reads only its ref names and the `request` row holds the events. A member with no such 200 is asked the same way first, within `CALL_DEADLINE`, and a 200 to that ask also keeps the send open: under B0-32 m3fr11's burst every held `/commit` took 2 to 4.6 s, so no isolate had a 200 younger than `COMMIT_RECEIPT` at the deadline, and 103 sends to a leader that held term 2 and answered `outcome_by_request` within 625 ms were reported and sent again [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "m3fr11 after the run"; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`burst-receipts`"]. The send's own 200, or a `done`, `NotLeader`, or `pending` (a ticket for `outcome`) by request id, is the reply; `unknown` or anything else leaves the send open, so every 2xx is still rendered from an `Acked` that `outcome` built. A member with no 200 to the ask either, or one that answers an error status, is reported and the command sent again, about 3 s after the send. On B0-32 m3fr8's burst, with the old rule, 110 of 121 extra `/commit` sends were these misses from a leader answering 200s throughout, 71 of them to handlers that ended in under 2 s, each carrying the 256 KiB pack again, and the 82 `suspect` waves they sent were 27 to 32% of the followers' inbound with no leader change [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "m3fr8 after the run"; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`receipt-rule`"]. Time a command spends queued in admission counts against none of these. In the account-separated option each call goes over signed HTTPS to the site Worker of the member's site instead. "Leader site" below means the site of the current leader of the named group.

**Journey 1. Atomic push of three branches to a busy monorepo.** The monorepo's group runs on `Tri`, with its home and leader on A (enam).

1. The edge authenticates the token from its isolate's cache when it holds the row at most `READ_CACHE` old, else on its `Tokens` group leader; the push's leader reads the token row and the actor's `Account` row again once the entry's commands are frozen, and that read binds (`write-identity`). The edge then reads the org's `Authz` group through `fresh`. A push starts its reads at once: the token and the `o/r` resolve start together, and the install's `Authz` (its `AgentApp` rows, which class the pusher for `agentish` and admission) starts beside both when every push reads it (32.8 enabled), else as soon as the token names a caller whose push reads it, and its `Placement` and fold reads are chained on the isolate's last `Placement` answer or the genesis record, as `resolve`'s reads are (`chained`); under `cold-write` (proposed, `decisions/DECISIONS.md`), receive-pack's advertisement and POST also take the data center's link guess for `resolve` (no rows read beside), both put the read gate's fresh `Allow` in the data center, and the POST, whose `decide` re-reads the credential and the owner's `Authz` and binds (`may_write`), also serves its gate from that entry under `colo-gate`'s stamp rule; and a spread read's learner refresh (`/status`) runs after the read answers, not before it. On a local bed of the edge's own wasm with member round trips of 60 to 150 ms, a cold isolate's first POST paid front 452 and gate 148 ms at the median, and these cut it to 160 and 2 [measured, `tests/coldwrite/README.md`]; and the read gate's access rows and an agent push's `AgentPolicy` and tasks are one read of the repository group (`{"rows": {"access": true, "agent": true}}`) that the gate and the push share, and `admission` reuses the install's `Authz` read rather than reading it again. In B0-32 m3fr7's burst, two of five traced pushes spent 10.9 and 17.3 s before validate in reads that ran one after another, many at about `FOLLOWER_WAIT` on the group's learners [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "m3fr7 after the run"]; overlapping them removes three to four serial member round trips per push [inference]. A commit goes to the leader this isolate holds without the group probe `ensure_group` sends first (the group exists: its leader answered), and after any `NotLeader`, or a reply from a term below the highest this isolate has seen, it holds none, so the next commit looks the leader up again; `ensure_group` sends no probe, join, or `/campaign` while the isolate holds the group's config at the record's generation, since a lost leader is elected through the lookup's `suspect` reports to every voter, not through the home member's `/campaign` (m42p: the probe was p50 13.7 s of a push [measured]), and a record of a newer generation after a move is not known until a member of it answers. A follower's or learner's read answer from a term below the highest seen leaves the leader held. A group whose leader answered this isolate's push `Stage` within `HOT_WINDOW` is hot here, and a push with an admission class and an inline pack over `INLINE_MAX_HOT` stages it before its first commit rather than after a refused one; nothing else makes a group hot, the mark lapses `HOT_WINDOW` after the last `Stage`, and a cool group's push keeps its inline pack, so it never pays the bucket place [inference: m3fr7's Stage re-commits paid a second commit round each]. One push stages first on any group: one the edge classes Agent whose inline pack is over `AGENT_INLINE_ENTRY`, because the leader's per-entry budget answers every such pack `Stage`, hot group or not (`agent-stage-first`, re-landed after `AGENT_CMDS_ENTRY`). In B0-32 m3fhs1, 148 of 293 `ok` burst pushes paid that refused first commit and the place, and staging first would bring the within-5 s line from 364 to at most 437 [inference, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "B0-32 after m3fhs1"]. Its first landing bunched 307 small commands into entries of 191 and 160 (m3fgt1), which `AGENT_CMDS_ENTRY` now caps at 64. A test install answers each push and each upload-pack POST with its stages (`resolve`, `auth`, `install`, `front`, the wall until the token, the name, and a push's install `Authz` have all answered, `gate`, `context`, `validate`, `admission`, `commit`, `stage`) as `server-timing`, plus its batched identity reads: `rcread` (reads it ran), `rcshare` (answers it shared) and `rcwait` (ms it waited in batches).
2. The edge streams the request body to the network's `GitCacheShard` on site A. It counts streamed bytes and refuses at its own cap, below the plan limit, with a receive-pack `report-status` message that keeps HTTPS as the default it names and points to SSH where the install has it on, and otherwise to Enterprise or splitting the push. A push whose body passes the plan limit never gets that message: Cloudflare answers 413 with its own page while git is still sending, and git prints `RPC failed; HTTP 413`. That holds for chunked bodies too, which the limit counts as it streams [measured on workers.dev at 100 MB, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp6.md`]. So the edge's message reaches only pushes between its cap and the plan limit, and the install docs and the 413 runbook name, for larger ones on Pro and Business, SSH if the install has it on, and otherwise Enterprise or splitting the push into smaller pushes. In parallel it tees the same bytes to the three site buckets with multipart parts of 16 MiB [guess]. It holds the commands and at most two parts whatever the pack's size, each part one copy every bucket's put reads, and the slowest bucket still taking the pack paces the push. Pushes of 60 MB to 2 GiB held the edge under 37 MB [measured on workers.dev through a service binding, `tests/am1/results/stream-push.md`]. A buffered body (a pack up to `INLINE_MAX`, which every B0-34 and most journey 9 pushes are: 20 KB human pushes, 10 to 70 KB agent packs and 5% at 1 MiB) can be sent again, so a shard that fails moves the validate to the next site of the validate order, and a human push's validate that one shard has not answered in `VALIDATE_HEDGE` (1 s) is also sent at once to every site left in the order, beside it, with every try kept running: the first 200, 422 or 429 decides, and the passed shard is tried last for `COLD_SHARD_MS`. An agent's validate is not hedged, since the container bounds it with its 2 s admission wait and its p50 is 1.5 s, and the last site keeps its whole wait, so a push never runs out of shards that would wait (`chain-wait`, G30). Asking only the next site first was measured and changed: on B0-34 mt17 the push that straddled the kill hedged at 1 s to site b, which was taking the killed home's load and also passed 1 s, and its validate took 2,294 ms after a second hedge to site c [measured, `tests/am1/results/b034-mt17.md`]. B0-34 mt16's one push that straddled the home-shard kill spent 2,087 ms in validate, 36 of them the shard's work, and answered at 3,454 ms against 3 s; outside kills, human validates took p50 295, p95 1,122, p99 1,725 ms over 2,105 pushes of mt13 to mt16, so the hedge sends a second validate for about 7% of human pushes [measured, `tests/am1/results/b034-mt16.md`] (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`validate-hedge`"). A body that streams cannot be sent twice, so it fails over only before its first byte, and keeps doing so with no hedge: the edge asks each shard of the validate order but the last with a bodiless catch-up at that try's wait, and the pack goes to the first that answers caught up, or to the last unasked, without the trailer, which it learns only at the end. A shard that fails once the pack streams makes the push a 503 (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Pushes stream through the edge"). Sent to the first shard only, a big push in B0-34's home-shard stall would have answered 503 with the other sites never asked [inference, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, round 18c]. The first two buckets to finish make the pack durable. The third-bucket filler, which lists `Committed` markers, fills the third if it lags, and the sweeper aborts orphan multipart uploads.
