The edge's validate names the owner's placement record, as its merges do, so the network's first shard reads no `Names` rows before it forwards: without it the shard read the repository's owner and then the record for every push, and B0-32 m3fr8's burst pushes waited p50 3,038 ms there against the pool shard's 370 ms [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "m3fr8 after the run"]. The container runs `index-pack --strict` and checks connectivity against refs the leader gave it, on the shard's disk as it stands: once the shard object has caught that container up, the push does not wait for another catch-up first. That is safe because the disk holds only committed packs, which a catch-up ingests and nothing prunes, and its refs only tips whose objects are present, so a pack that resolves its bases and connects against that disk does the same against any later one, and `decide` still checks every ref's old value at the leader. A base the disk lacks (`index-pack`'s unresolved delta or expected object, the walk's bad or missing object), an old tip that is not the disk's ref, or a new commit no disk ref reaches and the pack does not hold, is a missing base: the container keeps the request, so the body is not sent twice, and checks it again after a catch-up that started after the push arrived, when every failure is the pack's; between that catch-up's pieces it is checked again on the disk as it stands, and a base still missing keeps it again (`catchup-pieces`). A pool shard never waits for that catch-up on a push the first shard forwarded: it answers 409 and the first shard validates the push itself. On B0-32 m3fr11 the 10 burst pushes that waited on two pool shards' catch-ups, each ingesting the burst's own packs for 4.4 to 21 s, answered `warming` after 15 s and then validated on the first shard in 131 to 244 ms; they were the burst's whole validate tail, p99 15.8 s against 1.3 s for the rest [measured, `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`burst-receipts`"]. Every other failure stays a refusal. Caught up first, each forwarded push of m3fr5c's B0-32 burst waited out two catch-up runs of 4 to 9.5 s; that burst replays at validate p50 1.0 s, against 7.7 s caught up first [inference, `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`disk-validate`"]. A pack of 1 MiB or less skips the buckets and is stored inline in the entry, but only when the encoded push with it stays under `COMMAND_MAX`.
3. `commit(Push { updates: [main, release, hotfix], atomicity: AllOrNothing })` is held at the leader for up to `COMMIT_HOLD` (1 s), and the edge waits up to 2 s for its reply. A 1.5 s hold was measured and reversed: on B0-33 chA the leader's commit wait was longer, so as many commands outlived 1.5 s as had outlived 1 s, and its `outcome` inputs did not fall [measured, `tests/am1/results/combined-row.md`] (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`commit-hold`"). The push joins the group's next entry. The leader freezes that entry's commands when the entry in flight commits, or earlier, once that entry has been in flight its mean round less one mean dependency read, so the reads overlap its round (B0-29). A command admitted after that freeze does not join the frozen batch: a late join for Human commands was measured and reversed, since on B0-33 crA about a fifth of Humans joined yet their freeze wait and commit p99 still rose against sabA [measured, `tests/am1/results/control-row.md`] (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "B0-29 late join deferred" and "`human-join`"). It reads the org's `Authz`, named by the repository's `RepoSettings`, and each distinct `CredentialShard` once for all of them. Once the entry in flight has applied, it reads any chain `deps()` now names that it did not read, and runs `decide` for each in order on that fold, with exactly those chains, checking each `expect` against the fold plus earlier commands of the same entry, and checks rules from the fold and the org's rulesets. It sends the entry to B and C at once, before its own write is durable, and counts itself only once that write confirms. B or C applies the previous entry, replays this one in `verify`, and stores it. The first ack makes the quorum, and every ticket in the entry resolves then, answering the held `commit` calls with their outcome (a call held past 1 s gets its ticket and calls `outcome` once). After `Acked` the leader puts the `Committed` marker of each stored pack the entry names.
4. At "ok", the entry is on A and B (or A and C), two continents and two site scripts. The pack is in at least two of the three buckets, or inside the entry.
5. Merge-queue merges (10 a minute) and bot ref moves (50 a minute) are commands on the same group. They serialize with the push, and group commit packs them into shared entries. Each queue entry's `parent_tip` must equal the previous entry's `group_tip`, or `decide` refuses it.
6. CI's 2,000 clones a minute (33 a second) each read refs from the leader. Each is then served from the R2 clone cache, keyed by the requested tips and depth. When a push to a network with CI traffic commits, the edge asks the network's shard to fill the clone-cache entry for the new tip at the depth its CI clones used last, so the first CI clone finds it in R2. A tip that still misses costs one `upload-pack` however many clones miss on it at once, and the shard writes the result to the cache. If the group's reads pass 500 a second, learners in the busiest regions take them. In Milestone 0, with the fill on push and clones 4 to 8 s after it, 100% of 33 clones a second were served from R2 at time-to-first-byte p99 406 ms. With clones 5 to 60 s after and no fill on push, 97.5 to 97.8% were. Every clone that misses goes through the shard's object, so with clones at the instant of the push and no fill on push, up to 44% were refused as overloaded [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp6.md`].
7. The next read from any client sees the push, because only a leader holding its lease answers `fresh`, and followers and learners answer only after read-index.
8. A 5 GB clone streams from the shard with bitmaps and pack reuse. It finishes well inside the 20 minutes a runtime update allows a running stream (`research/cloudflare.md`, Workers).
9. A 2 GB push needs the Enterprise request body limit of up to 5 GB (`research/cloudflare.md`, Workers) [doc; no Enterprise zone has run it]. On Pro and Business the edge refuses it at its own cap and names SSH where the install has it on, and otherwise Enterprise or splitting the push. Otherwise the path is the same, with the shard on a `standard-4` (12 GiB, 20 GB disk).

**Journey 2. Fork pull request merged while a review and a required check land.** The fork lives in the same network, so its packs share the network's prefix. Its refs are on the fork's own group.

1. The merge plan reads the fork head through the fork group's `fresh`, and the base fold through the base group's `fresh`.
2. The shard on the base leader's site runs `merge-tree`, `commit-tree`, and the tree-diff check (`design/SHARED.md`, "Issues and pull requests"), because `PrSync` and `MergeQueue` keep that shard caught up [inference, `tests/m2/results/m2-wp6.md`]. The pack rides inline in the command (Shape, "Merge queue").
3. `Merge::decide` runs on the base leader. The review, the `RequiredCheck` from `record_check`, and the merge are all commands on the base group, so they serialize, including within one entry. A review or check committed before the merge's command is in the fold `decide` sees. One committed after it belongs to a merged pull request.
4. At the 200, the `Merged` entry, with its inline merge pack, is on two sites.
5. `gh pr view` and `gh pr list` run at once. Both go to the base leader, and both read its fold at or after the merge, so both show it merged.

**Journey 3. Issue labeled `bug`, then two lists.**

1. `IssueOpened` plus `Labels` is one command on the repository group. It is applied to the metadata tier in the same SQLite transaction that records the commit on the leader.
2. `gh issue list --label bug` and `gh api repos/o/r/issues?sort=updated` both run `fresh` on the leader. Each is one indexed query, and both include the issue.
3. Nothing is eventually consistent on this path, and no search index is involved.

**Journey 4. Member removed and token revoked, then a push over HTTPS and SSH.**

1. Removal is an `Authz` entry on the org's group. Revocation is an entry on the token's shard group. A stateless installation token has no stored row, so `DELETE /installation/token` commits its `jti` and `exp` to the shard of a hash of the `jti` (`jti_shard`), and that row expires at `exp`. Each 204 follows its quorum.
2. The next HTTPS push gets 401: from the edge when its isolate holds no row of the token at most `READ_CACHE` old, else from `decide`, since the leader reads the token's shard, or an installation token's `jti` shard, and the actor's `Account` row once the entry's commands are frozen, so a suspended account fails the same way. A pack it sent in that window is placed and validated, and the sweeper deletes it. If the member used another token, the leader reads the org's `Authz` through `fresh` as a `dep` once the entry's commands are frozen, so `decide` refuses with 403 (404 for a private repository). A push already queued in admission is refused too, because the leader reads each command's credential shard once per entry before `decide`, however long the command waited.
3. Over SSH, a stock `git push git@<host>:owner/repo.git` reaches the install's Spectrum app on port 22, whose Worker origin is the edge. The edge's `connect()` pipes the socket through the site's `SshFront` object to `graft-sshd` in its container, trying the next site's front when one gives no SSH banner, before it forwards any client byte (Shape, "SSH front"; B0-24c and B0-24d [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp7.md`, `tests/m6/results/m6-wp2.md`]). `graft-sshd` asks the edge for the key over the site's `SshOutbound` and the `EDGE` binding (`GET /_graftyard/ssh/key?fp=<hex>`), and the edge reads the key's `Tokens` row on its leader through `fresh`: a removed key gets no row and the session ends before git starts. The push then runs as the same receive-pack request an HTTPS client sends, with the fingerprint in `x-graftyard-ssh-key`, so it carries `Credential::Ssh` in format 32.26 and the leader re-reads the key's row and the `Account` row once per entry. The same checks refuse the push. The install docs say a failed session is safe to retry, because git applies a push only after receive-pack completes, and suggest `ConnectTimeout 30`: without it, a connection the edge accepted and never answered hung `git` for about 15 minutes [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp7.md`, B0-24a]. Hosted end to end on a Pro zone (`graft-m3f`, 2026-11-20): clone in 5.6 s, push in 9.5 s and read back over HTTPS, a removed key refused, and one session held 633 s [measured, `tests/m6/results/m6-wp2.md`].
4. Reads with a cached token may succeed for up to 10 s after the revocation (`READ_CACHE`).

**Journey 5. The data center, then the region, holding the newest writes is lost mid-push.** The leader is on A (enam). The entry was appended on A and sent to B and C.

- **Data center.** The Durable Object platform fences the failed host and restarts the object. "As long as at least one follower is still running, it will ensure that all confirmed writes are safely persisted" (`research/cloudflare.md`, Durable Objects, failure). A restarted A that led the current term resumes it after one AppendEntries round that re-establishes its lease. If A does not return, the edge's 1 s receipt deadline misses and it reports `suspect` to B or C, without forwarding the push. The follower campaigns on that one report once its promise lapses, wins with the other's vote, and commits a `Noop` before it serves `fresh`.
  - An entry that reached B or C survives, and the push is applied even though the client saw an error. Git's retry sees its refs already moved. If the edge re-sends the push with its `RequestId` to the new leader, the leader answers from the `request` row and does not decide it again.
  - An entry that reached only A was never acknowledged, and the client retries.
- **Region.** The same as above, but A's bucket and A's containers are gone too.
  - Every acknowledged entry is on B or C. Every acknowledged stored pack is in two of the three buckets, so at least one of B's and C's buckets holds it.
  - B becomes leader within about 11.5 s of the group's first request after the loss [inference: at most 10 s of promise, the 1 s deadline, two rounds of about 200 ms]. Campaigns are request-driven, so an idle group stays led from A until a request comes, and that request waits: 16.8 s for the first push in BM-1, then 5.4 to 6.3 s [measured, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp8.md`]. The shard on B, or a new one, rebuilds from the surviving buckets.
  - Until a replacement exists, every new entry needs both survivors (2 of 2), and packs placed in {A, B} or {A, C} and not yet swept have one copy. Sweeper lag is an alarmed metric.
  - `ReplaceMember { lost: A, spare }` (a Workflow, signed only by the executor key, "Install operations") runs only after a customer-signed `SiteLost` or once A has been drained for 24 h with zero acks in both other sites' `SiteHealth`, never on elevated errors, which drain covers. It enumerates A's groups from the `MemberRegistry` rows on B and C that name A, and adds a member on the pre-created spare site on A's continent (wnam) through a `Joint` config, under a new member generation. It copies single-copy packs first, then copies packs once per network, not per group. The new member joins by `InstallSnapshot`: the latest snapshot from R2, the archives after it, then the log tail. B, C, the edge, and the Workflows script bound wnam's member class and bucket at install, so no script version is created. It refuses a spare whose canary group has not shown that its release reads every enabled format. The group then holds 2-of-3 durability again.
  - After A recovers, `RestorePlacement` moves groups back to their home sites, and transfer restores their leaders. Removed members run `deleteAll()` after 35 days.
- **Gray region (elevated errors, no clean loss).** A leader on the sick site steps down by transfer when its commit error rate or storage confirm latency over 10 s passes `STEP_DOWN`. A hot group's leader (pushes committed over HOT_WINDOW above HOT_PUSHES) that trips on its own measure holds instead, unless both other sites' `SiteHealth` call its site sick: a loaded leader that still commits stays, and one that stops committing stops being hot and moves on its next trip (`step-agree`; B0-35 m60's term 3 moved such a leader at the end of its load) [inference; the host does not yet set the verdict, F105]. A `CommitErrors` trip also holds, hot or not, on a leader that committed at least `HOLD_ENTRIES` (5) entries in the window and is not quiet (more than HOME_QUIET_QUEUE commands queued, or an entry in flight over half of ENTRY_SOFT): commits mean a quorum of its links works, and a backlog would move with it. A quiet leader on a gray link, or a loaded one that stops committing, moves as before (`step-busy`; B0-33 m63r33's leader committed 13 entries in the window of the trip that moved it, with 259 agent commands queued, while `hot` read its pushes, which commit in bursts, as cold) [measured, `tests/am1/results/b033-m63.md`; the hold's effect is inference until a row]. Edges whose receipts miss their deadline report `suspect` to a follower on another site. A follower that gets reports from two colos within 10 s stops granting promises to a leader that is still renewing, so a leader that cannot measure its own sickness still loses its lease, and it restores them after 20 s or on a higher term. A report counts only while the follower's leader makes no progress for it, no accepted append that stored an entry or moved its commit index for CALL_DEADLINE, so an edge's missed receipts withhold nothing from a leader that is overloaded but still committing, while a half-hung leader's empty read rounds hold nobody (long sweep live seed 10353, `progress-suspect`): on B0-33 m44 LAX and NRT reported a leader that both followers heard every few hundred ms, and the group went through 18 terms in 5 minutes (`late-suspect`) [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, round 39]. A report from `Colo::UNKNOWN`, a site script's own lookup, counts for no colo (B0-33 m43, "m43's CommitErrors step-downs"). Pre-vote refuses a candidate on a drained site, one with two boots in the last 5 minutes, one that stepped down in them, or one that two colos reported in the last 20 s, so a flapping or gray member cannot win back. A voter that grants a rival's pre-vote drops its own pre-vote and starts none for one CALL_DEADLINE, so two followers that the same `suspect` reports start do not split the real vote term after term [measured, simulator, `tests/m0/results/split-vote.md`]. A follower with `commit_fail_min` (3) of its own confirms over `STEP_DOWN`'s 500 ms in the window campaigns against no leader that appended to it in the last 3 s, so it does not depose a fresh LoopLag target [measured, simulator, `tests/m0/results/looplag-handoff.md`], and a confirm-tripped leader hands off to no follower whose answers run as slow, so slow storage on every site moves no leader it cannot cure [measured, simulator, `tests/m0/results/slow-storage.md`]. A voter that has seen no leader for 20 s waives the reset rule, so a reset of all three sites still elects. The target is every group moved within 20 s and at most two leader changes an hour, `mitigated` until Milestone 0 measures it.
- **Automatic drain.** Edges and members on the other two sites count missed receipt and member-call deadlines per target site into a `SiteHealth` object on their own site. `graft-ops` drains a site on its own when the objects on both other sites report it missing `CALL_DEADLINE` at 5 times its allowed rate for 5 minutes: 1 missed call in 100, over at least 300 calls, so a quiet window is no evidence either way [measured, one install: BF-39 hosted; the write SLO's 1 in 2,000 read steady load at up to 20 times, because a missed deadline is a slow call, not a failed write; OPEN-QUESTIONS R41]. It reads them over bindings, so the sick site never judges itself and no Cloudflare API is needed, and it signs `SiteMode` with its drain-only key. The `Release` group refuses a drain-key `SiteMode { Drain }` while another site is drained, because a drain moves leadership and removes no vote, and two drains would leave one site leading every group. A leaked drain key or a bad `graft-ops` therefore cannot drain a second site, and if every voter ends up drained anyway, pre-vote lets a drained member campaign when no undrained voter can lead. An automatic drain of a sick site preempts a site day. `graft-ops` stops the deploy steps, appends `Normal` for the rolling site with its N+1 members held to the 1% lead limit, then drains the sick site; the day then waits while another site is drained and does not drain its own site again. An operator may drain a site the same way.
- **Residency placement (`Eu2w`).** Losing weur leaves eeur (Full) and enam (Witness). They commit `Degraded { lost: weur }` and keep writing with one content copy until `ReplaceMember` restores two. "Downtime" states that RPO.
- **`Solo` placement.** The one member restarts wherever Cloudflare restarts it. A data center loss loses no acknowledged write (finding 32). A region loss stops the group until the region returns, and whether its acknowledged writes survive is unknown.

**Journey 6. Cloudflare-wide outages.** See "Downtime" for the full statement. In short:

- **November 18, 2025 class (core proxy failing everywhere).** Every Cloudflare-hosted surface fails, including HTTPS, SSH, API, and web.
  - Developers keep local git.
  - At a BYOC or self-hosted install with a lifeboat, developers who enrolled also clone, fetch, read pull requests and issues, and use `gh api` GETs against the lifeboat. Its lag is typically about 30 s and is published in every response.
  - No write is accepted anywhere until Cloudflare recovers, and no acknowledged write is lost.
- **November 2, 2023 class (control plane and API).** Request paths make no Cloudflare API call, and creating a repository only names new Durable Objects and keys in existing buckets. `graft-ops` deploys and its API-reading crons wait, and a failed check is a no-op plus an alarm. A site day that cannot advance for an hour undrains its site under the 1% lead limit and drains it again when the API returns. Edge and `graft-ops` swap-backs still work, because they are signed `EdgePointer` and `OpsPointer` entries read by `graft-front` and the edge. A bad `graft-front` percentage stays frozen until the API returns, because `graft-front` is the swap mechanism and has none of its own. Whether a new container instance starts during a control-plane outage is unknown. Warm shards are sized to carry a day of load, so developers see nothing for a day, after which cold networks may fail to fetch [inference, Milestone 0].

**Journey 7. Releases and bad releases.**

- **A site day.** `graft-ops` appends `SiteMode { site: B, Drain }`. Within one lease, every leader on B transfers to a caught-up follower, and B's members refuse to campaign while they keep voting and storing. `graft-ops` deploys the uploaded version of `graft-site-b` at 1%, 10%, 50%, then 100%, then appends `Normal`. For its first day after undrain, B leads only 1% of its home groups, the chains whose FNV-1a hash of their text form is 0 mod 100, each member stepping the rule from the `Release` view into its core (`may_not_lead`, chosen [inference]), so a record shape that only a rare command writes has a day to meet a follower on N. Each deploy step and the 1% day gate on B's N+1 members against its N members (apply lag, apply and verify exceptions, ack rate) and on zero `STEP_DOWN` trips on N+1 leaders. A breach makes `graft-ops` submit the release intent's pre-signed `Rollback { target: Site(B), release: N }` with no human signature, so a release whose followers throw on one event kind stops before the next site rolls. Only a passed day lets the next site roll, and B then takes back the rest of its home groups by preferred-site transfer. After the member sites, each spare and residency site gets its own site day, gated by its canary group replaying a corpus of current formats, and keeps its old images until then. A Workers runtime update also resets members, and `led_term` lets a reset leader resume its term after one round.
- **Tenfold identity reads.** The edge ships by gradual deployment, by percentage (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/versions-and-deployments/gradual-deployments/), and each edge deployment keeps N and N+1. Workers serve at most two versions in one deployment, and an override reaches a version "even those set to serve 0% of traffic" (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/versions-and-deployments/version-overrides/).
  - `IDENTITY_BUDGET_PER_ISOLATE` is compiled. Beyond it, read-class requests use cached entries and write-class requests get 503, so identity group load cannot grow tenfold.
  - A shard that still overloads returns Durable Object `overloaded`, which is not retried (`research/cloudflare.md`, Durable Objects). The damage is limited to the tokens in that shard.
  - The release intent for N+1 carries a pre-signed `EdgePointer` back to N. An edge isolate whose identity requests per edge request pass the compiled ratio submits it, and so does `graft-ops` from Analytics Engine. Each `graft-front` isolate picks it up within `POINTER_TTL` from any `Release` member and then pins every request to N. An isolate that starts while no member answers takes the pin from a site's copy of the group's signed entries (`release_copy`, Shape, `POINTER_TTL`) within `COPY_MS`, checked against its own anchor. No Cloudflare API call is involved, and an `EdgeRoll` that would drop N from the deployment is refused while that undo is live.
- **`graft-front` and `graft-ops`.** `FrontRoll` and `OpsRoll` run each at 1% for a day, then 100%, with N kept in the deployment. The edge calls `graft-ops`, which mints every installation token, over a `fetch()`-based binding with a version override from the signed `OpsPointer`, so a pre-signed `OpsPointer` back to N swaps a bad `graft-ops` with no API call. A bad `graft-front` is rolled back through the deployments API, so a control-plane outage freezes its percentage.
- **Record shape the previous release cannot read.** While `graft-site-a` runs N+1, B and C run N, and their `verify` refuses any entry that N cannot decode. The refuser is older than the leader and the entry is uncommitted, so A's leader truncates its uncommitted tail, transfers to an undrained follower that holds its last index, and persists `no_lead_until`, which clears when A's release changes or the `Release` group records a rollback of the refusing site. Later writes to that group succeed on the next leader, and nothing unreadable commits. Formats are enabled only through the `Release` group after every site, spares and residency sites included through their canary groups, and every registered lifeboat runs N+1 (`design/SHARED.md` two-release rule). After that, the group refuses and pages on a `Rollback`, a pointer pin, or an `Abort { intent, target }` to a release that cannot read an enabled format, so no pre-signed undo or `Abort` lands a site below the release that reads its own log.
- **A rolling site that refuses valid entries.** If B's N+1 members refuse valid N entries at 10%, the refuser is not older than the leader, so no leader steps down. Each leader keeps leading, adds B to `cannot_follow`, and commits with the other follower. The refusal alarms and breaches the gate, so `graft-ops` halts the site day and rolls B back.
- **The two-site residual.** Once A and B run N+1, they can commit what C on N cannot read. C refuses a committed entry, so the leader keeps leading, adds C to `cannot_follow`, alarms, and `graft-ops` halts the site day. The group runs on two members with no spare voter until C rolls forward, and losing A or B in that window stops its writes [inference]. A `Solo` group has no second voter, so it relies on the format gate alone.

**Journey 8. BYOC and self-hosted.** See "Deployment modes".

- A BYOC company runs the same artifact in one account of its own with three site scripts, or in four accounts with account-separated sites, plus a vault account and a runner account.
- A self-hosted company does the same with no vendor token, and its `Keys` entry names no vendor key.

**Journey 9. A 500-agent fleet on the monorepo.** 500 cloud agents each push their own branch every 2 minutes and open draft PRs while journey 1's humans, merges, and CI continue. The full trace is journey A1 in `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, with rates from `python3 scripts/cost_agents.py --a1` [guess constants].

1. The group carries 27.6 commands/s against the 300 commands/s Milestone 0 gate, 25.2/s of them agent class with task updates and lease renewals, and its leader about 305 req/s. Activity feed polls, 45/s, go only to followers and learners, which answer from their applied log with no read-index call, and API tier reads and the read gate's rows go to learners and followers by read-index, never on to the leader once one sheds them, and a whole-tier dump never at all ("Follower read"); every REST and GraphQL repository read was a whole-tier dump of about 1 MB, which learners serve from one copy per state of the tiers: 94.7% of 500 agents' API reads answered with 8 learners, against 25.7% at `HEAVY_READS`, at API p99 5.0 s set by the edge's handling of the dump [measured, `tests/am1/results/learner-dumps.md`]; those reads are now narrow reads of the rows each reply renders, the page of a list or one object, so the edge renders a few KB, not 1 MB: 99.8% served at p99 4.4 s, against 93.1% at 5.0 s on whole tiers (`tests/am1/results/narrow-reads.md`). Journey 9's API read target is p99 at most 2 s with 99% served, on B0-29's row; the edge now reads a request's token and name at once and caches identity, name, and `Authz` answers per isolate for `READ_CACHE`, which took API p99 to 3.15 s, set by the learners' tier read ("Follower read"; `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-2.md`); answering a waited learner read at or above its floor with no second read-index then took the tier read's p99 under 2 s, and API p99 to 2.1 and 3.3 s on two clean runs, the second set by cold edge isolates ("Follower read"; `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-3.md`). Writes to different refs never conflict in `decide`. Milestone 0 measured the leader at 330 req/s, the 305 plus one `outcome` call per command, and feed polls at 45.4/s with none on the leader [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-29]; with `outcome` folded into `commit` it served 301 req/s (sixth pass). The 305 does not count the edge read gate's access-rows `/read` per git request and App read: uncached for installation tokens, that added about 260 req/s at 500 agents, and the leader could not hold (12,000 to 23,000 `/read`s and up to 28,105 `/suspect` reports per 600 s row) [measured, `tests/am1/results/fanout.md`]; the gate now caches those decisions by `jti` (Shape, Identity). The 305 also counts requests, not their size: every agent push and every REST and GraphQL read did a whole-tier `{"rows": true}` read of 0.5 to 1.5 MB on the leader, and at 500 agents with the gate cached the repository's members peaked at 250 to 331 MiB against the 128 MB isolate, with thousands of `exceededMemory` outcomes, `suspect` storms, and leader churn [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rerun.md`]; those reads are now narrowed: since `ccd10b1` an agent push reads only `{"rows": {"agent": true}}`, the `agent_policy` row and the `agent_task` rows, and a whole-tier read answers one packed JSON string (`eb8eb0b`) without `path_set` and `provenance` (`a271fb3`), about 150 KB in place of 1.6 MB. Members still peaked at 265 to 318 MiB at 500 agents [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rerun2.md`]. The cause is whole-tier reads in flight on the leader: on a 500-PR repository, whose packed dump is 1.18 MB and costs up to 7.8 s of CPU, the leader's wasm memory was 10.5 MB at 1 read in flight, 59 MB at 40, and 92 MB at 60, and 100 at once reset the isolate; on a 230 KB dump memory stayed flat at 49 MB, a leader served at most about 46 such reads/s, and 900 held feed polls on a follower changed nothing. The edge's lookup took every read that missed its 3.5 s call deadline as a dead member and reported it with `suspect`, so a slow leader drew more reads and more reports, and one leader's input granted about 730 reads whose callers had already gone, built a dump for each, and answered nothing for 641 s [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`]. So a leader builds the packed dump once per state of its tiers and serves later reads at that state from the isolate, which is still a `Fresh` read because the lease check runs first and the key is read from the tiers' own meta rows; it builds nothing for a read whose caller has gone; and it serves at most `HEAVY_READS` (16 [guess]) whole-tier reads at once, counting each until its streamed answer has left the isolate, rebuilds a stale dump at most once per `DUMP_EVERY_MS` (1 s [guess]), because the tiers change with nearly every agent push and one input that granted reads rebuilt for 1.2 s of CPU [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`], caps every read in flight at `READS_MAX` (256 [guess]), because one quorum round grants every read waiting on it and 600 granted together preceded an `exceededMemory` on B0-29, and sheds any new read `overloaded` while the distinct bytes of answers still streaming reach `ANSWER_BYTES_MAX` (16 MB [guess]), an answer that many reads share counted once, because a new leader held 22.9 MB of answers a second after it won and reset 18 s later, and B0-33's leaders 26 to 30.5 MB, under the isolate's 128 MiB [measured, LM-1 round 17 and m3fr3, `tests/m0/results/memory-budgets.md`], builds each granted answer once per query and input as bytes that stream out as they are sent [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`], and answers the next at once with a 200 `overloaded` reply that the lookup retries after its `retryAfterMs` and never reports as a missed member. An edge isolate sends one `suspect` report per member and term in each `SUSPECT_GAP` (500 ms [guess]). Edges do not share one read among concurrent requests: a Workers isolate refuses a promise resolved from another request's context, and every shared read answered 500 [measured, same] (F65). Admission takes Human and MergeQueue commands first in every entry and counts caused commands against the agent share. A burst of 500 agent pushes of 256 KiB in 1 s was acknowledged at p99 2.5 to 3.3 s with every merge in the next entry, but at `INLINE_MAX_HOT` 256 KiB merges waited up to 2.3 s behind entries of inline packs, which the 64 KiB cap fixed [measured, B0-32].
2. The network's git cache needs 2.68 vCPU at a push every 2 minutes, with git CPU measured on torvalds/linux and clone-cache misses composed from the cached tip [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-34]. Head-push test merges use 0.21 vCPU of `TEST_MERGE_BUDGET`, so `fanout` keeps k = 1 `standard-4` shard per warm site, at 67% of its 4 vCPU, by git CPU. The shard's per-network validate lock capped one network at about 2 pushes/s hosted (am1-runs, 405 to 515 ms a validate); `fanout` runs one network's validates concurrently, 39/s on 1 vCPU and 58/s on 4 at 8 in flight against 16/s before in the container bench, the hosted rate was about 2.7/s before, and with k forced to 4 hosted validates on pool shards took p50 456 ms [measured, `tests/am1/results/fanout.md`]. So the lock no longer bounds k = 1 on paper, but no hosted 500-agent run has passed yet, and clone-cache entries go to both warm buckets. A request reads its body before it takes the network's lock, the repack takes none, and an `/ingest` runs `index-pack` into a quarantine under the lock shared and takes it alone only to move the pack in, record it, and write the midx (`indexIngest`), where B0-34 m41's catch-ups had held it alone 5.5 to 13.5 s for packs of 0.1 to 2.3 MB [measured, `tests/am1/results/b034-trace.md`]: a validate holding the lock shared through a streamed 1 MiB pack, or a repack holding it alone, held every later ls-refs and validate behind the writer waiting on it, so the home shard's ls-refs walls rose from 2.7 to 12 s within a minute at 0.65 vCPU, and a local mix of 26 ls-refs/s and 4 validates/s on 1 vCPU went from p99 5.3 s to 0.26 s [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, round 7]. Recomputing all 500 open test merges on each of 10 base moves a minute would need 4 vCPU, so test merges stay lazy and budgeted, and `mergeable` is null past the budget. In B0-34, stock clone-cache misses cost 25 to 46 CPU-s each and saturated 7 shards, and composed misses held human push p99 at 0.68 to 1.56 s in every phase.
3. Agent commits' trailers and `refs/notes/ai` become `Provenance` during `validate`, session logs land under host-owned `SESSION_REFS`, and agent Apps cannot approve, merge, mark ready, push outside their task's prefix, or change protected paths (`ActorKind::Agent`). Only a human writer, or the controller from an issue a human assigned, creates a Graftyard-run task, and only while the org's `agents_enabled` is on. `LOG_MAX_BYTES` holds each member's log at 2 GB, so 30 days leave 8.45 GB on a member seeded with a 3.93 GB metadata tier (4.57 GB on disk), growing 0.055 GB a day, and the member reaches `CHILD_GROUP_AT` in about 32 days [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-30] (Scale).

## Scale

Numbers are [guess] unless they cite a limit.

| Quantity | Value | Limit it meets | Headroom |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Monorepo leader requests (journey 1) | 33 ref reads/s for CI, about 2 entries/s, about 200 API reads/s [guess] | 1,000 req/s soft per DO (`research/cloudflare.md`) | 4x on the leader. Each follower adds at most 100 read-index calls/s while serving up to 1,000/s itself |
| Hot group reads, learners | Above 500 reads/s; up to 16 learners | 1,000 req/s per learner and per leader; 16 nested subrequests per call chain | Each learner adds at most 20 read-index calls/s (50 ms floor, up to `READ_PIPELINE` (8 [guess]) in flight, each answered on its own), so 16 add 320/s and serve about 16,000 narrow reads/s. Journey 9's mix of whole-tier dumps of about 1 MB and narrow rows is heavier: a follower shed it past about 170 reads/s, against 1,000 for B0-18's `ranges` reads, while a learner's batches left 50 to 100 ms apart and the leader answered each from its lease [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, sections 10 and 11], and with learners not held to `HEAVY_READS` and sharing one dump per state, 8 learners served 150 of journey 9's 1 MB whole-tier reads a second, about 19 each with 5.3% shed, at learner read p99 1.2 to 1.3 s [measured, `tests/am1/results/learner-dumps.md`], so `GroupLoad` counted each whole-tier read 50 times (`LEARNER_READS` / 20); journey 9's API reads are now narrow ("Follower read") and count once, so its 322 reads/s stay under `LEARNER_AT` and its learners are placed by its feed polls and by load, not by the dump; the hosted rerun measured the leader at 243 to 249 req/s [measured, `tests/m3/results/m3-wp5.md`, "B0-18 on `e174090`"]. A learner and its leader must not call each other at once, or the call depth climbs by 2 per exchange until the leader's own AppendEntries fail (Failure modes, chain groups, noisy-tenant). So a member never calls a learner that has a call open at it or called it within `QUIET` (1 s [guess]): that learner's messages ride the response to its next call, ahead of the call's own replies, and the 100 ms ticker sends them as calls once the learner has been quiet for `QUIET`. A learner's `ReadIndex` that needs a round waits inside its own call, up to `READ_INDEX_HOLD` (500 ms [guess]), until its answer leaves in that call's response or has left in any response to the same learner: a hedged or re-asked copy of an answered batch, or a call whose answer a sibling call's response took, otherwise waits the whole hold, and on B0-29 29,547 of 29,660 calls held 500 ms or more were copies of batches already answered, beside 18,619 learner hedges [measured, `tests/m0/results/learner-hold.md`]. A learner call unanswered after `HEDGE` (500 ms [guess]) is sent once more, so one call delayed on the network does not hold the learner's queued reads to `CALL_DEADLINE`; Raft ignores the second answer. A read-index call is copied only while its batch is the newest the learner sent, since a later batch's answer grants it (`read-newest`). Voters are never held |
| Largest owner's `Authz` | About 5,000 authorizations/s at peak for 20,000 developers plus CI [guess] | 1,000 req/s per DO | Learners placed by traffic carry it; the leader sees writes, appends, and read-index calls |
| Entries per group | About 5 to 9 entries/s per group, one in flight: one round of p50 107 to 189 ms for an enam leader [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`], plus the part of the next entry's `Authz` dependency read, on a leader in the owner's home region [guess], that the round does not hide: the leader starts that read once the entry in flight has about one mean read of its mean round left, so a command waits the round in flight, the read's overrun past it, and its own round, not two rounds and a read [inference; B0-29's hosted split, round p99 304 to 356 ms and read p99 239 to 432 ms, is from before this change]; and fewer for apac [inference], each holding many commands. Milestone 0 carried 573 commands/s on one group at 441 commands per entry, with the round slowed to p50 739 ms by 0.5 to 1.8 MB entries [measured] | Entry under `ENTRY_SOFT` (100 KB since `m0-carried-8`) past its first command, or `ENTRY_SOFT_DEEP` (150 KB) while more than `DEEP_QUEUE` (2) entries' worth of Human and MergeQueue bytes waits, because on B0-35 m62 every byte-ended cut stopped at `ENTRY_SOFT` with the queue growing while the round rose only from p50 198 to 257 ms between entries under 40 KB and over 130 KB [measured, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`, "B0-35 on a test-faults build after site day 62"] (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`soft-grow`"), not counting Agent inline packs, which `AGENT_INLINE_ENTRY` bounds (counted, one 10 to 70 KB agent pack filled an entry and B0-29's commit p99 at 500 agents went from 702 to 1,718 ms [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, round 9]), and one command under `ENTRY_MAX` (1.5 MB) against the 2 MB row limit, because a member's storage confirm grows with the entry: over 48 one-minute B0-02 runs confirm p99 was 95 to 132 ms at 20 to 80 KB, 168 ms at 80 to 100 KB, 300 ms at 100 to 130 KB and 315 ms at 130 to 200 KB, and with the leader's loop behind 5 to 15% of confirms under 80 KB took 150 ms or more against 26 to 65% above, and a follower's AppendEntries body steps just past 64 KiB, 0 to 2% of bodies up to 66 KiB taking 60 ms or more to arrive against 10 to 40% at 68 to 83 KiB, where a bare object with no write saw no step, so the cap keeps an entry and its envelope under 64 KiB [measured, `tests/m0/results/carried-6.md`, `carried-7.md`]; a backlog splits into capped entries, one round each; in memory `load` holds at most `LOG_WINDOW` entries and `LOG_BYTES` (8 MB [guess]) of them, reading rows one at a time, because a count alone let 53 inline packs of 0.7 to 1 MB exhaust a 128 MB isolate in `load`, and the byte bound restored that group, whose members then took an 800 kB push (`the_in_memory_log_is_bounded_by_bytes` fails on the old code) [measured, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp2.md`, "F45"] | The monorepo needs about 2 entries/s; the merge queue 0.2/s and bots 0.8/s; the 50 comments/s cap batches into entries |
| Member storage | Folds plus 30 days of log, capped at `LOG_MAX_BYTES` (2 GB) per member; the largest repository's metadata tier (200,000 issues, 2 million comments [guess]) about 6 GB, because a 1,800-byte comment with its FTS5 postings took 2,997 bytes, so 4 GB holds about 1.33 M comments [measured, `tests/m2/results/m2-wp1.md`, "Data size"]; `request` rows about 4.9 GB at a sustained 300 commands/s over REQUEST_RETENTION, which does not fit beside a full tier and log, and 0.44 GB at journey 9's 27.6/s, before row and index cost [inference] | 10 GB per DO, after which writes fail with `SQLITE_FULL` (https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/platform/limits/) | Older entries archive to R2 snapshots, and above the cap to `R2Key::Archive` in two buckets; at `CHILD_GROUP_AT` (8 GiB of member SQLite) the metadata tier moves to a child group (Milestone 7) |
| Clone cache reads | 33 GETs/s on one key at a moving tip | No published per-key read rate (`research/cloudflare.md`, R2) | Hot keys are written as 8 replicas (`CloneCache.replica`), read at random. In Milestone 0 one replica served 33 GETs/s of 40 MB at read p99 316 ms, the same as 8 [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp6.md`], so the replicas are a margin, not a need at this rate |
| Pack writes | One write-once key per pack per bucket | 1 write/s to the same key | Keys are ULIDs, so never contended |
| Push size | 2 GB | Request body: 100 MB Free and Pro, 200 MB Business, up to 5 GB Enterprise | The edge refuses at its own cap below the plan limit and names SSH where the install has it on, and otherwise Enterprise or splitting the push; install preflight refuses a plan below the largest imported pack |
| Clone duration | 5 GB at 50 MB/s is about 100 s [guess] | 20-minute drain on runtime updates | 12x |
| Git cache shards | Daily working set 400 GB at 2,000 developers [guess], 25 `standard-4` shards per site, 50 warm (200 vCPU), plus 3 `basic` SSH fronts only where the install turns SSH on, so none in the default; a start cap of 32 per site (25 shards and the hot pool of 7) bounds them at 384.0 vCPU (`cost.py`) | 15,000 vCPU per account default [doc: Containers limits page, 2026-11-15; it said 1,500 on 2026-10-26] | 39.1x. An alarm fires at 70% (10,500 vCPU). Hosted runners run in a separate runner account. The quota request is on the day-0 list |
| Agent fleet, group commands (journey 9) | 27.6 commands/s for 500 agents, 25.2/s agent class with caused commands, task updates, and lease renewals; 252 agent-class commands/s offered at 5,000 (`scripts/cost_agents.py --a1`) | 300 commands/s Milestone 0 gate | 11x at 500. At 5,000 the token bucket admits `AGENT_SHARE` of measured capacity, 150/s if capacity is 300, about 3,000 agents per group, and the rest get the git `ng` or REST 429 shape after 30 s. Milestone 0 measured 350 to 2,350 commands/s of capacity at this mix, so all 252/s were admitted, and pinned at 300 it admitted 149.5/s with every refusal in those shapes [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-33]. With `outcome` folded into `commit`, `AGENT_INLINE_ENTRY` on every group, and `hot` from the log, three far-flung runs (ATL leader, weur and apac followers, fresh groups) held their leader with 0 `suspect`, Human and MergeQueue p99 746 to 943 ms, 253 agent commands/s admitted, and the leader at about 500 req/s [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-33, seventh pass], and a fourth run with `commit_fail_min` passed at 674 ms (eighth pass). Before the fold the leader served 750 to 950 req/s and 2 of 3 runs lost it by election (fifth pass). So zero leader changes at 5,000 agents is measured for this mix, with a thin margin: 57 to 326 ms under the 1 s line over a run, least in the first 30 s, where the apac follower's appends miss `CALL_DEADLINE` while the group warms up (997 and 1,050 ms in two runs' first 30 s) |
| Agent fleet, leader requests | About 305 req/s, none of them activity feed polls, plus the read gate's access-rows `/read`s while installation tokens went uncached (about 260/s at 500 agents, `tests/am1/results/fanout.md`), 330 req/s measured with one `outcome` call per command (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-29) and 301 req/s with `outcome` folded into `commit` (sixth pass, `outcome` 0.1/s); 45 feed polls/s on followers and learners, about 22/s each | 1,000 req/s soft per DO | 3.3x; group reads 322/s, learners above 500 reads/s |

Both warm sites take k from the larger site's `NetworkLoad`. Stock misses would need 38 to 69 vCPU, past `FANOUT_MAX`.
