Head pushes use 0.21, GETs share the other 0.79, and the rest see `mergeable: null` |
| Agent fleet, member bytes on the monorepo group | 8.45 GB after 30 days of journey 9 on a member seeded with a 3.93 GB metadata tier (4.57 GB on disk), with the log held at 2.06 to 2.15 GB by `LOG_MAX_BYTES`, growing 0.055 GB a day [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-30]. `scripts/cost_agents.py --a1`, with on-disk factors of 1.16 for the tier and 1.4 for small agent rows, prints 8.29 GiB: a 4 GiB tier at 4.64 GiB, 2.0 GiB of log (71 GB uncapped), and 1.65 GiB of agent rows | 10 GB per DO; `CHILD_GROUP_AT` of 8 GiB member bytes | 1.2x. The member reaches `CHILD_GROUP_AT` in about 32 days [measured, B0-30]. The script gives 25 days (6.7 GiB fixed, plus 0.56 GiB a month of check rows for 90 days and 1.07 GiB a month of PRs, provenance, and tasks), because its 4 GiB tier is 0.34 GiB of rows larger than the bed's. An A1-class fleet runs only once metadata child groups (Milestone 7) exist [inference] |
| Container instances per class | 2, 5, and 25 shards per site at 50, 300, and 2,000 developers, plus a hot pool of 7 per site at every tier in the shard class's instance size, so the start cap is 9, 12, and 32 | No per-class limit under `durable_object`; the account's 15,000 vCPU | The cap is compiled, so a change is a site-day operation, and fan-out draws on the pool without one |
| Container image storage | N and N-1 images per container class (git, SSH front) [guess: a few hundred MB each] | 50 GB per account, including rollback images (https://developers.cloudflare.com/containers/platform/limits/) | Older images are deleted after each site day, except that a spare or residency site keeps its images until its own site day; runner images live in the runner account |
| Fan-out | (8 pushes + 30 jobs) x 3 events per developer per day is 2,700 messages/s per 100,000 developers at 10x peak | 5,000 msg/s per queue | One queue per site; more by hash |
| Workflow starts | Member replacement for every group with a member on a lost site, only on `SiteLost` or 24 h of zero acks | 300 instance creations/s per account, shared by the three sites | 1 million groups re-protect in about an hour. Meanwhile new entries are on 2 of 2 survivors, and unswept packs have one copy until `ReplaceMember` copies them first |
| Managed at GitHub scale | 3.35 billion pushes a month is about 1,300/s (`research/git-storage.md` finding 5) | Unlimited DO storage per account on Paid | Spread over millions of groups, so no group is near its limit; cells add accounts behind the front account |

Latency [guess, Milestone 0, except where measured]:

- Commit on `Tri` is one round to the nearest follower plus up to one round of waiting for the entry in flight. A leader keeps up to `PIPELINE` (4) AppendEntries out to each voter, each new one carrying only entries after the last, because when the near follower went quiet for about 1 s the far one made the quorum and, with one call per voter, got each entry one whole call late (50 to 984 ms in 9 of 9 such calls) [measured, `tests/m0/results/carried-8.md`]. A leader whose isolate is behind reads `Date.now()` clamped to its overdue 100 ms tick, so its slow confirms read whole ticks; `STEP_DOWN` trips on `LOOP_LAG`, 2 syncs within 2 s whose confirms read 200 ms or more on whole ticks of the isolate's clock, which matched 11 of 25 failing B0-02 runs and 1 of 57 passing ones [measured, same]. The member clock, raised by peer stamps, moves off the tick, and one sync ends several confirms: on B0-29 m3fp2 two confirms of one sync read 500 / 500 there and 532 / 533 on the isolate's clock, and tripped it with no lag [measured, `tests/m0/results/confirm-storage-m3f.md`]. Milestone 0 measured that round, enam to weur through bindings, at p50 107 to 189 ms and p99 283 to 473 ms under 100 commands/s, against an idle ping of p50 135 ms and p99 151 to 216 ms, and commit at p50 172 to 370 ms and p99 399 to 690 ms over 15 runs. Neither SQLite commit added measurable time, because the leader sends before it writes [measured] (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`, B0-01). Sending first keeps a round clear of that input's own write only: the output gate holds a send "until all previous writes have been confirmed flushed to disk" (https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/api/storage-api/, `allowUnconfirmed`; SQL has no such option), so an AppendEntries issued while an earlier entry's transaction is unconfirmed waits for it, and was late by about what was left of that confirm (p50 342 ms with over 150 ms left) [measured, `tests/m0/results/carried-6.md`]. A Rust host that held each round for the leader's own write measured p99 645 to 791 ms in six one-minute runs against 433 to 481 ms for Milestone 0's final code [measured, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp2.md`, "On Durable Objects"], so the leader sends first. Sending first is not enough on its own: a SQLite write that starts while a member call's request body is still in flight held that body over 150 ms (up to 1,079 ms) in 1.3% of calls of 250 to 400 KB, against 0.2% with no write and 0.05% at 150 KB, and the same call whose body went through a `FixedLengthStream` stalled no more than the call with no write, so a member call streams its body [measured, bare-object control, `tests/m0/results/carried-4.md`]. The design's earlier 80 ms for the call was a guess.
- Push p50 is about 300 ms beyond pack transfer: token, `Authz`, commit, and report.
- Fresh reads on a held lease cost one DO call in the leader's region, plus the client's distance to it. Learner reads add up to 50 ms of read-index wait plus one round trip to the leader. Milestone 0 measured 16 learners serving 16,000 reads/s with the leader at 171 to 180 req/s, with learner read p99 at 683 to 743 ms: p50 39 to 108 ms on the leader's site, 135 to 208 ms on weur, and 245 to 451 ms on apac [measured] (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`, B0-18). The operator accepted that latency on 2026-10-28, and the learner gate is 1 s (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Measured latency is accepted, and the build continues"). Preferred-site transfer keeps leaders near owners.
- The account-separated option adds one site Worker hop to every member call (Milestone 0 bounds it at 20 ms p50).

## Downtime

| Incident | Evidence | This design | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Partition and cross-country primary promotion, 24 h | `research/uptime.md` finding 40 | No primary per database. Each chain is a three-site quorum, a minority side cannot commit, and a deposed leader stops answering `fresh` when its 9 s lease ends. Stale reads need a signed flag. There are no divergent writes to reconcile | removed |
| Hardware and power, 4 core incidents | `research/uptime.md` finding 43 | A data center loss inside a site is absorbed by Durable Object storage (3 of 5 followers) and R2's in-region replication. A whole-site loss costs one member per group | removed |
| Database version bug and major-version upgrade | `research/uptime.md` finding 44 | No database engine to choose or upgrade. Durable Object runtime updates are Cloudflare's and roll by cohort, free customers first (`research/cloudflare-uptime.md` finding 25). Cohorts are by customer and the three sites share one account, so a bad runtime release reaches all three at once. Late cohorts see fewer bad changes, and `led_term` makes a runtime reset cheap | mitigated |
| Shared permissions cluster overloaded, App tokens down 12 h | `research/uptime.md` finding 44 | No shared cluster. Permissions are on each owner's `Authz` group, with learners for huge orgs, and tokens on 1,024 shards per family. Overload of one shard returns `overloaded` for that shard only | contained |
| Partial disk failure that failover could not detect | `research/uptime.md` finding 44 | Failure detection is by the protocol and by measured health, not health checks. A member whose storage stalls stops acking, and the leader commits with the other follower. A stalled leader loses its lease. A gray leader that still acks some calls steps down on `STEP_DOWN` or loses its promises through `suspect` reports from two colos. Milestone 0's B0-06 passed 9 of 9 runs with 30% slow storage, failing writes, or failing calls on one site: every group moved within 13.2 s with at most 2 changes [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp4.md`, "F35"] | mitigated |
| Misconfigured index and online migration overloading a table | `research/uptime.md` finding 44 | No DDL anywhere. Fold schemas change by lazy refold per group, one site at a time (`design/SHARED.md`, "Folds") | removed |
| Dropped column still referenced (record shape) | `research/uptime.md` finding 44 | Followers on release N `verify` every entry. A refusal from a follower older than the leader, of an uncommitted entry, deposes the leader that wrote it, so other writes continue on the next leader. Any other refusal keeps the leader, marks the refuser unable to follow, and halts the site day. Formats are enabled only after every site, every spare's canary group, and every registered lifeboat runs N+1, and no rollback or `Abort` may then land below a release that reads them. The residual is that once two sites run N+1 they can commit what N cannot read, and the group runs on those two members with no spare voter until the third rolls forward | contained |
| 32-bit key overflow | `research/uptime.md` finding 44 | Indexes are 64-bit. Ids are 53-bit `DbId`s that the cell's `Ids` group leases in blocks, with an alarm when a cell has leased 50% of its blocks | removed |
| Middleware redeploy, LB change, expired certificate, proxy rollout | `research/uptime.md` finding 45 | Site scripts roll one per day after a drain, gated on follower health, with a pre-signed rollback. The edge and `graft-ops` roll by percentage, and swap-back is a pre-signed `EdgePointer` or `OpsPointer` applied with no API call. `graft-front` rolls at 1% for a day. Certificates are Cloudflare-managed at the edge. No database proxy or sharding tier exists | contained |
| Internal DNS failure cascading | `research/uptime.md` finding 46 | No internal DNS. Members are reached by Durable Object binding and name, and only the account-separated option uses fixed site hostnames. Object routing behind bindings is shared by all three sites | mitigated |
| Cache TTL cut plus tenfold client reads overloaded auth | `research/uptime.md` finding 47 | Identity load is bounded by a compiled per-isolate budget, not by request rate or a TTL. The read-class cache bound is compiled | contained |
| Redis failover left no writable primary | `research/uptime.md` finding 47 | Leadership moves by transfer and election, the steady-state protocol. A site deploy drains first, so leaders move by transfer before the reset (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/versions-and-deployments/gradual-deployments/with-durable-objects/). An unplanned leader reset costs its group one AppendEntries round when it resumes `led_term`, or up to about 11.5 s when it cannot | removed |
| LB tier failed to scale; a client retry loop slowed recovery | `research/uptime.md` finding 48 | There is no LB tier to scale, because the anycast front runs Workers. Retries carry budgets of 10% per isolate, commit-path member calls retry at most twice each outside it, and a DO `overloaded` is never retried | mitigated |
| Merge queue reverted 2,092 pull requests | `research/uptime.md` finding 49 | `parent_tip` chaining checked in `decide`, so queue tips only fast-forward. Tree-diff check before every `Merged`, and `RestoredTo` per repository (`design/SHARED.md`) | contained |
| Search overload emptied PR and issue lists | `research/uptime.md` finding 49 | Lists come from folds on the leader. Cross-repository search is a derived index fed by Queues, and a timeout returns `incomplete_results` | removed |
| 2023-10-04 1.1.1.1 SERVFAIL | `research/cloudflare-uptime.md` finding 1 | Not a Graftyard dependency. Clients that use 1.1.1.1 may fail to resolve the host | contained |
| 2023-11-02 control plane and API, about 41 h | finding 2 | No request path calls the Cloudflare API, and repository creation needs none. Edge and `graft-ops` swap-backs are signed entries, not API calls. `graft-ops` deploys and API-reading crons wait, and their failures are no-ops plus alarms. A site day stuck for an hour undrains its site under the 1% lead limit. A bad `graft-front` percentage stays frozen until the API returns. Whether new container instances start without the control plane is unknown, so warm shards carry a day and Milestone 0 tests it | contained |
| 2024-03-26 PDX01 power | finding 3 | As 2023-11-02 | contained |
| 2024-06-20 CDN errors on 1.4 to 2.1% of requests, 114 min | finding 4 | Front door errors reach clients for the event's duration. Replication uses bindings, not the CDN path [inference], and no data is affected | exposed |
| 2024-06-27 1.1.1.1 BGP hijack | finding 5 | Not a dependency | contained |
| 2024-11-14 Logs lost 55% | finding 6 | Audit lives in `Identity` and `Authz` entries and is copied to Basin from cursors, so log loss is an observability gap only | contained |
| 2025-02-06 R2 disabled globally, 59 min | finding 7 | Packs up to 1 MiB are inline in entries, so pushes of that size still commit [guess: most developer pushes], on hot groups too for Human and MergeQueue pushes, while agent pushes over `INLINE_MAX_HOT` get the agent refusal [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-31]. Warm shards serve fetches. Large pushes, cold clones, and clone-cache hits fail with 503. An abuse action on the install's account would reach all three sites in the default; account separation limits it to one, and the vault account keeps a copy outside it | mitigated |
| 2025-03-21 R2 writes 100% failed, 67 min | finding 8 | As 2025-02-06 for writes. Reads mostly served | mitigated |
| 2025-06-12 storage dependency under KV: DO and D1 22% errors, Queues down, Access down, 2 h 28 min | finding 9 | No KV, D1, or Access on any user path. A push makes three member calls (token, `Authz`, commit), so at 22% errors one attempt succeeds 45% of the time and three attempts 84%. Commit-path member calls are exempt from the 10% retry budget and retry at most twice each, which gives 96% [inference, arithmetic in `history/review/cloudflare-foundation-attack.md` F5]. Milestone 0 measured 92.3% to 98.7%, median 95%, because injected write failures reset the member and fail every call in flight on it [measured] (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`, B0-25). Reads on a leader alone succeed 78%. This class breaks both monthly SLOs. `Solo` groups fail 22% of attempts. Queues down only delays webhooks, because cursors hold position | mitigated |
| 2025-07-14 1.1.1.1 withdrawal | finding 10 | Not a dependency | contained |
| 2025-08-21 AWS us-east-1 interconnect congestion | finding 11 | No origin outside Cloudflare. Durable Object storage still has a third-party storage dependency (finding 23), shared by all three sites | mitigated |
| 2025-09-12 dashboard and API, 75 min | finding 12 | As 2023-11-02 | contained |
| 2025-11-18 global core proxy failure, about 3 h | finding 13 | Every Cloudflare-hosted surface fails. Data is unaffected. Installs with a lifeboat serve enrolled developers' clones, fetches, and reads from it, typically about 30 s behind with the lag published. Writes wait | exposed |
| 2025-12-05 FL1 killswitch, 28% of HTTP, 25 min | finding 14 | Client HTTP fails for affected zones, as 2025-11-18. Replication uses Durable Object bindings, not the zone's proxy, so a commit that reaches the leader should still get its quorum [measured: binding calls carry no proxy headers, `tests/m0/results/wp11.md` (a); inference that a proxy fault spares them]. In the account-separated option and on managed front-to-cell hops, calls cross the proxy, so affected hostnames also stall commits or cut that cell's traffic. The zone enables only the features it uses [guess that this narrows exposure] | exposed |
| 2026-01-08 1.1.1.1 CNAME ordering | finding 15 | Not a dependency | contained |
| 2026-01-22 Miami route leak, 25 min | finding 16 | Reachability for some networks. Anycast moves traffic to other locations | contained |
| 2026-02-20 BYOIP withdrawal, 6 h | finding 17 | Graftyard uses Cloudflare-managed addresses, including Spectrum's | removed |
| 2026-10 regional DO and R2 incidents: ENAM, WEUR 6 h 18 min, Denver | finding 20 | One member per group sits in the impaired region. A clean loss costs a request to a group led there at most about 11.5 s. The WEUR event was elevated errors, the gray case, where step-down and `suspect` target every group moved within 20 s; until Milestone 0 measures that, groups led there may see errors for longer. `Solo` groups there wait for the region | mitigated |
| 2026-10-23 North America connectivity, about 3 h | `research/cloudflare-uptime.md` open question 6 | `Tri` has one site in North America, so its groups keep a quorum. `Na3` and `Us2w` groups lose writes, and serve stale reads only if an operator signs the stale flag | mitigated |

**SLOs** (month of 43,200 minutes; a minute is bad when over 5% of a journey's requests fail, GitHub's definition, `research/uptime.md` finding 51). They apply to every placement except `Solo`, which has no availability SLO.

| Journey | Target | Budget (min/month) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Git fetch, REST, GraphQL reads | 99.99% | 4.3 |
| Push, merge, writes | 99.95% | 21.6 |
| Web pages, Actions job start, webhook first attempt | 99.9% | 43.2 |

A provider-wide event like November 18, 2025 (core traffic failing from 11:20 to 14:30, about 190 minutes) breaks every target in its month, and Graftyard cannot prevent that inside Cloudflare. So does a June 2025 class partial storage failure. The annual targets are 99.95% for reads and 99.9% for writes, about 263 and 526 minutes. Both absorb one such event a year. The two front-door events of 2025 (`research/cloudflare-uptime.md` findings 13 and 14), about 215 minutes together, would have used 82% of the annual read budget [inference]. Without drain, each site deploy would cost about one bad minute, about 13 a month against the 4.3-minute read budget (`history/review/cloudflare-foundation-attack.md` F2), which is why every site-script version is a drained site day.

**RPO and RTO per durability class.**

| Durability class | Data | RPO | RTO | Mechanism |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Chains on `Tri` or `Na3` | Refs, pull requests, issues, reviews, checks, `Authz`, identity, Actions job state | 0 for loss of any one site: a data center, a region, or a site script's release. In the account-separated option also an account | Writes and reads alike at most about 11.5 s (promise remaining, the 1 s deadline, and two rounds) [inference]; Milestone 0 measured 11.0 to 12.9 s over 26 runs, 0 failed operations [measured] (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`, B0-03). Gray failure about 20 s once Milestone 0 passes | Raft over three sites; `Acked` requires two regions |
| Chains on `Tri`, continent loss | Same | 0 | Same | One site per continent; the spare is on another continent's site |
| Chains on `*2w` residency placements | Same | 0 for one Full site, then a single copy until `ReplaceMember` finishes | About 11.5 s into `Degraded` | Two Full sites plus a digest witness |
| Chains on `Solo` | Same | 0 for a machine or data center (3 of 5 followers, finding 32). Unknown for a region, because no page says whether the followers span regions | Cloudflare's restart for an instance or data center, unpublished. Until the region returns for a region loss, 6 h 18 min in the WEUR sample (finding 20) | One member; DO PITR of 30 days |
| Stored packs | Pack and index files | 0 for one site (two buckets before the ack), with the third copy minutes later and sweeper lag alarmed. `Solo` has one bucket, with R2's in-region durability only | As the chain, because any bucket in the set serves | `DurablePack` |
| Inline packs | Packs up to 1 MiB | As the chain | As the chain | Bytes inside the entry |
| Account compromise or destructive deploy | Everything in the install account | Vault lag, typically about 30 s [guess], or the time from the chosen cutoff to the loss after a compromise that wrote through the protocol. Managed, BYOC, and self-hosted must run a vault, and an install without one loses everything | Rebuild a new install account from the vault's install-scoped snapshots, log tails, and packs, up to an operator-chosen cutoff | Vault account the install's tokens cannot reach, R2 bucket locks (https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/buckets/bucket-locks/) |
| Repository, operator error | Its named refs | Any index in 35 days | Minutes | `RestoredTo` from the log, archives, and `ref_log`, whose 35 days of ref values are GC roots, plus one snapshot per tier at or below every index in the window |
| Member corruption or bad release data | One member's SQLite | 0 (two others hold it) | Minutes | Drop the member and `ReplaceMember`; DO PITR of 30 days per member as a last resort (`research/cloudflare.md`) |
| Derived state | Folds on a new member or learner, git cache shards, clone cache, `SearchShard`s, `Inbox` objects | Rebuilt. A rebuilt `Inbox` lacks a first event from a new source chain in its snapshot's last day | 2 s core tier, minutes for caches [guess]. A shard rehydrated a 5 GB network from R2 in 52 to 118 s, 98% of it the copy through its object [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp6.md`] | Snapshots in R2 plus the log |
| Actions blobs | Logs, artifacts, caches | Lost with their site's region | Rerun | One bucket on the job's leader site |
| Delivery history | Webhook attempt records | Lost with their site; deliveries resume from cursors (at-least-once) | Seconds | Cursor in the chain's group |
| Cloudflare-wide data loss (no precedent found) | Everything | Lifeboat lag, typically about 30 s and published, for installs that run one | Whole-install restore from the lifeboat export ("Install operations") | Lifeboat holds the log and packs off Cloudflare |

## Failure modes

Components are `chain groups` (voters and learners of every group), `pack buckets`, `git caches` (git cache shards and clone cache, derived), `front door` (`graft-front`, the edge, site scripts, installation-token minting, the SSH ingress (the Spectrum app with the edge as its Worker origin), the `SshFront` objects and their containers, and `SshOutbound`), `event pipeline` (cursors, Queues, consumers, the `HookSequencer` delivery stores, and the Basin Pipelines audit stream), `actions state` (Actions groups, `RunnerBroker` and `LogRelay` objects, blobs, and `graft-runners` and runners in the runner account), `release control` (the `Release` group, `graft-ops`, site days, keys), `vault` (the vault account), `lifeboat`, `derived stores` (`Inbox`, `SearchShard`, and `RateCounter` objects, and the watcher index that `Counts` writes, all in Shape, Identity), and `colo cache` (the edge's Cache API entries: read-gate `Allow` decisions and chain header sets, `colo-gate`, built, not yet measured hosted).

| Component | Failure | Blast radius | Containment |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| chain groups | instance | One member's object resets; in-flight requests on it fail. A panic or wasm error in any member makes workers-rs 0.8.7 rebuild the wasm instance of every object in that isolate, so its neighbours' in-flight requests fail too [measured, `tests/m2/results/m2-wp1.md`, "The root cause"] | The DO restarts and replays its SQLite, without loading `request` rows, so a restore cannot run the isolate out of memory again (BF-20). A fold read, scan, or clear that storage refuses resets only that member with `ctx.abort()`, not the isolate (`131ee88`) [measured, `tests/m2/results/m2-wp5.md`, "The `tier.rs` panic"]. Restore moves the stored log and fold into the member instead of copying them, the fold audit refolds at most `REPLAY_BYTES` (16 MB [guess]) of log in memory, and a reset member's tick loop holds only a `Weak` host, because each held a second large copy of a member's state and the isolate, out of memory, trapped as a wasm `unreachable` [measured, `tests/m2/results/f47.md`]. The leader commits with the other follower. A reset leader resumes `led_term` after one AppendEntries round; otherwise `suspect` and the 1 s deadline give a new leader within about 11.5 s. A `Solo` group waits for the restart |
| chain groups | data-center | Members whose host DC failed | DO storage follower fencing (`research/cloudflare-uptime.md` finding 32). The quorum continues on the other two sites |
| chain groups | region | One member of every group with a member there | Two other sites keep a quorum and elect within about 11.5 s of a group's first request. A total loss is left by `suspect` and election, because a leader whose every call fails completes no commit attempt and `STEP_DOWN` cannot trip (BM-1, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp8.md`); a gray region by `suspect` or a signed drain, and by `STEP_DOWN` only on a group busy enough to fill its window: at 30% call errors no `STEP_DOWN` fired in BM-1's gray rerun (F41). `ReplaceMember` runs only on `SiteLost` or 24 h of zero acks, onto the same continent's spare, and `RestorePlacement` moves groups home after recovery. `*2w` groups go `Degraded`. `Solo` groups there wait. Learners there drop out |
| chain groups | provider-wide | Every member: DO errors (June 2025) or no reachability (November 2025) | 1 s deadlines, a `suspect` report to a follower on another site as the only hedge, and two core resends per commit-path call outside the 10% budget (the host sends each message once). Writes return 503 and nothing acknowledged is lost. Stale reads only under a signed flag. A partial proxy failure (December 2025) leaves binding replication up [inference] |
| chain groups | control-plane | None at runtime | Members and learners are created by name on first use, except every `Tokens` shard's group, which install creates ("Install operations", Genesis). Classes, site scripts, and spare sites exist from install, and every script binds the spares' member classes and buckets, so `ReplaceMember` creates no script version |
| chain groups | bad-deploy | One site script's members, because each script rolls on its own day after a drain | Followers `verify` with the old release. A refusal by an older follower of an uncommitted entry makes the leader truncate, transfer to an undrained follower holding its last index, and stop leading until its release changes or the refusing site rolls back. Any other refusal keeps the leader, marks the refuser unable to follow, and halts the site day. Each step and the new site's 1% day gate on N+1 apply lag, apply and verify exceptions, ack rate, and `STEP_DOWN` trips, and a breach runs the pre-signed `Rollback` with no human signature |
| chain groups | bad-config | One site script's config, such as a wrong jurisdiction or region. An account-level setting reaches all three sites in the default | The latency check refuses misplaced members, and a failed check is retried with a new member generation. Site config rolls per script as a site day. Account settings are code, reviewed by two people, applied off-peak. Account-separated sites contain account settings to one voter |
| chain groups | noisy-tenant | One group, for example a viral repository, or its learners nesting calls into the leader | 1,000 req/s per object bounds it to itself. Per-repository admission, follower and learner reads, and a cap of 50 comment commands/s. The runtime charges an object's outgoing calls to the newest request it received, even after that request finished, and fails a call 16 deep "Subrequest depth limit exceeded" [measured, `tests/m3/results/m3-wp5.md`, "Where the depth comes from"]. When the leader called learners that called it, each exchange added 2 until the leader's own AppendEntries to its voters failed and `STEP_DOWN` tripped, so B0-18 failed 12 of 12 traced runs; with the leader holding a calling learner's messages for that learner's next call, the deepest call was 4 to 6 hops and B0-18 passed 6 of 6 [measured, same] |
| chain groups | noisy-tenant | An agent fleet bursting on one group (journey 9), or agents on user tokens | Admission by origin and behavior takes Human and MergeQueue first, gives Agent a floor Bot cannot take, and caps it with a token bucket of `AGENT_SHARE` and at `AGENT_CMDS_ENTRY` commands per entry, with caused commands and fast user tokens counted as Agent. Overflow waits up to 30 s, then gets the git `ng` or REST 429 shape. `ActorKind::Agent` refusals keep a Graftyard-run or confined App inside its prefix, and lease and open-task caps bound what one holder, App, or requester can write |
| pack buckets | instance | Single requests 5xx | Retries; ULID keys never contend |
| pack buckets | data-center | None | R2 replicates "across several data centers within a geographic region" (`research/cloudflare.md`, R2) |
| pack buckets | region | One bucket of three | Two-of-three placement means every acknowledged pack has a copy elsewhere. Pushes place into the two live buckets. `ReplaceMember` copies single-copy packs first, then each network once into the spare's bucket |
| pack buckets | provider-wide | All buckets (February and March 2025) | Inline packs up to 1 MiB keep most pushes committing. Warm shards serve fetches. Large pushes and cold clones return 503 |
| pack buckets | provider-wide | Hot groups during an R2-wide outage | While placement fails or a bucket's readiness probe is red, a hot group inlines up to 1 MiB for Human and MergeQueue pushes, and `LOG_MAX_BYTES` archives the extra after recovery. Agent pushes over `INLINE_MAX_HOT` get the agent refusal at once and are never queued, because at one 1 MiB push per entry the group commits 1.3 to 2.0 MB/s from apac, under half of journey 9's 4.37 MB/s [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-31] |
| pack buckets | control-plane | None at runtime | Buckets, including spare buckets, are pre-created per site and their location checked at install |
| pack buckets | bad-deploy | Bad packs from one site's release | `index-pack --strict` before placement; a rolled pack is validated and placed as a promotion's, with its `.idx`, before its `Compacted` commits, only on a site whose `GRAFTYARD_ROLLUP` a site day set after `FormatsEnable` enabled 32.25, and a rehydrate that cannot load it loads the packs it consumed, which the sweeper deletes, then their markers, only past CONSUMED_KEEP (7 days [guess]) once every shard holding the network answers a warm `/status` past that `Compacted` or holds no disk for it; GC deletes only objects outside the fold refs and `ref_log` for 14 days, at a fixed rate |
| pack buckets | bad-config | Writes to one site's bucket fail | A readiness probe writes and reads a canary object per bucket. Placement fails over to the other two. A misplaced bucket outside a jurisdiction is recreated under a new name as a site day; inside one the hint is ignored (R16) |
| pack buckets | noisy-tenant | One network's prefix | Push bytes and concurrency caps per account and network |
| git caches | instance | One shard's networks and in-flight streams; on the site's first pool shard, also its pool ledger | Clients retry. While the ledger's object restarts, leaders that ask it keep k, and the ledger's SQLite keeps its leases. The time it was down counts against none: its alarm runs it every 45 s while it holds a lease, so a longer gap is an outage, never a quiet ledger. A new instance rehydrates from R2, at most 118 s for a 5 GB network in Milestone 0 [measured], with prebuilt indexes. Real pushed packs are thin and re-index on ingest: 257 of them, 2.62 GB, took 1,694 to 2,496 s per shard on m29 [measured, G5]. A promoted network loads each promotion through its stored `.idx` and skips the pushed packs it covers, about 90 s for 5 GB at m29's one-stream R2 rate with four fetches at once [inference, `crates/git/tests/catchup.rs`; the multi-stream rate is unmeasured], with its connectivity check before its repack, which runs after it serves; pushes promotion has not caught up still re-index (G9). Clone-cache hits never touch a container. A new leadership's warm catch-up of its site's shard (BM-2) reaches only a running container and is not a request, so a group with no clients, which re-elects after its members idle out, starts and keeps no shard. A due range's promotion starts a stopped shard (`warm=start`) but is no request either: on a shard no client used in `WARM_FOR` it stops `PROMOTE_LAPSE` (10 minutes [guess]) after the promotion's last call, not 6 h. A leader's pool warm of a slot its network holds does start a stopped pool shard, since a pool shard nobody started never becomes warm, and the ledger tears the slot down when the lease goes, so no idle group bills one; a rehydrate loads the marked packs a chain's tail names with the tail, in index order, because a stored pack thin against an earlier inline pack failed every rehydrate loaded first [measured, `tests/am1/results/idle-shards.md`] |
| git caches | data-center | Shards on that host set | As instance. Each network is warm on two sites, and `fanout` sizes a hot network's k on both from the larger site's `NetworkLoad`, so the other warm site already holds k shards. A newly warm shard serves only after `rehydrate`: in B0-34 six shards copying 8.76 GB from one bucket took 166 to 207 s, and while the surviving site held k = 1, miss time to first byte was 19 to 20 s at p99 [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-34]. The surviving site's shards start and keep no maintenance while they carry the lost site's work (`maint-yield`) |
| git caches | region | That site's shards | The other warm site serves. Each site's start cap lets any two sites carry every network |
| git caches | region | A hot network's k shards and clone-cache entries on one warm site | The other warm site holds its own k shards, sized from the larger of the two sites' `NetworkLoad`, and a copy of every hot-network clone-cache entry, so it carries the network alone, 67% of 4 vCPU in journey 9. Without that sizing the surviving site held k = 1 in B0-34, new shards took 166 to 207 s to rehydrate, and miss time to first byte was 19 to 20 s at p99 meanwhile, while clone-cache hits stayed at 0.34 to 0.37 s [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-34] |
| git caches | provider-wide | All containers | Clone-cache hits keep serving if R2 serves. Otherwise git is down with everything else |
| git caches | control-plane | New container instances may not start [unknown]; new images cannot be uploaded | Warm shards carry a day of load, their alarms renewing a 6 h inactivity timeout until 24 h after the last request (recorded at most once per `TOUCH_EVERY`, 1 min, so a burst of pushes writes it once, not once a push), so no start is needed for a day [inference]. A `ContainerDay` and its rollback need no API call, because the image map shipped on the site day. `fanout` runs in band from `NetworkLoad` and draws on the hot pool, so a network that turns hot needs no API call. Running images keep serving. Milestone 0 tests starts and a newly hot network with the API unreachable |
| git caches | bad-deploy | One site's git image | A new image reaches shards only on that site's `ContainerDay`, never with member code, because a new version restarts no running container [measured]. A conformance clone, fetch, and push gate each step, and a breach commits the pre-signed `Rollback { target: Containers(site) }`, which returns shards to `git_prev` with no API call |
| git caches | bad-config | Shard count or start cap too low on one site, or a start check that lets a container run outside its site's regions | The other warm site absorbs. Working set and shard count are measured; a change is a site day; alarm at 70% of account vCPU |
| git caches | noisy-tenant | One giant network's repack or clone storm | Per-network CPU caps, the clone cache, one repack per shard at a time, at nice 19 on 2 of 4 threads with a bounded delta window, which leaves kept promotions alone. A roll-up takes the repack's slot, at nice 19 on 2 threads, and reads at most ROLL_IN_MAX (2 GiB [guess]) of a chain's oldest consecutive promoted packs no `Compacted` consumed, so its RSS stays near 1.7 times that, 3.6 GB [measured, `roll-up`]; a rolled pack never rolls again. Each site starts at most its cap of shard containers, enforced in the shard object rather than by the platform, and hosted runners run in a separate account, so Actions cannot take git cache vCPU. Nice 19 bounds the repack's CPU, not its harm: on B0-34 mt18 a repack that ran through the kill on the surviving site held the midx lock about 330 s while starved, its unkept packs grew from 122 to 268, and its clone misses took a median 3.3 to 12.6 s a minute [measured, `tests/am1/results/b034-mt18.md`; the lock as the cause is inference]. So maintenance yields to moved load and to client CPU above `FANOUT_UP`, at most `MAINT_DEFER_MAX` in all (Shape, git cache shards; `maint-yield`) |
| git caches | noisy-tenant | An agent fleet's git CPU on one network (journey 9), or one owner's several hot networks | `fanout` up to 8 shards per warm site from a hot pool budgeted per owner by each site's pool ledger, which grants every network distinct pool shards and keeps what a network holds before it grants another owner more, `AdmitClass` in each shard's queue so agent work sheds first, `TEST_MERGE_BUDGET`, and the agent layer's conflict predictor capped at 50% of the second site's shard vCPU and paused while that site leads |
| front door | instance | Requests on one isolate; for SSH, the sessions on one front container, or the `connect()` sessions one edge isolate holds | Anycast and clients retry. Sessions the container held are cut. The next session to reach that `SshFront` starts the container again and waits for its banner, a cold start of about 2 to 6 s [measured, `tests/m6/results/m6-wp2.md`]; the object keeps no alarm and no pending `monitor()`, so its container stops within `IDLE_MS` (1 h) of its last session [measured, `tests/m6/results/m6-wp2.md`, "Idle stop"]. A new session's `connect()` tries the next site's front when no banner comes, and a failed session is safe to retry. Kills on this ingress are not yet run (M6-WP2) |
| front door | data-center | That colo's traffic, including the SSH sessions Spectrum and the edge hold there | Cloudflare routes new connections to the next colo; open SSH sessions there are cut and the client retries [inference] |
| front door | region | Members and the SSH front of one site | The edge calls the other members through their bindings, and `suspect` moves leaders. Leader hints in isolate memory update on `NotLeader`. A new SSH session's `connect()` gets no banner from that site's front and fails over to the next site's front before it forwards a byte |
| front door | provider-wide | Everything (November 18, 2025), or client HTTP for some zones (December 5, 2025). A Spectrum-wide or Workers-wide failure, or the private-beta Worker origin withdrawn, cuts SSH | Lifeboat for BYOC and self-hosted installs. Otherwise stated as exposed. HTTPS, the default transport, does not use Spectrum, so SSH users fall back to HTTPS |
| front door | control-plane | No deploys, WAF, or Spectrum changes | Runtime needs none. The Spectrum app is set once by `graftyard ssh spectrum` and needs no API call to serve. Edge and `graft-ops` swap-backs are signed `EdgePointer` and `OpsPointer` entries. A bad `graft-front` percentage stays frozen until the API returns. Rules are frozen in signed zone config |
| front door | bad-deploy | Edge, `graft-front`, or `graft-ops` version at its rollout percentage, including a bad edge `connect()`, which reaches SSH sessions at that percentage; a bad `graft-sshd` image | `graft-ops` rolls the edge at 1%, 10%, 50%, then 100% with N and N+1 both in the deployment. An isolate or `graft-ops` submits the pre-signed swap-back and `graft-front` pins N with no API call; an `EdgePointer` pin cannot reach a TCP session, so SSH follows the deployment's split until the swap-back's deployment lands. `FrontRoll` and `OpsRoll` run at 1% for a day, then 100%, with N kept. The edge defines no member class, so its deploy resets no member. A `graft-sshd` image changes only when its `SshFront` object restarts the container on its `ContainerDay`, one site at a time, and a synthetic SSH clone gates each step [measured for M0's front, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp7.md`] |
| front door | bad-deploy | Installation-token minting. A bad `graft-ops` or a Secrets Store (beta) failure stops every Actions `GITHUB_TOKEN` and GitHub App token install-wide (`design/SHARED.md`, "Identity and tokens"), against the 99.9% job-start target | `OpsRoll` exposes 1% of mints for a day. Edge isolates whose mint errors pass a compiled ratio submit the pre-signed `OpsPointer` back to N, which the edge applies with no API call. Each `graft-ops` isolate caches the unwrapped key for at most an hour, so isolates that hold it ride out a shorter Secrets Store failure [inference]. Mint errors alarm |
| front door | bad-config | A WAF or rate rule that blocks git, a wrong location hint compiled into the edge, which misplaces new members of every site, or a wrong Spectrum app (origin, port, or hostname) or a site missing its `EDGE` binding with `{"ssh": true}` props, which cuts SSH | Zone config is code in Terraform, reviewed by two people, applied off-peak with a synthetic clone before and after, over SSH too where the install has it on; `graftyard ssh spectrum` is idempotent and rewrites the app whole. Misplaced members fail the latency check and rejoin with a new generation |
| front door | noisy-tenant | One IP, token, key, or repository flooding | WAF rate rules, per-token buckets in the identity group, per-repository admission at the leader. The edge's `connect()` sees the client's address [measured, B0-24c] but does not pass it to `graft-sshd` yet and Pro has no PROXY protocol, so SSH limits count by key and token |
| event pipeline | instance | One consumer invocation, or one `HookSequencer` object; for `PrSync`, a first attempt lost with its leader's isolate or its git cache shard, or a slow `/merge`, which holds that leader's later batches behind its one in-flight attempt for up to `FIRST_FOR` (25 s [guess]) in the leader's isolate | At-least-once redelivery. The leader runs a `PrSync` batch's first attempt in band, and Queues carries only the re-send: work the committed cursor has not passed `REDELIVER_AFTER_MS` (30 s [guess]) after it was handed out, or a first attempt that failed or ran past `FIRST_FOR`, goes to the site's queue, from the cursor, under whichever member leads. Every attempt, first or re-sent, ends at `FIRST_FOR` in the invocation that runs it, and asks no test merge in its last `COMMIT_RESERVE_MS` (10 s [guess]), shedding those it did not ask, so it commits its head moves and cursor in time however many pull requests its batch names; the leader waits `FIRST_FOR` and 2 s for its answer, so no first attempt starts while another runs. Before, an attempt past `FIRST_FOR` ran on, the leader started another every 25 s over a growing batch, and 14 at once in one stateless isolate failed together past 128 MB [measured, LM-1 round 16d, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md` on `lm1-memory`]. Dispatchers are keyed by (term, boot), so a reset leader waits on no attempt of its old self, and an attempt older than `REDELIVER_AFTER_MS` counts as lost [measured, `tests/m2/results/m2-wp5.md`, "2026-11-05": BF-24 75 of 75 within 10 s, dropped first attempts covered by the re-send at 34.7 to 38.2 s, `76abf81`]. A `PrSync` or `MergeQueue` chain whose cursor has not moved after `PARK_AFTER` (10) batches, first attempts and re-sends, is parked: no more go out from that cursor, the member stores the park so a new leadership keeps it, and `/stats` and the logs show it until an operator's replay (`/prsync {"replay": true}`) or a moved cursor clears it (`design/SHARED.md`, "Event fan-out", Isolation). The consumer acks a failed re-send, so Queues' own retries do not multiply it. Unbounded, one batch whose test merge failed every time re-ran for hours, each attempt waking its network's git cache shard [measured, `tests/am1/results/idle-shards.md`]. A cursor advances only after its work is recorded, for webhooks the `HookDelivery` rows. A reset sequencer resumes its queues from its SQLite, and an attempt left unreported for 30 s is re-sent under its GUID. A feed round lost between its export object and its `FeedState` is repaired by the next, which folds in objects stored after the state. The audit stream: a failed `send` leaves the `Audit` cursor, and the next batch sends the range again, keyed by (chain, index, slot) [inference]. |
| event pipeline | data-center | Sequencers on that host set | As instance. Queues and cursors are elsewhere. The audit stream as instance. |
| event pipeline | region | One site's queue and consumers (site A's holds the export bucket), and the delivery stores of owners homed there; `PrSync` for that site's leaders: a lost queue delays only re-sends, and a lost git cache shard holds `mergeable` and both `refs/pull/N/*` refs until leaders move [inference] | Leaders on other sites keep pushing, and cursors on the lost site resume on the new leader. A lost store is rebuilt on the owner's new home site by replaying each `Webhooks` cursor from `replay_from`, which redelivers finished deliveries under their GUIDs. Delivery history and manual redelivery for that range are lost, as the RPO table states The audit stream's ingest in a region: `Audit` batches retry from their cursors, rows arrive late and none are lost [inference]. |
| event pipeline | provider-wide | Queues or Durable Objects down (June 2025) | Cursors hold position. Webhooks, notifications, and the lifeboat feed deliver late and none are lost; with the export bucket down, the feed holds its `FeedState` and readers pull entries from followers. Basin Pipelines down holds every `Audit` cursor at its last confirmed range, so finishing waits; a site day without `--audit-stream` finishes without streaming [inference]. |
| event pipeline | control-plane | None | Queues pre-created per site, and the export bucket with its lifecycle rule by Terraform. Sequencers are created by name on first use. The audit stream is created at install; nothing at runtime calls the Pipelines API. |
| event pipeline | bad-deploy | Wrong or stopped deliveries from one site, or wrong export objects; a `PrSync` consumer that decodes no batch acks none, as M2-WP5's first deploy did [measured, `tests/m2/results/m2-wp5.md`, `3b30017`] | Payloads render from captured entry fields. The feed checks each object holds exactly its chains' entries after their cursors (`check_object`). A stopped consumer or sequencer only delays, and replay is by cursor rewind to `replay_from` Audit rows are derived from entries: a rewind of an identity chain's `AuditCursor` resends them. |
| event pipeline | bad-config | A hook owner's concurrency set too high | `HOOK_OWNER_CONCURRENCY` (50 [guess]) is compiled, never config. A site bound to the wrong stream is rebound by a site day, and a rewind resends. A missing export-bucket binding writes no feed, and its lag alarms. |
| event pipeline | noisy-tenant | One hook owner with a slow endpoint, or one owner's delivery volume; one network's test merges on its shard, which `TEST_MERGE_BUDGET` bounds [inference] | One attempt in flight per (chain, hook), a 10 s deadline, and failing hooks held to half of the owner's slots, so a slow hook delays only its own queues. Each owner has its own sequencer, so one owner's backlog never delays another's. One owner's sequencer is bounded by the 1,000 req/s soft limit per object (open question) One busy identity shard's audit rows: `SCAN_MAX` entries and 500-row sends per batch [guess]. A feed batch names at most 2,000 chains [guess], the furthest behind first. |
| actions state | instance | One `RunnerBroker` or `LogRelay` object, one `graft-runners` invocation, or one `RunnerBox` object or its container (the hosted job on it, or a start in flight) | Brokers and relays hold derived state. A broker reloads from the shards, and runners re-poll under their sessions, which are entries in the group. A relay's viewers fall back to step log blobs, and the runner's uploads lose nothing. `graft-runners` is stateless, and the next webhook or sweep repeats the demand read. Leases live in the Actions group, so a lost runner's job re-queues on lease expiry. A box reset mid-start runs its alarm again, which finds the container running and starts none; a start never placed gives up before `HOSTED_IDLE`, and its registration goes stale and is deleted [measured in memory, `workers/runners/tests/box.test.mjs`] |
| actions state | data-center | As chain groups, plus brokers, relays, and runner boxes on that host set | As chain groups. Brokers, relays, and boxes as for instance |
| actions state | region | Job state survives (group). Logs and artifacts in that site's bucket are lost, and so are that site's brokers and relays, and the runner boxes in that region | Jobs re-queue on lease expiry, and users rerun jobs whose logs were lost. New boxes place where the platform has room [inference]. The shards elect leaders elsewhere, and the edge sends polls to fresh brokers on the new leader site. Live views of running jobs fall back to blobs |
| actions state | provider-wide | Hosted runners (Containers or Durable Objects down), `graft-runners`, brokers, and relays | Self-hosted runners wait. Job leases extend only by the recorded stall (`ExtendLeases`), so idle time never keeps a dead runner's job alive |
| actions state | control-plane | Runner images cannot be pushed or prepared, and new runner containers may not start [unknown] | Running runners serve. Each `graft-runners` version's image map shipped with the version, its image prepared before the upload (an unprepared image fails every start, [measured, R23's M1 check]), and a start calls no Cloudflare API; a start the platform refuses is retried every 10 s and gives up before `HOSTED_IDLE`. Queued hosted jobs wait, up to the 24-hour queue limit. Self-hosted runners are unaffected. Brokers and relays are created by name and need no API call |
| actions state | bad-deploy | Job dispatch, brokers, or relays on one site, or a bad `graft-runners` | Pinned-runner conformance gate per site. `graft-runners` deploys by `graftyard runners upgrade` only from a signed release, at 10% for a day, then 100% [guess], and a demand read that fails starts nothing. A bad runner image at 10% reaches only the boxes that version runs: the other 90% start their own version's image, and a new version restarts no running container [measured, `tests/m0/results/container-survival.json`]. Each box logs `launched`, `runner exited within 3 s`, or `gave up` with its image, `tests/m4/wp4/sandboxstarts.py` counts them by image, and the 100% run waits for that count to show the new image launching like the old one. A bad canary's runners never poll and go stale at `HOSTED_IDLE`, and their jobs go to later runners. Rollback is the previous version at 100%, with no image roll |
| actions state | bad-config | Runner capacity per owner, or a wrong hosted label table | `decide` refuses a hosted registration past the owner's cap, 20 [guess] unless its `OrgPolicy` says otherwise. `graft-runners` starts no sandbox past `RUNNER_VCPU_STOP_PCT` of the runner account's vCPU and alarms. A job with no matching hosted label waits like a job with no runner. `runners upgrade` refuses an account without `RunnerBox` (migration tag not `v2`), and `init` creates its application only on the `durable_object` policy, which is fixed at creation; a version with no `runner` image fails each start, which gives up and logs |
| actions state | noisy-tenant | One org's 256-job matrix, or more runners in one scope than BROKER_HELD_MAX | Per-owner Actions groups (16 shards, each repository's runs on one shard) and fair queuing by runner scope. Runner containers count against the runner account's vCPU, not the install's. `RunnerBox`'s application has no instance cap, so a cold burst is bounded by the per-owner cap across passes, `RUNNER_VCPU_STOP_PCT` within a pass, and the account's vCPU limit, which refuses placement |
| release control | instance | One member of the `Release` group, or one `graft-ops` invocation. A member whose write to its site's `release_copy` failed leaves that copy stale, or missing on a new site | Quorum, as chain groups. `graft-ops` is stateless and resumes each intent after its last committed `IntentStep`, and every step is idempotent. The next apply or restore of any member on that site fills the copy from where it is. A follower site's copy of an idle group lags the leader's by one entry until the next round. A cold or idle isolate reads every site's copy and takes the longest that verifies, so a stale copy loses to the leader site's, never rolls a newer view back, and the group's view replaces it once a member answers. With the leader's site's copy stale or missing too, waiting requests serve the last pins a copy holds, or the deployment split with none, as before the copy |
| release control | data-center | As chain groups | As chain groups |
| release control | region | One member, and that site's `MemberRegistry`, its `release_copy` included | Quorum. Site days pause and resume from the group. `ReplaceMember` enumerates groups from the surviving sites' registries. A waiting isolate takes another site's copy; a down site's read runs out at `COPY_MS`, so no request waits longer, and only a pin no other site's copy holds yet is missed |
| release control | provider-wide | No rollouts | Every site keeps its running release. Isolates keep their last `Release` view and members keep site modes. An isolate that starts then takes a site's `release_copy` if a registry answers within `COPY_MS`, else serves the deployment split unpinned until a member or a copy answers. A customer-signed `StaleMode` that cannot commit is honored from any reachable `Release` member's `pending` row |
| release control | control-plane | `graft-ops` cannot deploy or read bucket location and Analytics | Rollouts wait, and a site day stuck for an hour undrains its site under the 1% lead limit. | release control | bad-deploy | A bad rollout tool, a bad `graft-ops`, a leaked drain or executor key, or a vendor key the customer no longer trusts | The vendor holds no Workers permission. `graft-ops` uploads only bundles from the release bucket whose release signature verifies, and deploys only on intents signed by a key in the latest `Keys`, bound to this install and position, before `not_after`. The group refuses a second drain while one site is drained. A leaked executor key can record progress and replace only a site drained for `ZERO_ACKS`, and it cannot pin, deploy, or restore. A customer revokes the vendor token, signs `Halt`, signs `Keys` with `vendor: None` and the next `generation`, then `Resume` with re-signed undos or `Abort { intent, target }` ("Install operations") |
| release control | bad-config | A wrong pointer, for example enabling a format early, or a rollback below an enabled format | `formats:enable` requires every site's, every spare's and residency site's canary, and every registered lifeboat's reported release, and the release-signing key and the customer key sign it. `Abort` names its target, and the group refuses and pages on any rollback, pin, or `Abort` to a release whose `ReleaseUploaded.reads` lacks an enabled format |
| release control | noisy-tenant | None | Not tenant-facing |

| vault | data-center | None | R2's in-region replication |

| vault | provider-wide | The vault and the install together | The vault is Cloudflare-only by design; the lifeboat is the copy outside Cloudflare |
| vault | control-plane | None | It needs only HTTPS to the install's edge and its own R2 |

| lifeboat | data-center | The customer's site | As instance |
| lifeboat | region | The customer's region | As instance |
| lifeboat | provider-wide | The feed stops | It serves its last state with its lag published, and resumes from its cursor on recovery |
| lifeboat | control-plane | None | It needs only outbound HTTPS to R2 and the edge. When the R2 feed is stale it pulls log tails and packs from followers through the edge's export endpoints, signed with its registered key |
| lifeboat | bad-deploy | A bad lifeboat release, or a workerd version change | Lifeboat releases trail site releases by up to a week and keep the previous binary. | lifeboat | bad-config | Wrong scope mirrored | It reads only the install's export bucket, with an R2 read-only token scoped to that bucket, and from `Tokens` keeps only `lifeboat:read` hashes |
| lifeboat | noisy-tenant | One huge org saturating the feed | Feed bandwidth caps per org |

| derived stores | data-center | Objects whose host data center failed | DO storage follower fencing keeps their SQLite (`research/cloudflare-uptime.md` finding 32), and they restart in the region |
| derived stores | region | `Inbox` objects and `SearchShard`s on that site, and rate counters created there | After `SiteLost` the edge uses the `Inbox` on the account's next record site, rebuilt from the newest snapshot (Shape, `Inbox`). | derived stores | provider-wide | Every object, with DO errors | Notifications and cross-repository lists return 503, search returns `incomplete_results`, and rate limits fail open per isolate. Nothing canonical is lost, and consumer cursors hold position |
| derived stores | control-plane | None at runtime | Objects are created by name on first use, and their classes ship in the site scripts from install |
| derived stores | bad-deploy | Wrong rows written by one release of `Notify`, `Search`, `Counts`, or a class | Every store is derived. | derived stores | bad-config | An owner's search domain or rate limits set wrong | `Domain` comes from the owner's `PlacementRecord`, and the shard's DO jurisdiction keeps resident documents inside it. Rate numbers are compiled from GitHub's documented values, on only where GHES limits are on |
| derived stores | noisy-tenant | A viral repository's stars, one huge org's search writes, or one key's request flood | `Counts` coalesces watcher rows to one Bot entry a second per repository. `Search` writes through per-owner queue shards, and a slow shard is left out with `incomplete_results`.
