| colo cache | instance | One data center's entries, or one match or put that fails; `put` resolves the same whether it stored or not [doc] | Never required: a miss, an error, or a match past `COLO_MATCH_WITHIN` reads members or checks packs as before, so the gate and `chain_open` go back to their uncached latency |
| colo cache | data-center | That colo's entries; its traffic moves to a colo whose cache is cold | As instance: about one member read per key and colo per `READ_CACHE` refills it |
| colo cache | region | Every colo's entries there | As data-center |
| colo cache | provider-wide | Every match fails or hangs | `COLO_MATCH_WITHIN` bounds each match, then the isolate cache and member reads serve as before; member reads rise to their uncached rate, which the per-isolate cache (`jti`, fanout) already holds |
| colo cache | control-plane | None at runtime | Nothing is provisioned: the API needs no binding and makes no Cloudflare API call |
| colo cache | bad-deploy | An edge version that drops the credential, repository, or record from the key, puts a refusal, or serves past `READ_CACHE` | The key and the age check are one function each, with tests that fail when an input is dropped; the key path carries the edge's key version, so a fixed version never reads a bad one's entries, which also lapse within `READ_CACHE`; `EdgePointer` undo |
| colo cache | bad-config | Cloudflare Access in front of the edge's hostname, where the API is "not currently available" [doc], or a zone setting that changes cache lifetimes | Every match misses and the edge serves as before; the age check reads the value's own time, never `max-age` |
| colo cache | noisy-tenant | One tenant's keys evict others' entries in a colo | Their gates read members as before; each put is one of 1,000 Cache API calls a request may make [doc] |

## Deployment modes

One signed artifact is used in all three modes. It holds `graft-front`, the edge, the three site script bundles, `graft-ops`, the Workflows script, and the vault Worker (Rust to WebAssembly), the git, SSH, and runner container images, web assets, the record formats and fold schemas, the operator CLI, and the lifeboat bundle (a workerd config, a pinned workerd version, and the same images). The Workflows script rolls on its own `SiteDay { Workflows }`, and the vault Worker by `graftyard vault upgrade` ("Install operations"). The vendor's existing release registry and signing transfer unchanged (`design/SHARED.md`, "Release registry and signing"), and `graft-ops` is the in-account executor that applies them.

| Component | Managed | BYOC | Self-hosted |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| chain groups | A front account holds the zone, `graft-front`, the edge, and the global `Names` and `Tokens` groups on three sites. Each cell is its own account with three site scripts (enam, weur, apac) plus residency cells, reached from the front edge over signed HTTPS | One account in the customer's org with three site scripts and three spares (default), or three site accounts plus an edge account, with member calls over signed HTTPS | Same as BYOC, in the customer's own account, with no vendor token |
| pack buckets | One R2 bucket per site in the cell's account, location checked at install | Same, in the customer's account or accounts | Same as BYOC |
| git caches | Git cache shards per site script, `standard-4`, counted from the measured working set, plus a hot pool for hot networks (journey 9) | Shards per site script, sized by tier from `cost.py`, plus a hot pool | Same as BYOC |
| front door | The front account's zone (Enterprise for 5 GB pushes), its Spectrum app for SSH, and the Data Localization Suite for EU cells. A proxy failure on a cell hostname cuts that cell's traffic | The customer's zone. HTTPS is the default; SSH is off until the install turns it on (`SSH_OPTION=on` on the edge, the `SshFront` container config on the sites, and the Spectrum app from `graftyard ssh spectrum`, which needs a token with zone Spectrum edit). One Worker app per transport per pay-as-you-go zone [doc], so port 22 is the zone's one TCP Worker app. Business allows 200 MB pushes, Enterprise 5 GB [doc] | Same as BYOC |
| event pipeline | Queues per site, one Basin Pipelines audit stream per cell bound as `AUDIT_STREAM`, and one export bucket in site A's region | Same, in the customer's account | Same as BYOC |
| actions state | Actions groups; hosted runners in a separate runner account: one `RunnerBox` class on one `durable_object` container application, plus the Sandbox classes' two applications until release M3 | Actions groups; the same `RunnerBox` application in a separate runner account, or customer runners | Same as BYOC |
| release control | Vendor signs intents; `graft-ops` executes one site per day | Vendor and customer both sign intents. The vendor holds no Workers permission and writes signed bundles into one install bucket, and `graft-ops` uploads and deploys them with a customer-created deploy token. The runtime never calls the vendor | Customer alone signs intents, which is BYOC with `Keys { vendor: None }` |
| vault | Required: a vault account per cell that the cell's tokens cannot reach, with per-org exports for tenants that want their own | Required: a vault account the customer owns, separate from the install's tokens, holding an install-scoped export signed by the customer key | Required, as BYOC |
| lifeboat | None, unless the operator decides otherwise | Recommended: two workerd hosts on the customer's network | Recommended, as BYOC |
| colo cache | On in the front account's zone, where the edge runs | On in the customer's zone; every match misses where Cloudflare Access fronts the edge's hostname | Same as BYOC; off on workerd (lifeboat, evaluation), which keeps the per-isolate cache alone |
| derived stores | `Inbox` and `SearchShard` objects in each cell; `RateCounter` objects in the front account, with GitHub's limits on | The same classes; `RateCounter` only when the install turns GHES rate limits on | Same as BYOC |

Self-hosted here means the customer operating the same artifact in its own Cloudflare account, which is how `FRAME.md` defines self-hosted. workerd on customer hardware is supported only for evaluation, under the `evaluation` flag like `Solo`, and for the lifeboat. The reasons, as read at workerd commit `ec3982b2` (`history/review/cloudflare-selfhost-walk.md` F1, F3, F5):

- `localDisk` Durable Object storage is marked "EXPERIMENTAL; SUBJECT TO BACKWARDS-INCOMPATIBLE CHANGE" (`workerd.capnp` lines 807 to 808).
- The `localDocker` container engine is "Only used for local development and testing purposes" (`workerd.capnp` lines 836 to 838).
- A workerd server runs "One thread, one loop" (`src/workerd/server/AGENTS.md` line 106), so one host's members, edge, and streams share a thread.
- workerd has no Cron Triggers, its Workflows engine is "externally-supplied", a queue binding is "implemented by the named service", and Analytics Engine needs `--experimental` (`workerd.capnp` lines 274 to 305, 476 to 479, 490).

The lifeboat runs on the same `localDisk` storage and `localDocker` engine, and a BYOC lifeboat keeps every `Identity` shard and the `lifeboat:read` token hashes on a runtime whose README says it "is not a hardened sandbox" (`history/review/cloudflare-selfhost-walk.md` F1). It is accepted there and not for production because it is read-only, holds a copy rather than canonical state, and is rebuilt on a fresh directory at every workerd version change. Token hashes are of 30-byte random secrets, so a stolen copy cannot be reversed offline [inference]. The host never receives a main-host token, because developers present `lifeboat:read` tokens that the main edge refuses. Its other credentials are a DNS token for a validation zone in a separate account and an R2 read-only token for the export bucket, so a compromised host can read the copy it already holds and issue certificates for the lifeboat name, and cannot write to the install or repoint the production zone [inference].

**What each mode requires of the customer.**

- **Managed.** Nothing.
- **BYOC and self-hosted.**
  - one install account with Workers Paid ($5), or four for account-separated sites ($20), plus a vault account and a runner account ($5 each), and for a lifeboat a validation-zone account on the Free plan ($0, https://www.cloudflare.com/plans/);
  - a zone on Pro or higher for HTTPS, the default; Enterprise for pushes over 200 MB; for the off-by-default SSH option, the zone's one TCP Spectrum Worker app on port 22, which `graftyard ssh spectrum` creates with a token that has zone Spectrum edit;
  - Containers and R2 enabled, and the container vCPU quota request filed on day 0;
  - a customer-created account-owned deploy token with exactly Workers Scripts Edit and Containers Edit on the install account (https://developers.cloudflare.com/fundamentals/api/reference/permissions/), and a separate read token with Workers R2 Storage Read and Account Analytics Read. Both live in Secrets Store and only `graft-ops` reads them. Workers Scripts Edit "Grants write access to Cloudflare Workers scripts" with nothing narrower listed (same page), so the vendor gets no Workers permission. For BYOC it gets only an R2 Object Read & Write token scoped to the install's release bucket (https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/api/tokens/). Account-owned tokens list Workers but not Containers as compatible (https://developers.cloudflare.com/fundamentals/api/get-started/account-owned-tokens/), so Milestone 0 checks container deploys with one;
  - for account separation, the acceptance that a proxy failure on the ring hostnames stalls commits.

  The install keeps serving indefinitely with the vendor unreachable, because no runtime path calls the vendor. `graft-ops` crons call the Cloudflare API with the customer's read token, and a failed call is a no-op plus an alarm.
- **Evaluation (`Solo`), any mode, or workerd on customer hardware.** One site and the signed `evaluation` flag (`Genesis.evaluation`). It gets no region-loss guarantee and no availability SLO. A Cloudflare-hosted `Solo` install moves to `Tri` by a customer-signed `LeaveEvaluation` intent, which adds the new sites, swaps the system-chain records, clears the flag, and moves `Release`, `Ids`, `Names`, `Tokens`, and then each owner, one voter site per config change (a one-site `Joint`, then `Normal`), without downtime [measured for owner moves, BF-30] (Shape, "Release group" and "Sites and placement").

### Install operations

- **Genesis.** The install comes up in this order, and sites exist before genesis.
  1. `graftyard init` mints the install id into `install.toml` and creates nothing.
  2. `graftyard sites add` and `sites add-spare` create each site script with Wrangler and the customer's deploy token, and set the trust anchor on it: the `GRAFTYARD_INSTALL` and `GRAFTYARD_CUSTOMER_KEY` variables, which hold the install id and the customer public key.
  3. `graftyard genesis` creates `graft-front`, the edge, `graft-ops`, and the Workflows script the same way, so each binds every site. It generates the drain and executor key pairs on the operator's machine, writes each private key into `graft-ops`'s Secrets Store with the operator's Wrangler login, keeps no copy, and emits an unsigned `Genesis`. The genesis carries the install id, the install's own account, the sites and spares, the system-chain placements, and the first `Keys` at generation 0 (customer, vendor or `None` for self-hosted, release-signing, drain, executor, and ring keys).
  4. The customer signs it offline with `graftyard sign`.
  5. `graftyard install --genesis` sends it to the edge, which writes it to the empty `Release` group. The group accepts only a genesis whose install id and signature match the anchor, so whoever reaches the edge first cannot plant another. Install then creates the `Names`, `Tokens`, and `Ids` groups and the `Authz(Genesis.account)` chain: every `Tokens` shard's group (3,072, three home families of 1,024), in parallel, because a sign-in code lands on a shard by its hash and a person would wait 13 to 14 s for its creation; `Names` shards stay created on first use (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Install creates every `Tokens` shard").
  6. The edge is deployed again with the signed genesis's stored bytes in hex as `GRAFTYARD_GENESIS_SIGNED`, which the deploy tool writes from the committed genesis that `install.toml`'s `genesis` digest names, never by hand. An isolate accepts it once with `accept_genesis` against its own anchor, as it accepts the bytes a `Release` member answers, so an unsigned or another install's genesis is refused and the isolate reads the `Release` view instead. The genesis never changes for an install (a restore is a new install), so the copy holds the records genesis set; it serves system chains only until the view is needed: with the variable absent or refused, or once a system chain call fails because the placement is wrong (every candidate answered that it is not joined or holds no config), the isolate drops it and reads the view, so a later entry that moves a system placement (`LeaveEvaluation`) still wins. A cold isolate that read the view first spent 1.2 to 3.8 s on its genesis, token, name, `Placement`, and `Authz` reads before its tier read at 500 agents, which set API p99 at 3.3 s on a clean run [measured, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-3.md`]. Site scripts keep reading the view, since a variable there needs a site day; an edge deploy does not. The anchor never changes after install. A later customer key reaches isolates through `Keys`.
- **First admin.** `graftyard admin bootstrap` mints a one-time sign-in link for the first site admin, who then configures SAML or OIDC. `graft-ops` has no operator route (R14), so the CLI draws the link, keeps it in a 0600 file, and writes the operator's `PutCode { kind: SignInLink }` request for the link's code shard, the row `graft-ops` would write; `POST /login/oauth/link` spends it once and commits `SignIn { method: Link }`. It answers 201 with the session id and sets no cookie, and what the link opens in a browser is not designed (`decisions/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`, F55).
- **Keys.** The release-signing key stays with the vendor's release service, is registered in every mode, and signs releases and the pre-signed half of each `FormatsEnable`. The vendor intent key signs only vendor intents. The customer intent key stays with the customer offline and signs `Keys`, genesis, `Halt`, `Resume`, `Abort`, `SiteLost`, `StaleMode`, `LifeboatAdd`, `LifeboatRemove`, `RepoRestore`, `RepoReset`, `LeaveEvaluation`, and the customer half of `FormatsEnable`. `graft-ops` holds three keys. The installation-token signing key is wrapped by a Secrets Store key and cached unwrapped per isolate for at most an hour. The drain key signs only `SiteMode`. The executor key signs only `ReleaseUploaded`, `IntentStep`, `IntentDone`, `SiteRelease`, and `ReplaceMember`. The agents account's `graft-ops` holds the agents executor key (Shape, "Release group"). A leaked executor key can record false progress, or replace a site that has been drained for a day, and cannot deploy code, pin a pointer, or restore, because those need the deploy token or a human-signed intent or undo [inference]. The group and `graft-ops` refuse any entry signed by a key absent from the latest `Keys`, any entry not bound to this install and the group's last signed digest (or, for a pre-signed undo, to the current intent), and any `Keys` that skips a generation.
- **Tools.** Terraform owns zone, DNS, buckets, queues, and WAF; the operator CLI creates and deletes the Spectrum app (`graftyard ssh spectrum --zone <zone id> --hostname <name> --edge <edge script>`, idempotent, `--delete` removes it). At install, Wrangler creates the site scripts, `graft-front`, the edge, `graft-ops`, and the Workflows script with the customer's deploy token. After that only `graft-ops` creates versions, because any new site-script version resets members and must run as a site day. The vendor's release service writes signed bundles into the install's release bucket. `graft-ops` uploads every script of a bundle as a version with the customer's deploy token once its release signature verifies, commits `ReleaseUploaded`, then deploys each version through the versions and deployments API on a signed intent. Each run reads the `Release` view and its deployments and takes the next action: a deploy that is not yet in place, a gate judged once the step has dwelt `STEP_DWELL` from the later of its last entry and its deployment's creation, then the step's `IntentStep`, so a run killed between a deploy and its entry redeploys nothing and dwells no less. A site at 100% commits `SiteRelease` before that step's `IntentStep`. A site day that finds its site drained by an intent key skips its drain and pages at undrain, since only an intent key ends that drain. A breach submits the intent's first unused undo, and a breach with none left, or a refusal the group pages on, writes a `PAGE` line to Workers Logs, which the install's alert matches. A run is `graft-ops`'s 5-minute Cron Trigger: it takes at most 32 actions, each on a view read from a member just before it, uploads first (the release bucket holds one prefix per release with `release.json`, `release.sig`, and the files it lists; each version carries the release id as its `workers/tag` and keeps the deployed version's bindings), rereads the view after a 409, and ends at a refusal. Each step's gate is `graftyard_release::gate`'s judgment of the N+1 and N members' counters; until the host reads them, it is the script's `GATE` variable, `pending` by default, so no forward step passes on its own. A gate that cannot be read for `STUCK_DAY` undrains the site under the 1% lead limit, and the step goes on once it can (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "M5-WP4").
- **Release roll.** `graftyard release roll R` waits for R's `ReleaseUploaded`, then signs and submits the first intent below that has not finished. It submits one at a time, because the group refuses a new intent while one is current and each signature binds the latest digest. The operator reruns it, or the vendor's release service runs it, after each `IntentDone`. It skips an intent whose script or images R leaves unchanged. It reads the group's entries from a member's `/view` answers (`--entries`), checks each against the operator's trust anchor, and signs on the last digest. A pinned script's 100% step keeps N in the deployment at 0%, so its pre-signed pointer back to N still applies after the roll: an override reaches a version only if the deployment holds it (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/versions-and-deployments/gradual-deployments/).
  1. `SiteDay { Site(s), R }` for each member site, one a day. Only a passed day lets the next start.
  2. `SiteDay` for each spare and residency site, behind its canary group.
  3. `SiteDay { Workflows, R }`, which skips drain and undrain and starts only while no `ReplaceMember` or `RestorePlacement` instance runs.
  4. `EdgeRoll`, then `OpsRoll`, then `FrontRoll`, then `McpRoll`. The agents account runs `ControllerRoll` (`design/AGENT-LAYER.md`).
  5. `ContainerDay { s, R }` for each site whose images changed, one a day and never on a code day.

  Each intent carries its undos: a `Rollback` to N for a day target, and `EdgePointer`, `OpsPointer`, or `McpPointer` to N for a pinned script. `ContainerDay` carries a `Rollback { target: Containers(s), release: N }`, which shards apply with no API call. `FrontRoll` carries none. On its breach `graft-ops` returns the script to N through the API under the same intent, so a control-plane outage freezes it. `graftyard formats enable` comes after the roll, once every site, canary, and lifeboat reports R: the group refuses, without a page, a `FormatsEnable` while any site of the view, spares included, has no `SiteRelease` or any registered lifeboat no `LifeboatDecoder` naming a release whose `reads` holds the format (SHARED rule 3).
- **Site day.** Every operation that creates a new site-script version (code, secret, variable, binding, container config) runs as drain, deploy at 1%, 10%, 50%, then 100%, then undrain. Each step and the 1% day gate on the N+1 members' apply lag, apply and verify exceptions, and ack rate against the same site's N members, plus zero `STEP_DOWN` trips on N+1 leaders. A breach runs the release intent's pre-signed `Rollback { target, release: N }` with no human signature, unless N cannot read an enabled format, in which case the group refuses it and pages. Every release adds one site day per spare and residency site after the member sites, gated by that site's canary group replaying a corpus of current formats, and a spare keeps its images until that day. Each version's image map names its release's git image and the previous release's, each prepared (`POST /containers/image-preparations`) before the upload, since an unprepared image fails every start [measured, R23], and a new version restarts no running container, so a container image change reaches shards on a different day from member code, through `ContainerDay`. Rotatable secrets live in Secrets Store, read at runtime with `get()` (https://developers.cloudflare.com/secrets-store/integrations/workers/), so rotating one creates no script version. The steps and gates use these compiled constants, all [guess] until Milestone 0 and the first site days measure them.

  | Constant | Value | Use |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `ROLL_STEPS`, `SiteDay` | drain, 1%, 10%, 50%, 100%, undrain, first day (steps 0 to 6) | `IntentStep.step` for a site; a `Workflows` day runs steps 1 to 4 |
  | `ROLL_STEPS`, `EdgeRoll` | 1%, 10%, 50%, 100% (steps 0 to 3) | Edge deployment percentages, N and N+1 both kept |
  | `ROLL_STEPS`, `FrontRoll`, `OpsRoll`, `McpRoll`, `ControllerRoll` | 1% for `FIRST_DAY`, then 100% (steps 0 and 1) | N kept in the deployment |
  | `ROLL_STEPS`, `ContainerDay` | 10%, 50%, 100% of the site's shard objects, by each object's weight on `day#<site>` so each step covers the last, each restarting onto the new image (steps 0 to 2) | Image rollout on its own day, read by shards from the `Release` view |
  | `STEP_DWELL` | 2 h at each of 1%, 10%, and 50% | Least time before a step's gate is judged |
  | `STEP_MIN_ENTRIES` | 1,000 entries applied by N+1 members in the step | Below it the step dwells up to 2 x `STEP_DWELL`, then halts and pages |
  | `FIRST_DAY` | 24 h, with the site leading 1% of its home groups | The day gate |
  | `GATE_APPLY_LAG` | At most 2% of N+1's applied entries slower than N's p99 on the same site, and at most 1% slower than 5 s with or without N; at 100% and on the first day N's p99 is from the intent's 1, 10 and 50% steps. Relative because a drained site's followers apply about 1 s late on quiet chains, R29's commit heartbeat, so the old absolute 500 ms cap breached every drained day [measured, BF-39 hosted, replayed: healthy N+1 0.07 to 1.01% over N's p99, +300 ms 47 to 50%] (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`gate-relative`") | Breach |
  | `GATE_EXCEPTIONS` | 0 apply or verify exceptions on N+1 | Breach |
  | `GATE_ACK_RATE` | N+1 ack rate at most 0.1 points below N's | Breach |
  | `GATE_STEP_DOWN` | 0 `STEP_DOWN` trips on N+1 leaders | Breach |
  | `GATE_ERRORS` | N+1 5xx rate at most N's plus 0.1 points | Breach for edge, front, ops, MCP, and controller rolls |
  | `IDENTITY_RATIO` | 3 identity reads per edge request | An edge isolate above it submits the `EdgePointer` undo |
  | `MINT_ERROR_RATIO` | 1% of installation-token mints | An edge isolate above it submits the `OpsPointer` undo |
  | `STUCK_DAY` | 1 h | A site day that cannot advance undrains under the 1% lead limit |
  | `DRAIN_BURN` | Missed `CALL_DEADLINE` calls at 5 x the allowed 1 in 100 for 5 min, from both other sites, each report at least `MIN_CALLS` (300) calls [measured, one install: BF-39's steady load at most 1.0 x, its gray fault 9.6 to 27.5 x] (OPEN-QUESTIONS R41) | Automatic drain by the drain key (journey 5) |
  | `UNDRAIN_BURN`, `UNDRAIN_AFTER` | Burn under 2 x for 15 min, from both other sites [measured, one install: calm 15 min 40 s after BF-39's fault lifted] (OPEN-QUESTIONS R41) | Automatic undrain by the drain key |
  | `ZERO_ACKS` | 24 h drained with zero acks in both other sites' `SiteHealth` | An executor-signed `ReplaceMember` |
- **Site loss.** `ReplaceMember { lost, spare }` is signed only by the executor key. `decide` accepts it in two cases. Either a customer-signed `SiteLost { site: lost }` has committed, or `lost` has been drained for `ZERO_ACKS` by an entry at least that old, and `graft-ops` read zero acks from it in both other sites' `SiteHealth` over bindings before it signed. `graft-ops` then starts one Workflow instance per group, enumerated from the surviving sites' `MemberRegistry` rows that name `lost` (Shape, "Release group"). Each instance moves its group onto `spare` through a `Joint` config (journey 5). A site has recovered when both other sites' `SiteHealth` show it under `UNDRAIN_BURN` for 24 h and its canary group reports a release that reads every enabled format. The drain key then undrains it. `RestorePlacement { site }`, signed by the vendor or customer intent key and refused while `site` is drained, starts the instances that move groups whose `home` is `site` back.
- **Placement failures.** A failed latency check, including the near bound, is retried with a new member generation (`{chain}#{generation+1}#{kind}{slot}`, where `generation` is the highest its group has named), because only the first `get()` respects a hint. Votes and acks count by site, so a replacement on the same site, a retry or `ReplaceMember`, takes two config changes, `Degraded { lost }` then `Normal` with the new member [measured, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp7.md`, BF-11]. A re-placement runs every try first, while the group stays `Normal`: each try joins, measures its peers, and is checked by `Placement::new`, and none changes a config. Only once a member is chosen does the leader commit `Degraded { lost }` and then `Normal` back to back, after asking again that every voter the `Degraded` config needs is caught up; a voter behind at that point ends the re-placement with no config change, and so does a search in which no try is nearer than the member it would replace. So the group is `Degraded` for one config round, not for the whole search: on B0-30's short run a create's placement held a just-built group `Degraded` for 535 s over 8 tries [measured, `tests/am1/results/b030-split.md`, "A `/place` on the newly built group"]. Every config change, a re-placement's two, a `/move` step, and `/learners`, is built from the config committed when it is proposed and carries that config's learners, because learners belong to sites and slots, not to the voter being replaced. The leader appends it only while the committed config is still the one it was built from, checked in the same turn as the append, so a change built from an older read is refused instead of undoing the changes committed since. A re-placement whose voters or home moved during its tries ends with no config change. On B0-32 m3fr13 a create's background placement started before `/learners`, committed `Degraded` and `Normal` after it from its first read, and dropped all 8 learners [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "Rows m3fr13"]. The replaced member keeps answering with its last committed config and marks every reply's `meta` `removed`; the placement's answer and log name it as `old`, and a test install's teardown finds it by generation. A config change that names a new member at an address, a voter's site or a learner's slot address, resets everything the leader holds for that address and ignores answers to rounds it sent the old member there; reset for voters only, each new learner generation `/learners` placed on the same slots kept the last one's matched index, was probed above its empty log on every call, and never caught up [measured, B0-33, `tests/m0/results/learner-reset.md`]. A misplaced bucket outside a jurisdiction is deleted while still empty and recreated under a new name, and the site config change runs as a site day. Inside an R2 jurisdiction the location hint is ignored: `eu` buckets hinted weur or oc landed in EEUR and a `us` bucket hinted wnam in ENAM [measured, `tests/m1/results/m1-cli.md`], so a new name cannot fix it (R16).
- **Oceania.** An `oc` site's start check accepts a container in OC or APAC, because OC containers have limited capacity and "cannot be used exclusively" (https://developers.cloudflare.com/containers/concepts/placement/). It does not count as a separate region from apac for container placement.
- **Revocation runbook.** The customer revokes the vendor's release-bucket token, submits `Halt { intent }` signed by the customer key through `POST /api/graftyard/v1/release/entries`, then signs `Keys` with `vendor: None` at the next generation. `graft-ops` stops at the current step, and from then the group and `graft-ops` refuse every vendor-signed entry, including a replay of any earlier entry, because each binds an older digest or generation. The halted intent's pre-signed undos still verify against the `Keys` of the intent's index, so a gate breach while halted still rolls back with no human signature. The customer then signs `Resume { intent, undo }`, with every undo re-signed by the customer key, or `Abort { intent, target }`, and `graft-ops` executes either with the customer's deploy token. `Abort` moves each site the intent touched, on its own site day, and each script it rolled to `target`. The group refuses and pages an `Abort` whose `target` cannot read an enabled format, so the customer names a newer one.
- **Rotation runbook.** Each Secrets Store secret has two slots, both bound at install. The customer, or a site admin for App credentials, writes the new value into the inactive slot with their own Wrangler login, because no token `graft-ops` holds can write Secrets Store. The vendor or customer intent key then signs `Rotate { secret, slot }`, and the customer alone in self-hosted. `graft-ops` confirms one call with the new value, switches to it, and commits `IntentDone`. The old value is revoked at its issuer after 24 h.

  | Secret | Who acts | New value from | Reaches Secrets Store by | `Rotate` makes `graft-ops` |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `DeployToken` | Customer | A new account-owned token in the dashboard | The customer's Wrangler login | List the install's scripts with it, then switch |
  | `ReadToken` | Customer | As `DeployToken` | As `DeployToken` | Read one bucket's `location` with it, then switch |
  | `MintWrapKey` (holds the wrap key and the wrapped installation-token signing key: a JSON object `{wrap, kid, nonce, wrapped}`, hex fields, with the 32-byte Ed25519 seed sealed under the 32-byte wrap key by AES-256-GCM with the 12-byte nonce, as `graft-ops` reads it and `mint-key rotate` must write it [inference: `workers/ops/src/lib.rs`, `MintWrapKey`]) | Customer | `graftyard mint-key rotate` on the operator's machine | The customer's Wrangler login | Commit `TokensEvent::VerifyingKey` for the new key, mint with it, and retire the old `kid` after the longest token lifetime |
  | `SshHostKeys` | Customer | The host-key script in `deploy/ssh-front/` on the operator's machine (one Ed25519, one ECDSA P-256, one RSA 3072 key) | The customer's Wrangler login, into `SSH_HOST_KEY_A` or `SSH_HOST_KEY_B` | Publish both slots at `/api/graftyard/v1/meta/ssh` and have each `SshFront` restart its container one site at a time, so `graft-sshd` announces both slots for 30 days [guess], then make the new slot active |
  | `McpAppKey`, `McpClientSecret`, `InsightsAppKey` | Site admin | `graftyard apps rotate`, which adds a new key or secret to the App | The admin's Wrangler login, install account | Switch, then remove the old key or secret from the App after 24 h |
  | `AgentsAppKey`, `WorkspaceAppKey` | Site admin | `graftyard agents rotate` | The admin's Wrangler login, agents account | The agents account's `graft-ops` switches and removes the old key after 24 h |
  | Agents account `DeployToken` | Customer | As `DeployToken`, in the agents account | As `DeployToken` | The agents account's `graft-ops` switches |
  | Vendor release-bucket tokens (install and agents accounts) | Customer | A new R2 token scoped to the bucket | Never. The customer hands it to the vendor | No `Rotate`. The old one is revoked after the vendor confirms a write |
  | Export token | Customer | `graftyard vault token` signs new `ExportClaims` offline | Never. It goes to the vault Worker's secret under the vault-account token | No `Rotate`. The old `jti` is revoked after the vault's next pull succeeds |
  | Lifeboat validation-zone DNS token and R2 read token | Customer | New tokens in those accounts | Never. They go to the lifeboat host | No `Rotate` |

  The customer, vendor, release-signing, drain, executor, agents executor, and ring keys rotate by a new `Keys` entry. A new drain or executor private key goes into the inactive slot first, and `graft-ops` switches when the `Keys` that names it commits. A ring key's new public key enters `Keys` before its site switches to it. A lifeboat key rotates by `LifeboatRemove` and `graftyard lifeboat init`, and a developer's `lifeboat:read` token by re-running `enroll`, which revokes the old one.
- **Vault.** `graftyard vault init` creates the vault Worker in the vault account from the `vault/` folder of a release whose `release.json` verifies under `Keys.release` (`crates/release`, `check_vault`), under a vault-account token that the customer holds, and gives it an install-scoped export token (Shape, "Lifeboat feed", `ExportClaims`). The Worker pulls `export/batches`, then each moved chain's entries and the packs and snapshots they name, from the install's edge over HTTPS, and writes them to R2 under bucket lock rules of 35 days on `feed/`, `entries/`, `snapshot/`, and `pack/`, create-only, each pack and snapshot checked against its entry's checksum before it exists; R2 checks a lock rule before `If-None-Match`, so a create-only put to a held key returns 409 `ObjectLockedByBucketPolicy`, not 412 [doc: Cloudflare R2 bucket locks, Notes], and either refusal is judged by a HEAD of the key's size and stored SHA-256, never taken as stored; its cursors live in an unlocked `state/vault`, written compare-and-swap. A chain first carried past index 1 is filled from the entries endpoint, or from a snapshot once the range is archived (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, `vault-worker`). It deletes a pack no sooner than `RESTORE_WINDOW` after the install's GC deletes it, counted from the first probe that finds it gone; a 410 is never a GC. The export covers every `ChainId`, including `Release`, `Names`, `Tokens`, `Identity`, and user-owned repositories. Managed tenants keep per-org tokens for their own exports. A vault is required for managed, BYOC, and self-hosted. `graftyard vault upgrade --release R` verifies R's release signature on the operator's machine and deploys R's vault Worker under the same vault-account token, keeping its bindings and secret, because no install token reaches the vault account. The lock stops a bad vault release, not a holder of the vault-account token, who can remove the rules and then delete every object [doc: Cloudflare R2 bucket locks].
- **Whole-install restore.** From the vault after an account compromise or destructive deploy, or from a lifeboat export after a Cloudflare-wide data loss:
  1. Name a cutoff minute, chosen by the operator before the compromise, or the source cursor after a Cloudflare-wide loss. Create a new install account and sites from the signed artifact, with a customer-signed genesis that names the restore source and the cutoff.
  2. For each chain, create members from the source's newest snapshot at or before the cutoff plus its log tail up to the cutoff, and check the `prev` digest chain end to end.
  3. Copy every referenced pack to two site buckets and verify each `PackRef.checksum`. A chain with a missing pack stays read-only and is listed for the operator.
  4. Re-apply, from the vault log, every revocation-class entry newer than the cutoff: token, `jti`, and SSH key revocations, uninstalls, member removals, `Keys`, and `LifeboatRemove`. The new genesis `Keys` replaces the restored `Release` group's keys, and the installation-token signing key is rotated with every old `kid` dropped, so no credential revoked or stolen after the cutoff comes back.
  5. Open writes only when every chain's index equals its index at the cutoff plus the re-applied entries. The RPO is the time from the cutoff to the loss, which the restore report states. Because `Names`, `Tokens`, `Identity`, and user chains are in the export, names resolve and PATs, SSH keys, and user-owned repositories work after the restore.
- **Lifeboat.**
  - `graft-blob` writes a temp file, fsyncs it, links it to the final key (failing if the key exists), and fsyncs the directory before any 200. Reads verify `PackRef.checksum`, and a weekly scrub repairs from a sibling copy. Lifeboat hosts must use disks that honor cache flushes, which the install guide states as a host requirement.
  - The workerd version is pinned in the signed artifact. A workerd version change rebuilds the lifeboat on a fresh directory. `uniqueKey` and the key modifier are backed-up state, because losing them may make stored data "difficult or impossible to recover" (`workerd.capnp` lines 783 to 788).
  - `graftyard lifeboat init` generates the host's own key and registers it with `LifeboatAdd`, signed by the customer key. `LifeboatDecoder`, which the host sends to `POST /api/graftyard/v1/release/entries` when its decoder changes and at least daily, and the host's tail and pack pulls through the export endpoints are accepted only under that key, which signs nothing else. `formats:enable` waits for every registered lifeboat. `LifeboatRemove { host }`, signed by the customer key, drops a dead or retired one, and an alarm fires when a registered lifeboat has sent no `LifeboatDecoder` for 7 days. A lifeboat that meets an undecodable entry halts and alarms, and never skips.
  - `graftyard lifeboat enroll` calls `POST /api/graftyard/v1/lifeboat/tokens` with the developer's own token, which mints a per-developer `lifeboat:read` token that the main edge refuses (Shape, "Lifeboat feed"), and writes it into gh's hosts file and a git credential helper for the lifeboat host. Lifeboat reads are opt-in per client.
  - A BYOC lifeboat holds every `Identity` shard and, from `Tokens`, only the hashes of `lifeboat:read` tokens, so it never receives or stores a main-host token. A managed lifeboat, if the operator ever approves one, would accept only tokens minted for it, by org.
  - `_acme-challenge.lifeboat.acme.example` is a CNAME to a validation zone in a separate account, which ACME DNS-01 allows (https://letsencrypt.org/docs/challenge-types/). The host holds a DNS Write token for that zone only, because DNS permissions are zone-wide with nothing narrower (https://developers.cloudflare.com/fundamentals/api/reference/permissions/), and renews 30 days before expiry. It reads the export bucket with an R2 Object Read only token scoped to that bucket (https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/api/tokens/).
  - Lag is typically about 30 s and is published. The lifeboat serves its cursor age in `/api/v3/meta` and an `X-Graftyard-Lifeboat-Lag` header, alarms past 5 minutes, and pulls log tails from followers through the edge's export endpoints when the R2 feed is stale.

## Cost

Run `python3 scripts/cost.py` from the repository root. It uses `TIERS` and `ACTIVITY` from `scripts/cost_model.py` and the `cf.*` prices in `research/prices.json`.

Every per-operation constant is a [guess] until Milestone 0, except git CPU. Merges (0.05 vCPU-s), push receive (0.2 vCPU-s), and composed clone-cache misses (0.75 vCPU-s, plus one stock depth-1 build of 45 vCPU-s per network every 20 tip moves) were measured on torvalds/linux [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-34]. `TIERS` has no repository size per tier, so every network's base renewal is priced at the 6 GB network's cost, an upper bound. Rows written per entry are priced by family and kind, as local workerd counts them for the member's own statements; rerun them with `bash tests/entryrows/run.sh` (`tests/entryrows/README.md`) [measured]: a repository entry writes 6 to 23 fold rows per member (a tier table's row counts twice, once for its table and once for its key's index; a `Refs` push 10, a `Comment` 16), plus 6.3 for `request`, `log`, and `raft_state` and 2 later deletes; an Actions, Identity, or Tokens entry writes 8.3 in all, its fold being one row. K12's hosted `DefineLabel` agrees: 19 on the leader, 15 on followers [measured, `graft-m3f` site day m3frs]. The other constants are requests per git operation, CPU per request, container sizes, a clone-cache hit rate of 70%, the daily working set, the leader split, one site loss a year, and the `Inbox` fan-out, read share, and size. Free allowances are applied per account. The daily fold audit refolds a repository chain only if it wrote its fold since its last matching audit (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "audit-moved"), and a refold writes no tier table: it stages each snapshot as 256 KB pages and applies the log tail over them, writing each page and each changed row once and deleting them at its end ("`refold-stream`"). At 620 B of member SQLite per fold row (`ROW_KB`, an upper bound on bytes, so a lower bound on rows) and 65 B of snapshot per row [measured, K10], and a guessed 30% of tier rows in moved chains a day, the line is $0.01, $2.26, and $9.29 a month at the three tiers (rows written and read, R2 range reads, and member duration), $31 at 2,000 with every chain moved (`AUDIT_MOVED_SHARE` 1), and $0.10, $8.80, and $17.37 at the 65 B floor. Restoring into scratch tables, as the audit did before, wrote 2 rows per tier row refolded and cost $421, $2,517, and $3,110. The weekly live-row check reads every voter's tier rows once a week at 1,200 rows/s [measured, `tests/m2/results/m2-wp1.md`] and writes only `snap_undo` copies: $0.76, $4.31, and $35.52 a month, $7, $41, and $339 at the 65 B floor ("`live-check`"). Learners are priced in `scripts/cost_agents.py`: the largest network's REST and GraphQL reads are whole-tier dumps, which `GroupLoad` counts 50 times each, one learner serving about 20 a second [measured, `tests/am1/results/learner-dumps.md`], so its group places 0, 2, and 11 learners at the three tiers, $0, $16, and $237 a month, mostly the entries each learner applies; each holds about 8.3 GB of both tiers and is active 9 hours a day [guess].

The plan line uses Pro list at every tier (`FRAME.md`, "Operator decision, 2026-11-19, Pro plan"), with its 100 MB push body limit; Business is an install option outside the tables. Managed and pushes over 200 MB need an Enterprise contract on top of the table, which has no list price. Actions runner compute is not included either, the same as in `scripts/cost_model.py`. The tables include the vault and runner accounts, three spare sites, `ReplaceMember`, user records (stars, watches, follows, and notification state) as `Identity` entries, and one `Inbox` per account.

| Developers | Default, one account (`Tri`) | Account-separated sites (`Tri`, four accounts) | `Solo` (evaluation) | Git in Artifacts, one DO per repository |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 50 | $238 | $248 | $133 | $81 |
| 300 | $1,016 | $929 | $599 | $517 |
| 2,000 | $9,335 | $9,232 | $5,395 | $4,602 |

Git cache shards come from a guessed daily working set, the bytes of networks fetched at least once a day, which Milestone 0 measures. Each shard keeps 4 GB of its disk for scratch, and each network is warm on two sites.

| Developers | Working set | Instance | Shards per site | Warm instances | Hot pool per site | Capped vCPU (start cap x 3 sites; SSH fronts only where SSH is on, so none here) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 50 | 9 GB (30% of git) | `standard-2` | 2 | 4 | 7 `standard-2` | 27.0 |
| 300 | 60 GB (20%) | `standard-3` | 5 | 10 | 7 `standard-3` | 72.0 |
| 2,000 | 400 GB (10%) | `standard-4` | 25 | 50 | 7 `standard-4` | 384.0 of 15,000 (39.1x), alarm at 10,500 |

The hot pool counts in each site's start cap. Its idle shards keep hot networks warm, so a step up in k needs no rehydrate (Shape, `HOT_POOL`). The tables price that as the pool of 7 per site running on both warm sites for a 9-hour working day, warm only for networks hot in the last hour, at the tier's instance size, memory and disk with no active vCPU [guess: 8 working hours plus the hour]. Only the 2,000-developer tier has a hot network: at 50 and 300 developers the largest network's git CPU, 0.05 and 0.32 vCPU (`scripts/cost_agents.py`), never reaches `fanout`'s up line, so the pool stays reserved there and bills nothing. The line adds $433 a month to the default at 2,000 developers and $217 to `Solo`. The agent layer prices any extra warm shards it holds.

Container egress is priced by each site's region, at $0.025 per GB in North America and Europe with 1 TB included and $0.04 elsewhere with 500 GB included (https://developers.cloudflare.com/containers/platform/pricing/), and split by where leaders sit. Leaders sit on each group's home site, so the default assumes an NA-heavy split of 50% enam, 30% weur, and 20% apac [guess; apac priced at the "elsewhere" rate, inference]. An APAC-led install (15%, 5%, 80%) pays $137 instead of $92 at 300 developers, and $2,271 instead of $1,708 at 2,000, for a total of $9,894.

Account separation costs $9 more at 50 developers. At 300 it costs $88 less and at 2,000 $103 less, because three site accounts each get their own free Durable Object, R2, and Containers allowances, which outweigh three more subscriptions and the site Worker requests ($134 a month against the default's $48 at 2,000, `--detail large separate`).

At 2,000 developers the largest lines of the default are:

| Line | Per month |
| --- | --- |
| Container memory for 50 warm `standard-4` shards | $3,942 |
| Container egress by site region and leader split | $1,708 |
| R2 storage (three pack copies, clone cache, Actions blobs) | $816 |
| Hot pool kept warm for hot networks, 14 `standard-4` for 9 hours a day | $433 |
| DO SQLite storage (three member copies of 700 GB) | $422 |
| Durable Objects SQLite rows written (by family and kind, K12) | $420 |
| Vault account (plan, a locked copy of packs, snapshots, and feed) | $160 |
| `ReplaceMember` and `RestorePlacement`, one site loss a year amortized | $63 |
| `Inbox` Durable Objects (notifications, feeds, cross-repository lists) | $32 |
| Runner account (Workers Paid only) | $5 |
| Agent layer on top of the default, from `scripts/cost_agents.py` (not in the totals above) | $2,939 |
| Spare sites (canary operations on three empty buckets) | under $1 |
| Spectrum SSH data, SSH fronts, and any Spectrum Worker-app fee: SSH is off by default per install, so no tier here runs it (`SSH_PLANS` in `scripts/cost.py`); `--ssh-option` prices it below | $0 in the table |

Agents add $22, $329, and $2,939 a month at 50, 300, and 2,000 developers on top of the default, 9%, 32%, and 31% (`python3 scripts/cost_agents.py`); the largest network places no learners on cost grounds, because its API reads are narrow reads counted once in `GroupLoad`, not 1 MB whole-tier reads counted 50 times (K9, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads.md`). Monthly lines use 5 pushes a task. With git CPU measured on torvalds/linux and composed clone-cache misses, `fanout` keeps the largest network at k = 1 at 2,000 developers, so the layer adds no extra warm shards ($0), and test merges on agent head pushes cost $1. The conflict predictor, at a measured 0.37 vCPU-s a pair (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp16.md`, L0-05), costs $54. They include agent CI egress with every agent CI checkout missing the clone cache, the `Inbox` line grown by agent activity, and $342 as an upper bound for members held active by activity feed long-polls. At journey 9's push every 2 minutes k is also 1, with 0 extra shards ($0 a month), by git CPU. The validate lock that held one network to about 2 pushes/s hosted is gone: one shard now validates 39 a second on 1 vCPU, about 9 times journey 9's 4.17/s [measured, container bench, `tests/am1/results/fanout.md`]. Hosted, at 100 agents every push landed with k = 1 (commit p99 885 ms), and the 500-agent rows failed on the read gate, not on validates, so the $0 line stands but is not yet shown hosted at 500 agents; LM-1's load rows are being rerun. Sandbox compute and model tokens for Graftyard-run agents, $2,827 and $182,400 a month at 2,000 developers with a guessed $2 of tokens a task, sit outside this comparison as runner compute does, and opt-in Code Mode is excluded. Graftyard-run agents are offered and off until an org enables them (`FRAME.md`, agent-layer answer 1). Line items are in `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "Cost".

The quorum costs $3,940 a month over `Solo` at 2,000 developers, 73% more, most of it the second warm copy of every shard. At 50 developers it costs $105 more. That buys RPO zero and an RTO of about 11.5 s per request for the loss of any one region, continent, or site release, and a vault copy outside the install account. `Solo` costs $133, $599, and $5,395 a month at the three tiers. It has one member per chain, one pack bucket, one warm copy of each shard on one site, and a runner account, and it carries no vault. Its RPO is zero for a machine or data center and not covered for a region, because a write is confirmed by followers in the object's own region (`tests/m0/results/wp11.md`, (b)) [measured]. Its RTO is Cloudflare's restart, or the region's return. It carries no availability SLO, so it is not a reliable setup.

The SSH option is off by default and outside every total above. Turning it on runs the 3 `basic` SSH front containers and sends SSH bytes (20% of developer git bytes [guess]) through Spectrum to the edge and the fronts. Spectrum does not publish a price for Worker-origin apps, so the script prices their data as Spectrum's SSH app: 5 GB a month on Pro (10 GB on Business), then $1 per GB [unverified for Worker-origin apps], plus the bytes leaving the containers (`python3 scripts/cost.py --ssh-option`). SSH option on Pro: +$33 a month at 50 developers, +$114 at 300, and +$664 at 2,000; the containers are $22 at each tier, Spectrum SSH data $11, $89, and $624, and egress $0, $3, and $18.

**Cheapest reliable setup for 50 developers: $238 a month** (`--detail small design`). It is the default itself. It runs one install account with three site scripts in three hinted regions, which keeps the quorum across regions and release days, plus the vault and runner accounts. It uses the Pro plan, with 100 MB pushes over HTTPS, the default transport; SSH, through a Spectrum Worker app on the same Pro zone, is off by default and adds $33 a month when on. It has four `standard-2` shards, two on each of two sites.

66% of that bill is container memory. A shard slept overnight would halve that line, but it would add 1 to 3 s cold starts plus rehydration to the first morning fetch (`research/cloudflare.md`, Containers), and it would need new instance starts, which a control-plane outage may block. I keep them warm.

## Build order from zero

Durations are [guess]es for a team of six.

### Milestone 0

About six weeks on one throwaway account with three site scripts, before any git code. Every row runs in that one account: no second account and no trial account or zone (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Milestone 0 runs in one account"). The account-separated row compares a public HTTPS hop between Workers of the account with a binding, and the plan-dependent rows run on the plans of zones added to the same account. Any fail value sends the design back.

| Measurement | Pass | Fail |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Commit p99 on `Tri`, leader enam, 1 KB commands, 100 commands/s, member calls through cross-script DO bindings | At most 600 ms (operator decision, 2026-10-28) | Over 1 s |
| Group commit on one group, one entry in flight | At least 300 commands/s at commit p99 at most 600 ms | Under 100 commands/s |
| Write and read RTO, with one site script's member class failing every call (simulated region loss) under 100 writes/s, run once with every client in one colo and once with calls to that class blackholed (hung, not failing) | New leader committing and serving `fresh` within 13 s at p99 in both runs | Over 60 s in either run |
| Site deploy under 100 writes/s, with groups led from every site and clients on the leaders' continents: drain, deploy at 1%, 10%, 50%, 100%, undrain | Zero failed writes, commit p99 at most 1 s for clients on the leader's continent | Any failed write |
| Follower regression: a release whose N+1 followers throw while applying one event kind | The apply gate stops it at 1% and `graft-ops` runs the pre-signed rollback, with no failed write and no human signature | It reaches 10%, or any write fails |
| Gray site, with 30% errors injected on one site's member calls, then separately on its storage writes | Every group led there moves within 20 s and changes leader at most twice in an hour | Any group slower, or more than two changes |
| Gray site C during B's site day at 10%, with automatic drain on | Callers on A and B report C, `graft-ops` undrains B under the 1% lead limit and drains C. Every group led on C that served a request or committed an entry in the 50 s before the drain moves within 20 s of it, and every other group led on C moves within 20 s of its next request | Two sites drained at once, or any group on C slower |
| `verify` refusal by an older follower: release N+1 on site A writes a shape N cannot read | Every group A led moves to an undrained follower holding its last index; other writes keep committing; A stays non-leader | Any group without a leader for more than one lease |
| Rolling site refuses: N+1 followers on B refuse valid N entries at 10% of a site day | No leader steps down, B is marked unable to follow, an alarm fires, and the site day halts and rolls back | Any group without a leader for more than one lease |
| Two-site residual: A and B on N+1 commit a shape C on N cannot read | Each group keeps its leader and commits on A and B, C is marked unable to follow, and the site day halts | Any group without a leader for more than one lease |
| Linearizability: 48 hours of Porcupine-checked histories while member calls to one site are dropped at random, members are reset, and leadership transfers, some abandoned mid-way | Zero violations, zero lost acknowledged entries | Any one |
| Member placement: 1,000 members created with each site class's hint, median of 20 binding round trips to each other voter | At least 95% of voter pairs at 30 ms or more and consistent with the hinted regions | Under 80% |
| Bucket placement: 20 buckets per hint, `location` from the get-bucket API against a latency probe | 100% agree | The API reports the hint, not the placement |
| Daily working set of git cache shards per tier, in bytes, on a synthetic replay | Recorded; `cost.py` shard counts updated | None. The result sets the shard count |
| Idle cost: 100,000 idle groups for 24 hours across a UTC midnight, with the daily `Rtt` recheck shortened to hourly, beside 1,000 control groups that serve one request an hour | Zero duration billed and zero `Rtt` checks for idle groups, while the control groups show both | Any duration billed, or any `Rtt` check, for idle groups, or a control group that shows neither |
| Account-owned deploy token with Workers Scripts Edit and Containers Edit runs a deploy with a container image | Deploys | Fails, which moves the deploy token to a user token held by the customer |
| Account-separated option: member RPC over signed HTTPS through the site Worker, against a cross-script binding | p50 overhead at most 20 ms | Over 60 ms, which withdraws the option |
| Learners: 16 learners serving 16,000 reads/s on one group | Leader under 700 req/s, learner read p99 at most 1 s | Leader returns `overloaded` |
| Container starts with the control plane out of reach: 100 new instances started with every account API token revoked | 100 of 100 start | Any fails; the control-plane row stays `contained` either way and warm shards stay sized for a day |
| Push size: 2 GB push through the edge on Enterprise, teed to two buckets and a container; on Pro and Business, a push over the edge cap | Enterprise completes with Worker memory under 100 MB; Pro and Business print the edge's `report-status` naming Enterprise or splitting the push | Fails, needs buffering, or shows a bare 413 |
| Chunked push on Pro, Business, and Enterprise | Recorded either way | None. The result sets each plan's stated cap |
| Container rehydration of a 5 GB network from R2 | At most 120 s | Over 600 s |
| Clone cache: 33 CI clones/s of a monorepo whose tip moves 10 times a minute | Hit rate at least 95%, time to first byte p99 at most 500 ms | Under 80% |
| SSH. History: B0-24a, the retired `cloudflared access ssh` option to cloudflared in three containers with one killed every 10 minutes, served 99.886% of 2,637 sessions over 2.2 h on Pro, then 99.587% of 28,347 over 24 h with 143 kills, with no acknowledged push lost; on Pro the Spectrum API refused a Tunnel origin (10012, 13002) [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp7.md`]. B0-24b, Spectrum to a Tunnel virtual network on Enterprise, is dropped. Now: B0-24c, a Pro zone created a TCP Spectrum app on port 22 with a Worker origin, whose `connect()` took raw TCP both ways and held 600 s with 1 MiB echoed byte-exact [measured, same]; B0-24d, a Worker called a Durable Object's `connect()` and the object piped the socket to its container's port 22: 1 MiB byte-exact in 18 ms after open, 4 MiB in 839 to 991 ms, open 20 to 137 ms warm, about 2 s on a cold start, and service-binding `props` reached `ctx.props` while a public request saw `{}` [measured, `tests/m6/results/m6-wp2.md`]. B0-24a's gate carries to the Spectrum ingress with three fronts and one killed every 10 minutes for a day in M6-WP2 (not yet run) | At least 99.9% of sessions succeed | Under 99%, which leaves SSH off |
| Partial DO failure: inject 22% errors into member calls and into storage writes, which reset objects, and count whole pushes | At least 92% of pushes succeed within three attempts | Under 84% |
| Reset storm: every member on all three sites reset within 60 s under 100 writes/s, with `suspect` reports from two colos | Every group commits within 12 s of the last reset | Any group over 12 s |
| Drain guard: two drain-key `SiteMode { Drain }` entries for different sites submitted straight to the `Release` group, then a test group whose voters are all drained by customer-key entries | The second drain-key entry is refused and pages, and the all-drained group elects a leader within 12 s | Two sites drained by the drain key, or the all-drained group leaderless over 60 s |
| Restore window: delete a branch and force-push another on day 1 with GC and archiving running, then restore both to day 0 on day 30 (clock-skewed replay) | Both refs return to their day-0 targets, every object they reach is readable, and archives are named by committed `Archived` records in two buckets | The restore is refused, any object is missing, or a follower truncates past a range with no committed `Archived` |
| Agent fleet replay (journey 9) on one `Tri` group, leader enam. 500 agents push distinct refs every 2 min (40 KB mean, 5% at 1 MiB), 27.6 commands/s with PrSync, test-merge records, check runs, task updates, and lease renewals, 45 feed polls/s on followers and learners, 10 merges/min, 33 ref reads/s, 200 API reads/s. The Graftyard-run token runs once with its shipped permissions and once on an installation widened to every permission, and a refusal from either counts. Milestone 0 answers four platform questions from research and its own measurements instead of a written answer from Cloudflare: the route of a binding call, whether storage followers span regions ("Synthesis decision"), where an object restarts after a region loss, and whether Artifacts forks share objects (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Platform questions are answered by measurement").
